# api decision log

> Why api is built the way it is. Each entry says what was decided, why, what was rejected and where it is described.
>
> **Rule:** do not re-propose what was rejected without new facts. If a decision has to change, ask the author and update this file. An entry is never rewritten: an amendment with its date at the end of the entry corrects it, or a new entry replaces it, and the old one stays where it is, marked "Superseded".
>
> The repository was set up 2026-10-19 from the template of `spec` v0.1.0 (R21 spec), moved 2026-10-20 to `spec` v0.2.9 (R01), 2026-11-02 to v0.6.2 (R02) and the same day to v0.6.6 (R16), 2026-11-07 to v0.9.4 (R23), 2026-11-08 to v0.9.11 (R24), 2026-11-09 to v0.12.0 (R40), 2026-11-11 to v0.15.0 (R52), and 2026-11-12 to v0.15.1 (R60) and the same day to v0.16.3 (R62), and 2026-11-13 to v0.16.5 (R64) and the same day to v0.16.7 (R68), and 2026-11-14 to v0.17.1 (R69). How it comes to differ from the template over time — tools in the devcontainer, rules in RUN.md — is recorded here as a decision, as R46 canvas-api recorded the differences from its model. Next number — R72.
>
> Here entries are cited as `R03`, in other repositories as `R03 api`. Decisions of `spec` are cited as "R18 spec", MathTrail's decisions as "R37 canvas-api", and in a list as "canvas-api: R37, R38". Each log has its own numbers.
>
> Open questions that concern several repositories are in the registers of `spec`: О- in product 13 spec, П- in privacy 9 spec, Т- in repositories 8 spec. What is still to be decided in the component itself is in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 2.

## The `spec` tag

**R01. `api` stands on `spec` v0.2.9: the submodule `spec/` is at this tag, links to `spec` pinned to a tag name it, and what came from the template is as in the template v0.2.9. Since then the documents are checked by `just -f spec/justfile docs-check-repo`.**
- **What the tag brings to `api`** (`git -C spec diff v0.1.0 v0.2.9`, read 2026-10-20; Д6 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`):
  - the 2026-10-19 amendment to R11 spec — the format of the protocol `app` ↔ `api` is decided: the API contracts are protobuf and buf, and Т-7 is closed in this part, while what stayed open in it is the module format. The card's item is now "the protocol between `app` and `api` — its messages and services" (architecture 3.5 spec). T07 was going to recommend the same — protobuf and buf, by the recommendation of Т-7 —, so the decision of `spec` does not diverge from it. For what `api` needs from the contract, T07 now refers to the contract itself — to epic E3 of RUN.md `spec` — and to Т-25;
  - R26 spec — one account can hold both the owner profile as a learner and the profiles of the owner's children, and consent is still recorded for each learner. The sign-in and permissions of such an account are detailed by T01, and export and deletion per profile and for the account as a whole by `api` (privacy 2 spec), that is, by T08. R26 spec opened П-17 — how a child on a shared device will not get into a parent's profile —, and `spec` assigned it to T01 together with T05 `app`. It also added to П-14: what remains of consent even after a child's profile is deleted, when the account holds other profiles too. For whom an own key is on such an account, R26 spec does not decide: that is still О-4, П-4 and Т-9;
  - R28 spec — the graph types are set by `learner-model`, and `engine` takes them from it: `api` converts the graph from the module package into one form, not two;
  - R29 spec — the list of the engine's reason codes is a contract in `spec`: the codes are proposed by `engine` and recorded by epic E3 in RUN.md `spec`. `api` stores the reason code together with the choice (architecture 1 spec);
  - the opening of architecture 3 spec — the answer to В9 `spec`: the task that set up a repository could name items beyond the card or skip an item of the card, and the card is corrected for this by the repository's own "Result of the detailing", when the decision has already been made. T11 `spec` did not name two items of card 3.5 — carrying progress over and tariffs —, and `api` took them by the card: these are T04 and T06. `api` has no items beyond the card, so there is nothing to correct in it yet, and T09 checks the decisions against it, as before;
  - R30 spec — the CI checks are the same for all repositories, and `api` will have them even before its code: secret scanning, DCO, CodeQL, Scorecard and the documents check — the shared checks from `.github` —, and with them SonarCloud. All but Scorecard hold the merge — the SonarCloud quality gate included —, and the merge is only by a merge commit. T21 `spec` will connect them: its session writes the CI files here together with `dependabot.yml` and the exclusion of `spec/**` for SonarCloud (Д7 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`), and it also sets the ruleset of the branch `main`. Until then `api` has no CI, and the Go checks and the releases will come with the first code;
  - R33 spec and rule 12 of RUN.md — the findings of SonarCloud, CodeQL and Scorecard on the previous task are closed before a new one. For findings that a change cannot close, the reasons common to all repositories are recorded in R33 spec, and the repository's own ones are recorded here;
  - R31 spec — a release of `spec` comes out by itself, after green CI on `main`: the next patch of the line, immutable, with a GitHub attestation. A repository still moves to a new tag by a task of its own;
  - R32 spec — a repository's documents are checked by `docs-check` of the `spec` its submodule stands on: the links, its own sections and log, the tables «В» and «Д», and the citations of `spec` — by the submodule at its tag. An example of a citation is written in inline code, so that the check does not take it for a citation (RULES.md, Citations);
  - RULES.md, beyond the examples of citations: a development or CI tool that ships nowhere and puts none of its own code into what ships — a check, an analyser — may also carry another OSI-approved license, or free terms for open source. But only as a named exception: in the license check or in the decision that brings it in (R12 spec). This concerns the tools of the future code of `api`;
  - R23, R24, R25 and R27 spec — about other components: the success corridor, the key for eval, the samples page, the randomness generator and the derivation of seeds. They do not change the documents of `api`. R24 spec names `api` only to separate the key for eval from the keys of the service account of `api`;
  - devcontainer — the base image `devcontainers/base` 3.0.10 by digest; Claude Code is installed without package scripts, its `install.cjs` is run by name, and `claude --version` stops the build on a stub; `git.alwaysSignOff` — a commit from Source Control is signed off by DCO (R22 spec, R30 spec).
- **What changed here:**
  - `spec/` — at v0.2.9, commit 5eafb1a. The links to `spec` in README.md and the tag in CLAUDE.md and RUN.md — v0.2.9. That the repository was set up from the template v0.1.0 the opening of the log still says: that is history;
  - five examples of citations of the old template are in inline code: two in CLAUDE.md, Documents, two in «Ссылках» of RUN.md and one in the opening of the log. Rule 6 of RUN.md and the section Environment in CLAUDE.md name `docs-check-repo`, and RUN.md has the new rule 12. These places are as in the template v0.2.9;
  - `.devcontainer/` — the one that the recipe of `spec` v0.2.9 (`editDevcontainer`) builds for `api` of the kind `go`. The name of the container and of the network, `postStartCommand` and the Go extension are its own, as on v0.1.0 too. The move introduces no differences from the template;
  - what the tag decided for `api` is named in its documents:
    - R11 spec — T07, T08, T09, «Почему такой порядок» in RUN.md, [architecture.md](architecture.md), the introduction of section 2 and section 2.7. The name of T07 and the heading of section 2.7 no longer have Т-7: Т-25 remained there. Section 2.4 stipulates that the module format is still open in Т-7;
    - R26 spec and П-17 — T01 and section 2.1; R26 spec and П-14 — T08 and section 2.8;
    - R26, R28, R29 and R30 spec — section 1; R30 spec — also «Не входит» of epic E1.

— *Why now:* T21 `spec` connects the shared checks from `.github` here (R30 spec). The shared `documents.yml` builds the stage `toolchain` from the `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` of whoever called it and runs `just -f spec/justfile docs-check-repo` in it ([`documents.yml`](https://github.com/altnovatrail/.github/blob/ff2a359/.github/workflows/documents.yml), the release v0.1.0 of `.github` — the latest on 2026-10-20, commit ff2a359, read 2026-10-20). And `spec` has this recipe only from v0.2.3 (R32 spec): on v0.1.0 the required check would be red and would hold any merge. v0.2.9 is the latest release of `spec` on 2026-10-20, and immutable (`gh release view -R altnovatrail/spec`); `gh release verify v0.2.9 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed it by its attestation, and the tag is on commit 5eafb1a. Taking the latest release at the time of the task is also what Д6 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec` requires.

— *Rejected:*
- staying on v0.1.0 until the first code: the documents check from T21 `spec` would hold any merge, T01, T07 and T08 would read `spec` without the amendment to R11 spec, without R26 spec and П-17, and a link to a section of `spec` that is not at the tag, or a gap in the log, would pass unnoticed (Д6 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`);
- an older tag, for example v0.2.6, which `module-kit` and `tutor` stand on: it lacks rule 12 of RUN.md — it is in the template from v0.2.7 — and R33 spec, which came with v0.2.8. And Д6 requires taking the latest release. The template and the devcontainer of v0.2.9 are the same as those of v0.2.8;
- moving without the devcontainer, or carrying over its changes selectively: the container of `api` is the container of `spec` (CLAUDE.md, Environment), and a difference would have to be recorded as a decision, and there is no reason for one;
- examples of citations in guillemets, as before: `docs-check-repo` checks a number in guillemets against the log, and R32 spec rejected not checking them.

— *Consequence:* T10 goes first in table 1 of RUN.md, and T01 and T03 depend on it. The devcontainer image was not built here — Д1 in table 4 of RUN.md, until Rebuild Container. The CI files — the calls of the shared workflows, `dependabot.yml`, `.sonarcloud.properties` — are written here by T21 `spec`, not by a task of this repository. The commit, push, pull request and merge of the move are made by the executor — at the author's request and only in T10: the following tasks again go by rule 7 of RUN.md. — RUN.md, T10, T01, T07, T08, T09; CLAUDE.md; README.md; `.devcontainer/`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2, 2.1, 2.4, 2.7 and 2.8.

**R02. `api` stands on `spec` v0.6.2 — a tag of the line 0.6, the move to which the cross-repository plan puts as the first task of the turn of `api` (R76 spec). The tag brings the module format 0.2 with the package, the index and the types by which `api` reads them, and three new items of the card — the lesson, the path of a task with a rubric and a hint at the learner's request. The lesson and the rubric are decided by the new tasks T12 and T13, the hint by T05, instead of the pause that R69 spec decided.**
- **What the tag brings to `api`** (`git -C spec diff v0.2.9 v0.6.2`, read 2026-11-02; rows 6.1, 6.2 and 6.5 of `COORDINATION.md` `spec`; Д18–Д59 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec` — their parts for `api`):
  - the cross-repository plan — R76 spec and `COORDINATION.md` in the root of `spec`. The move to a tag of the line 0.6 is the first task of every repository on the way to the first app, for `api` row 6.1; the detailing of `api` is row 6.2, and T01 goes in it at once. The first app is the first build of `app` with `api`, on which the author solves tasks in a browser themselves: with handwriting, lessons and the engine, with sign-in by T01, locally in the devcontainer, without children and without a deployment, and the database of `api` lies outside the repository. The dependencies of the tasks in RUN.md `api` do not change from this (R76 spec). The epic of the code of `api` is row 6.5: after the detailing, the code of `module-kit`, the first batch of `modules`, the code of `learner-model` and `engine`, the first code of `tutor` and the contract `app` ↔ `api` in epic E3 of `spec`;
  - the module format: JSON Schema 2020-12, and the author writes a module in YAML (R45 spec; Т-7 closed). The format has its own version, MAJOR.MINOR (R44 spec; Т-11 closed). While it is v0, the platform reads only the current version — from v0.6.0 it is 0.2 —, and there is no window "the current major and the previous one": `api` and `app` move to a new version together (R44 spec; repositories 4 spec). `api` reads modules and packages 0.2 through `module-kit` and the types of `spec` of the same tag (Д59 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`). The parts of a module — the module manifest, the skill graph with `retired_skills`, anchor skills, tasks, the trap and technique catalogs, the solving steps, lessons, templates and solvers — are R46, R49–R51, R56–R58, R60, R61 spec; Т-14, О-12, О-18 and О-19 are closed;
  - the package and the index (R62, R63 spec; Т-15 and Т-30 closed). The package digest is the digest of its inventory. The server part is the canonical view, and the device part is `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and the media files, and `api` serves it. The configuration of `api` pins a release of `modules` and its index. A new attempt is only on a version that the index has not marked withdrawn, and one begun on a withdrawn version is closed "unchecked", and `api` no longer serves the files of its device part. The form of the configuration is T03 (R63 spec);
  - the signature (R77 spec; Т-27 closed; RULES.md, Hard rules): a file of a NovaTrail release enters code or CI only after the recipe `release-verify`. For `api` this is CI of the pull request that pins the release of `modules` and its index: it checks the signature of the index (Д54 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`). From then on the packages are held by the chain of digests from the pinned index, and the library of `module-kit` checks no signatures;
  - the Go module `spec` (R64, R72 spec): code in Go takes the tag as the Go module `github.com/altnovatrail/spec` — the same tag as the submodule's —, and `api` reads the open view by its types `types/formatv0/open`, not by copies of its own. The version of the module in `go.mod` and the submodule are moved together, and the module `spec` is excluded from Dependabot `gomod` (Д46 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`). This comes with the first code;
  - the attempt (R44, R51, R58–R62 spec): besides the package digest, the format version and the seed, an attempt stores the version of `module-kit`. `api` takes the attempt's seed from a cryptographically secure source and does not give it to the device. It gives a task only to a learner of its age categories, and a lesson only if the lesson and each of its tasks suit the learner. A template's instance — by the seed, from the numbers of the package, by the code of `module-kit`, without Starlark at the attempt;
  - the lesson (R58 spec; Д39 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`; О-19 closed) — a new item of the card: how the app gets the segments and tasks of a lesson, and what `api` records about a passed lesson. This is T12 and [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 2.9;
  - the path of a task with a rubric (R41, R70, R71, R75 spec; П-19; О-17; О-22 closed) — two new items of the card: what the learner sees instead of feedback that did not pass the feedback rule, and how the learner sees an essay that cannot be read, and what is recorded about it. What the server stores of such an attempt is П-19. This is T13 and section 2.10;
  - a hint at the learner's request, and the pause. О-8 was decided by R69 spec: the work is read only on the learner's action, and on a pause only the button blinks. The card's item is now "showing the solution (О-7) and a hint at the learner's request (О-21)": before the first submission the request gives what R57 spec decided, and what it gives after a wrong answer and "explain differently" is the detailing of `api`. Here too: which answer slot the fallback text speaks of when a submission has several wrong ones (R57 spec). This is T05 under a new name and section 2.5;
  - reading and words (R52–R54, R67–R70 spec; Т-23 closed). Reading and analysis are two calls, and handwriting is read by two readers of one provider. Nothing of the writing history goes to AI. The tutor's words go to the device whole, after the leak check, not as a stream. Besides the leak, `api` checks the form of a hint and the feedback rule — by the code of `tutor`. The place in the observation is fractions 0–1000 of the crop, and on a workbook it is the answer slot. This is T06 — the quota counts both reading calls (the 2026-10-28 addition to О-14) — and T07: Т-25 is now decided with words without a stream;
  - the modules of the first slice are three: olympiad maths, school maths of grade 3 on a workbook, and short essays in English (R41 spec; О-1 closed). The first batch names `adult` in the audience too: the first app is tested by an adult (R76 spec). Google's models are closed on the whole platform (R47 spec), and `api` calls AI only through `tutor`, so this does not change its documents;
  - the template: status badges in the README (R48 spec; repositories 2.3 spec), rule 10 of RUN.md — with R42 spec, and the container in CLAUDE.md, Environment — "Node.js, Go and just". Rules 6 and 8 of RUN.md and AGENTS.md about the review in one round `api` took as early as 2026-10-21, by R35 spec;
  - RULES.md: code comes with its tests, and with Codecov the merge waits for `codecov/patch` (R48 spec); an archive that the Dockerfile downloads is checked against its sha256 (R34 spec), and an apt package is taken from a snapshot of the Ubuntu archive (R40 spec); a file of a NovaTrail release is checked by `release-verify` (R77 spec); the normative texts for authors are in English (R42 spec); the free terms of a tool are within two new bounds (the 2026-10-22 amendment to R12 spec). Before the code of `api`, of these only the rules of the Dockerfile apply, and the container of `spec` carries them;
  - devcontainer: in the stage `toolchain` — Go 1.27.3, with which `spec` checks its Go module (R64 spec), and a sha256 for each archive (R34 spec). The Dockerfile installs no apt packages. The base image and the versions of the other tools are as before;
  - R36–R40, R43, R65, R66, R73 and R74 spec — about other components and tools: UI packages and npm, synthetics in CI of `tutor`, licenses, the apt snapshot, the review board, fast-inference providers, the reading model on the device, TypeScript types. They do not change the documents of `api`.
- **What changed here:**
  - `spec/` — at v0.6.2, commit 49d0fb6. The tag in CLAUDE.md, README.md and RUN.md — v0.6.2;
  - the template: a three-way merge of the template v0.2.9 → v0.6.2 with CLAUDE.md, README.md, RUN.md, AGENTS.md and this file. Two places gave a conflict: the README badges went after the heading, and the opening of the log stayed its own;

The cross-repository plan requires the same: the move to a tag of the line 0.6 is the first task of the turn of `api` (R76 spec; Д53 and Д54 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`), and T01 goes right after it. The submodule, the module `spec` and the contracts module are moved together, by one move to a tag, and the contracts module is excluded from Dependabot `gomod` the same way as the module `spec`: otherwise Dependabot would raise it apart from the submodule (Д46 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`). This comes with the first code. — R16; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 1.

## Sign-in and permissions

**R03. The sign-in service is Firebase Auth, with sign-in through Google; Apple by the first deployment. `api` checks the ID token with the Admin SDK for Go behind a narrow port, on every request with revocation. Without a token, only the files of the package's device part and the open views of the package: these are the content of modules, not a learner's data. A fresh sign-in is by `auth_time`.**
- **What was decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1; sources — section 4):
  - the sign-in service is Firebase Auth without moving to Identity Platform. The move is "an optional upgrade", and its capabilities — SAML and OpenID Connect, blocking functions, user activity and audit logs, multi-tenancy, an SLA — are not needed by the first slice, while on the Spark plan it sets a limit of 3,000 daily active users. Return to it with teachers and schools at stage 5, or when an SLA is needed;
  - the sign-in method is Google. Apple by the first deployment: sign-in through Apple can be set up only by a member of the Apple Developer Program, and the membership costs 99 USD a year. This is spending outside the repository, and the author decides it (rule 4 of RUN.md; Д3 in table 4 of RUN.md). There is no email and password, no email links and no phone: the sign-in service would hold a password, an email address or a number, while the adult's contacts are only those without which sign-in does not work (privacy 1 spec), and `api` sends no emails (architecture 7 spec);
  - the token check is the Admin SDK for Go, `firebase.google.com/go/v4`, as in R14 canvas-api. `VerifyIDToken` checks only the signature, by Google's keys from the cache, and `VerifyIDTokenAndCheckRevoked` also the revocation, by a network call on every check. `api` checks every request with a token with revocation: after revocation an ID token lives for up to an hour more, and an old token from another device must neither create the account again, nor read a learner's data, nor spend the quota (R14 canvas-api);
  - without a token `api` gives the files of the package's device part — the media files, `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` — and the open views of the package by its digest: the module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog, the solving steps (R62 spec). These are the content of modules, not a learner's data, and the official modules lie open in the releases of `modules` anyway (R20 spec; repositories 4 spec). `app` loads a module's picture with a plain `<img>`, and the browser sends no header with a token with it, and the files are cached by HTTP. A withdrawn version `api` does not serve all the same (R63 spec). A lesson, a task and everything about a profile — only with a token. With the modules of third-party authors, stage 4, this rule is reviewed together with their licenses (О-10);
  - a fresh sign-in is the `auth_time` of a token checked with revocation. Its period and the actions that need it are decided by T08. Before a user is deleted at the sign-in service — `RevokeRefreshTokens`, then `DeleteUser` (R14 canvas-api);
  - the port: `api` calls the sign-in service through three operations — check a token and get the UID of the sign-in service and `auth_time`, revoke the tokens, delete the user. The UID lies only in the account's record, and the subject of permissions is the account ID of `api` (R04). Then another sign-in service, for example at self-hosting, changes the adapter, not the permissions and the data. The first slice has no shared OIDC adapter (Д4 in table 4 of RUN.md);
  - the first app (R76 spec) signs in through a dev project of Firebase on the free Spark plan, through Google: the token's path is the same as at a deployment, with a signature, and the devcontainer does not change. The author creates the project when the epic of code gets to it (rule 4 of RUN.md). The key of the service account lies outside the repository — not in the folder `/workspaces/api` —, and how `api` gets it is decided by the epic of code (RULES.md, Hard rules). The project has one user — the author (Д5 in table 4 of RUN.md).
- **What the sign-in service holds** — checked against privacy 1, 4 and 9 spec: the account owner's sign-in method, for Google their Google account. Firebase Auth processes data only in the USA, keeps IP addresses from its logs for a few weeks, and removes the rest about a user from its production and backup systems within 180 days after deletion. `api` neither reads nor stores the email from the token (R29 canvas-api). That the sign-in service works only from the USA concerns П-11 and П-9 — this is В2 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec».

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-02, by the recommendation that the planning of T01 proposed. Firebase Auth already worked in canvas-api with the same Admin SDK for Go. Firebase's price table marks sign-in without moving to Identity Platform — "Other Authentication services" — at the free Spark plan too. The limit of 50 thousand monthly active users there stands at the move (section 4). Deleting a user, revoking tokens and `auth_time` are what deletion by a fresh sign-in needs (architecture 4.5 spec). The check with revocation on all requests but the hot path was introduced by R14 canvas-api. The hot path there was issuing Centrifugo tokens. Here there is no real-time channel (Т-25), and the most frequent requests — for the package's files — carry no token.

— *Rejected:*
- comparing sign-in services without a preference — Identity Platform, Auth0, Clerk, Zitadel Cloud: the author chose Firebase Auth;
- Apple at once: the membership would have to be paid as early as the deployment, and the first app is tested only by the author (R76 spec);
- email and password, email links, phone — superfluous contacts of the adult (privacy 1 spec);
- moving to Identity Platform now — the limit of the Spark plan, and its capabilities are not needed by the first slice;
- a check without revocation on all requests: the token of a deleted account would live for up to an hour more (R14 canvas-api);
- the files and the open views of the package by token, even without revocation: they protect no learner's data, `<img>` sends no token, and loading each picture through `fetch` loses the HTTP cache;
- the Firebase Auth emulator for the first app. It needs `firebase-tools` and Java 21 or newer, and that is a difference of the devcontainer from the container of `spec` (CLAUDE.md, Environment). Besides, it issues tokens without a signature, so the path of the check would differ from the deployment. Whether the tests of the code need it, the epic of code will decide (Д5 in table 4 of RUN.md).

— *Consequence:* T08 takes the fresh sign-in by `auth_time` and the deletion at the sign-in service. The parameters of the sign-in service in the deployment configuration are T06 `app` (architecture 3.1 spec). What is deferred is Д3, Д4 and Д5, and what concerns `spec` is В2 in table 3 of RUN.md. — RUN.md, T01; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.1, 3.1 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* `app` takes the media files by `fetch`, not by `<img>`: the canvas gets a picture as a `Blob`, by the function `media` (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R36 spec), — and `api` on its origin answers with CORS on all paths and all responses, without a token too («origin `api` и версии» at table 1 of RUN.md `spec`; Д79 in its table 4). T07 rechecked this entry's arguments against a token on the files by the primary sources on 2026-11-02 ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4): "`<img>` sends no token" is true, but `<img>` is no longer the path of a picture; "loading each picture through `fetch` loses the HTTP cache" is false: `fetch` without a cache mode of its own goes through the HTTP cache. The decision stays — the files of the package's device part and the four open views go without a token —, and two arguments hold it: they protect no learner's data, and a request with `Authorization` is not a simple one and goes with a preflight, and the preflight cache is by the exact address, so on its origin `api` every file would get one of its own. A `fetch` of a file and a Connect GET of an open view without headers go without a preflight and through the HTTP cache (R18). — R18; RUN.md, T07; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 3.9.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the period of a fresh sign-in and the actions that need it were decided by R20: `auth_time` no older than 5 minutes — for deleting a profile, deleting an account and changing a forgotten PIN. The port of the token check gives not only the UID and `auth_time` but also the class of refusal — "expired", "revoked", "no such user", "disabled" or "failure". `VerifyIDTokenAndCheckRevoked` gives no token on any refusal, so `api` reads the UID of a revoked token and of a deleted user's token by `VerifyIDToken` — by Google's keys from the cache, without `GetUser` —, and it does not wrap the errors of the SDK before the helpers `Is…`: those compare the type. A second parsing of the signature happens only on these two paths, and the comparison of revocation stays the code of the SDK, not a copy of it in `api` ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4). The order of deletion as a whole was decided by R21: `RevokeRefreshTokens` and `DeleteUser` go after the database transaction and the tuples of OpenFGA, and what did not go through the operator's job completes. — R20, R21; RUN.md, T08; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 3.10.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T22; R32): the emulator is needed neither by the first app nor by the tests of the code. The adapter is tested with the real SDK against a stand-in Google over TLS. One more was added to the arguments of "Rejected": in the emulator's mode `VerifyIDToken` checks revocation too, so the UID of a revoked token cannot be read through the emulator. The key of the service account is the secret `API_FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS` outside the working tree, only of the type `service_account` and only outside a deployment; in Cloud Run — the service's credentials, without a key. The port revokes the tokens and deletes the user by one operation, `RemoveUser`, and it also lists the users (R21). — R32; RUN.md, T22; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T30; R40): "without moving to Identity Platform" is about the dev project of `api`. The platform's production project works with Identity Platform: `infra` moves it and sets it up by code — the authorized domains `<project>.firebaseapp.com` and `<project>.web.app`, sign-in only by the Google popup, email, phone and anonymous sign-in off, the activity log off, and only `api` deletes a user (architecture 3.11 spec; Д141 and Д145 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`). The argument against the move — the limit of the Spark plan — does not apply to the platform's project on Blaze. The service account of `api` gets the role `signInUsers` — `firebaseauth.users.get`, `.update` and `.delete` — exactly for the four calls of Identity Toolkit that the Admin SDK makes: `accounts:lookup` — the check of an ID token's revocation, `accounts:batchGet` — listing the users, `accounts:update` — revoking the tokens, and `accounts:delete` — deletion (R32; the source code of the SDK v4.22.0, read 2026-11-09). The README names them and what `api` expects of the sign-in settings, and a new call of `api` comes with a row «в реестр spec» before the release that makes it: without the role's permission it will be refused in production.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T43; R52): "without moving to Identity Platform" applies to the dev project of `api` on the Spark plan; the platform's production project is on Firebase Authentication with Identity Platform, and `infra` holds its sign-in settings (R16 infra; Д145 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`). The calls of Identity Toolkit that `api` makes are as before: the tag brought no new ones.

**R04. Permissions are OpenFGA, embedded as a library in the process of `api`. The subject of permissions is the account: the token always belongs to the owner, and a learner's profile is a parameter of the request. The server distinguishes kinds of actions, not people. The PIN is a barrier of the interface in `app`, and `api` holds its hash. Access to the closed slice is by one-time invite codes.**
- **The OpenFGA model** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1):
  - the types are `user` (an account of `api` by its ID), `learner` (a learner's profile) and `classroom`. The relation `owner` to a learner's profile is held for every profile of the account, the profile of the owner themselves too (R26 spec). The rights are `can_manage`, `can_learn` and `can_view`;
  - the model describes teachers and the class in advance, and the first slice writes no tuples for them, as in R06 canvas-api. At stage 5 a teacher bound to a class will get `can_view` at once (architecture 3.5 spec; product 11 spec);
  - attempts, lessons and everything else about a profile are not objects of OpenFGA. Their rows in the database carry the profile ID: `api` checks the right to the profile and reads the row with this ID. There is one tuple per profile, not one per attempt, as there was with the canvas in canvas-api.
- **How OpenFGA works:**
  - as a library in the process of `api`, not as a sidecar, as in R09 canvas-api. The OpenFGA project documents embedding and gives `RunMigrations` for it (section 4). A separate process only where it is needed (architecture 1 spec), and the first app works in the devcontainer without Docker (R76 spec);
  - the storage is Postgres, a separate database beside the database of `api`, as in R09 canvas-api. Which Postgres is decided by T02;
  - the migrations of the storage are `RunMigrations` in the operator's migration job (architecture 3.5 spec), and `api` applies the model at start, as in R28 canvas-api;
  - a tuple is written in the database transaction before `COMMIT`, and "dangling" tuples of profiles that are not in the database are cleaned up by the operator's daily job (spec 9.1 canvas-api). The order of deletion is decided by T08;
  - the version of OpenFGA is exact, in `go.mod`, the license is Apache-2.0 (RULES.md, Licenses).
- **The actions of an adult and a learner.** On a shared device there is one token, and the server does not know who holds the device (R07 canvas-api). So it distinguishes kinds of actions:
  - learning requests — an attempt, a submission, a hint, a lesson — carry the profile ID and require `can_learn`;
  - an adult's actions on a profile — the profile, consent, settings — require `can_manage`;
  - actions on the account — the PIN, an invite code, a child consent while there is no profile yet — concern the account from the token;
  - irreversible actions also require a fresh sign-in (R03; T08).

  How an adult confirms showing the solution to a child is decided by T05 by this model.
- **The PIN** is a barrier of the interface, not a security boundary (R07 canvas-api; spec 12.2 canvas-api). `app` keeps the section for adults behind it (architecture 3.1 spec), and when `app` asks for the PIN is decided by `app`. The PIN's hash is argon2id, the PIN is 4–6 digits, no more than 5 failed attempts in 15 minutes; `api` keeps the hash, the counter and the lock time in the account, as in spec 12.2 canvas-api. So the PIN is one for all the account's devices, and changing the device does not reset the lock. A forgotten PIN is changed by a fresh sign-in.
- **П-17:** `app` opens the owner profile on a shared device behind the same PIN as the section for adults. `api` accepts an own key only with a request of a profile of the category `adult` (Т-9; О-4) — this rule the server checks itself. Whether a device is shared, the server does not know, so whether to keep the key on a shared device is decided by T05 `app` after О-4 and Т-9. This is the recommendation of `api` to П-17, «в реестр spec» — В1 in table 3 of RUN.md.
- **The checks before a learner's data** — in order in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1. On every request — the token, access to the closed slice and the owner's statement that they are an adult (П-6). A request with a profile ID first checks the right in OpenFGA and only then reads anything by this ID: another account's profile and a profile that does not exist get one and the same refusal. Then — consent (П-1), the AI route by the age category (Т-20) and the quota. Creating a profile checks the country against the list until the DPIA (П-2) and the consent.
- **Access to the closed slice** — one-time invite codes, as in R29 canvas-api. The operator creates them by a command of `api`, the database holds only the SHA-256 of a code, at the first sign-in the code is bound to the account, and `api` does not read the email. Before access `api` creates neither profiles nor learners' data. The first app takes the same path: the author creates a code for themselves by the operator's command. How the quota is linked to access is decided by T06.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-02. OpenFGA — as in canvas-api, where it was kept for readiness for B2B (R08, R41 canvas-api). For teachers the card asks for permissions in the form "who got access to what and in what role" (architecture 3.5 spec), and the OpenFGA model expresses them by relations. An embedded library, not a sidecar — by facts that canvas-api did not have: the OpenFGA project itself gives a path of embedding with migrations, the principle "a separate process only where it is needed" (architecture 1 spec) and the first app without Docker (R76 spec). The subject is the account, as in R07 canvas-api: a child gets no credentials of their own. The PIN's hash is in `api`, so that the PIN is common to the account's devices. Invite codes — as in R29 canvas-api: with them no email is needed, Apple's "Hide My Email" does not get in their way, and codes are not personal data.

— *Rejected:*
- permissions by code over the database — a table "subject — relation — object" with one relation "the account owns the profile". The planning of T01 proposed it, with arguments that R41 canvas-api did not have: a separate process only where it is needed (architecture 1 spec), the first app without Docker and without a deployment (R76 spec), teachers only at stage 5 (product 11 spec). The author chose OpenFGA: a model ready for teachers matters more to them than a second store. Do not propose it without new facts;
- OpenFGA as a sidecar (R09 canvas-api): it is a second process, and locally an OpenFGA binary or Docker in the devcontainer;
- an OpenFGA object per attempt, like the canvas in canvas-api: a double write at every start of an attempt, though the rights to an attempt do not differ from the rights to the profile;
- `api` checks the PIN and issues a short confirmation for an adult's actions. The owner's token lies on the device all the same, and this will not become a security boundary, while there will be more code and state;
- the owner profile and an adult's actions only by a fresh sign-in: on a shared tablet every switch would become a new sign-in through Google;
- the PIN only on the device: each device would have its own PIN, and changing the device would reset the lock;
- access by an account ID that the person passes to the operator: the ID leaves the platform, and the person has more steps. A list of emails or of their hashes was already rejected by R29 canvas-api.

— *Consequence:* the account gets the UID of the sign-in service, the owner's statement that they are an adult, with its date, the binding of an invite code, the PIN's hash and the counter of its attempts, and the OpenFGA store gets the tuples "account — profile". The OpenFGA store is a second database of `api`, beside the main one, and export, deletion and backups (T02, T08) must cover it too. On the server, learners' data are still held only by `api` (architecture 1 spec). This adds to privacy 4 spec and architecture 5 spec — В3 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec». The recommendation to П-17 is В1. T05 takes the model of the actions of an adult and a learner, T06 access, and T08 the deletion of tuples and the order of deletion. The tools of the first app are Д5. — RUN.md, T01; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2, 2.1, 3.1 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* which Postgres the storage has was decided by R05: the database `openfga` in the same Neon project as the database of `api`, with the same region and history window. OpenFGA does not clean up its `changelog` itself, and the operator's daily job deletes its records older than a day, as in R27 canvas-api: a record lives no longer than two days. — [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* П-17 is closed by R84 spec, and both of its sides — this entry and R08 app. On an account that has a profile of another category, `app` opens the owner profile by the same PIN as the section for adults, at every opening. An own key lies on the device only if the key's owner decided so on this device, and otherwise in the memory of the tab while its profile is open; on the server, only in the request's memory. The key and its provider come with every request of the owner profile that calls AI, and `api` still accepts them only with a profile of the category `adult` (R84 spec). `spec` recorded the row В1 of table 3 of RUN.md by this answer (R16). How the key goes in the protocol and how `api` checks it when it is connected is decided by T07. — R16; RUN.md, T07; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* irreversible actions — deleting a profile, deleting an account and changing a forgotten PIN — wait for a fresh sign-in no older than 5 minutes, and export does not wait for it (R20). The owner profile is deleted the same way as a child's profile, by `can_manage`, and the account does not delete its last profile (R21). Into the order of checks of section 3.1, before step 1, go the token's class and the mark "being deleted": for a marked account any request, both creating an account and redeeming a code, gets "account gone". The fresh sign-in is checked last, after the right. The order of deletion, which this entry left to T08, was decided by R21: first the database transaction, then the tuples of OpenFGA. A "dangling" tuple gives no rights even after deletion: there is no row of the profile, and a request by it gets "no such profile". Every write transaction under an account takes its row `FOR KEY SHARE` and checks the mark, and the operator's command that confirms a consent refuses a marked account. — R20, R21; RUN.md, T08; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 3.10.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08* (T21; R31): the model in the OpenFGA store is applied not by `serve` at start but by the command `api migrate`, under its own lock, together with the store of `api`; at start `serve` only finds the store and among its models the embedded one, and does not start if it is not there. The migrations of the storage are not `RunMigrations` but the files of OpenFGA through goose with a lock: the package `RunMigrations` brings into the binary a MySQL driver under MPL-2.0.

## Database and learner model

**R05. The database is Neon Postgres: one project, holding the database of `api` and the OpenFGA store, and the region is П-11 among Neon's regions in AWS. The first app is a dev project on the Free plan. The periods are as П-9 recommends, and beyond that the words of hints live until the end of the attempt, and an account without requests for 12 months is deleted with its profiles. The honest period of the backups is Neon's history window.**
- **What was decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2; sources — section 4):
  - the database is Neon Postgres, as in canvas-api (R08 canvas-api). One project holds the database of `api` and the database `openfga`, the OpenFGA store (R04; R09 canvas-api). Export, deletion and backups cover both;
  - the region is П-11, until the first deployment. Neon works only in AWS and no longer creates projects in Azure, so the region of the database is a Neon region near the region of Cloud Run, like the pair `aws-us-east-1` and `us-east4` of R54 canvas-api. This is В5 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»;
  - connections are only over TLS. Whether to go through Neon's pooler in transaction mode or directly is decided by the epic of code, and with it the migration tool; in canvas-api it is goose (Д6 in table 4 of RUN.md). The migrations of both databases are the operator's job (architecture 3.5 spec);
  - the first app (R76 spec) — a dev project of Neon on the Free plan, like the dev project of Firebase in R03. The author creates it when the epic of code gets to it (rule 4 of RUN.md). The connection string lies outside the repository, not in the folder `/workspaces/api` (RULES.md, Hard rules), and how `api` gets it is decided by the epic of code. The devcontainer does not change. The plan of the platform's project is spending, and the author decides it before the first deployment (Д7);
  - the backups are Neon's history window: within it the database can be restored to any moment, and what was deleted lives in the history until it leaves the window. On Free the window is 6 hours, on Launch from a day to 7. `api` takes no snapshots of the database: a snapshot would hold what was deleted even after the window;
  - what lies in the database, where, for how long and what goes into the export is the table of section 3.2. The export carries everything about the learner and the account owner except secrets and service fields: the PIN's hash, the counter and the lock, the UID of the sign-in service, the binding of the invite code and the attempt's seed;
  - the periods: attempts, observations, the evidence log and the engine choices live while the profile lives, and a profile without activity for 12 months is deleted, as П-9 recommends. Beyond П-9:
    - the words of hints live until the end of the attempt: only the ladder of the same task needs them (privacy 4 spec). After that, what remains of a hint is the rung's number and the source — the model or a fallback text;
    - an account that nobody signed in to for 12 months is deleted with its profiles and with the user at the sign-in service, and what remains of the consents is what T08 decides by П-14: until the lawyer's answer, a minimal record without the child's data. "Nobody signed in" `api` reads as "there were no requests with the account's token". The sign-in time in the token, `auth_time`, changes only at a new sign-in, and the sign-in service refreshes the token itself, so by `auth_time` the account of an owner who learns every day but has not signed in again for a year would be deleted. This reading is that of the executor of T02, not the author's words;
    - the words of hints are deleted when the attempt is closed, in the same transaction, and when an abandoned attempt is closed is decided by T05;
    - the `changelog` of OpenFGA — no longer than two days: the daily job deletes the records older than a day (R27 canvas-api).

    The deletion is done by the operator's daily retention job, in the order of T08. `api` sends no emails (architecture 7 spec), so the period is named by the text of the consent, as in privacy 8 canvas-api.
- **What Neon holds** — checked against privacy 1, 2, 4 and 9 spec: the processor is Neon, a part of Databricks. Its processor agreement is Neon's addendum to the Databricks DPA, and AWS is a subprocessor. The addendum says nothing about children's data, and whether it is enough before the first child is decided by the lawyer together with П-1 and П-3 — В5.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-02, by the recommendation that the planning of T02 proposed. Neon already worked in canvas-api, and it scales to zero, as R11 spec requires: compute goes to sleep after 5 minutes idle and wakes up in a few hundred milliseconds. The OpenFGA store in the same project — as in R09 canvas-api: one database for backups, deletion and the region. A dev project for the first app — for the same reason as the dev project of Firebase in R03: the path is the same as at a deployment, and the devcontainer does not change. An adult does not see the words of hints — they see progress and the repeating traps (product 3 spec; concepts 7 spec) —, and we collect a minimum of data (privacy 1 spec). An account without requests would keep the UID, the PIN's hash and the owner's statement to no use at all.

— *Rejected:*
- Cloud SQL for PostgreSQL — in one cloud with Cloud Run and with the regions of Google Cloud, but an instance is billed every second while it runs, and it can be stopped only by hand, by the activation policy NEVER. So there is no scaling to zero on idle, which R11 spec asks for. Free is only a trial instance for 30 days;
- Postgres in the devcontainer for the first app: this is a difference from the container of `spec` that would have to be recorded as a decision (CLAUDE.md, Environment), and the path would differ from the deployment. The container has no Docker and `docker compose`, as canvas-api has;
- snapshots of the database beyond the history window: what was deleted would live in them longer than the honest period says;
- the words of hints while the profile lives, exactly by П-9: nobody reads them after the attempt;
- keeping what was read for a shorter time than the profile lives: a verdict is repeated by the observation without a time limit (architecture 1 spec), and a short period would require a correction of `spec`;
- an account until its owner deletes it: without sign-ins it would hold the owner's data indefinitely.

— *Consequence:* Д5 names the OpenFGA store in the dev project of Neon, Д2 is closed, and the new ones are Д6 (the dev project of Neon, the database for the tests of the code, the migration tool) and Д7 (the plan of Neon) in table 4 of RUN.md. What concerns `spec` is В5 in table 3: to П-9, П-11 and privacy 2 spec. T08 takes both databases, the `changelog`, the history window and the deletion by period, T05 the setting "ask before showing" in the profile, T06 the quota counters in the database, T12 the passed lesson as a row of the database, T13 that the database holds the words of hints only until the end of the attempt. — RUN.md, T02; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.2, 3.1, 3.2 and 4; R04, the 2026-11-02 amendment.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the author's decisions on Д6 and Д7, taken after the report of T02, by the recommendations:
- the deployment is the Launch plan, the history window 1 day, as Launch has by default. The honest period within which what was deleted leaves Neon's history is a day. Neon estimates the typical spending on Launch at $15 a month. Rejected: Free — when the 100 CU-hours a month run out, compute stops until the next month, and with it `api` —, and a window of 7 days: what was deleted would live in the history for a week;
- the tests of the code with a database — `embedded-postgres` v2, license MIT. It is a dependency of the tests only, it does not go into the build, and the exact version goes into `go.mod` in the epic of code. The library downloads Postgres from the releases of `theseus-rs/postgresql-binaries` and checks it against the SHA-256 sums of its manifest, and `go.sum` holds the manifest. The epic of code builds the supervisor from the module's source code and passes it through `Config.Supervisor` or `EP_SUPERVISOR`: a downloaded supervisor the library checks against `checksums.txt` of the same release at run time, and `go.sum` does not hold that sum. Rejected: a service container of Postgres in CI — without Docker in the devcontainer the tests with a database would not run locally —, and a Neon branch: it needs the network and a secret in CI, and a pull request from a fork gets no secrets;
- the author sets up the dev project of Neon at the epic of code, in `aws-us-east-1`, as in canvas-api.

Д7 is closed, and Д6 keeps the migration tool and the connection pool. — RUN.md, T02, Д6; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* an essay — an attempt of a task with a rubric — has neither what was read nor the words of feedback, and it has no observation row. What was read this entry keeps while the profile lives, because the verdict is repeated by it (architecture 1 spec), and a rubric has no verdict, and there is nothing to repeat (П-19). What was read of an essay is its whole text, and storing it would mean keeping the essay on the server without the owner's choice, contrary to П-8 and the promise of privacy 7 spec. The words of feedback, unlike the words of hints, do not live even until the end of the attempt: a rubric has no ladder, which is what needs the words of hints. So is decided what this entry left to T13. The author's decision when T13 was planned. — R15; RUN.md, T13; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.8.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* what remains of the consents after deletion was decided by R21, by the recommendation of П-14: of a child consent, a minimal record without the account and profile IDs, until the lawyer's answer, and an adult's consent is deleted whole. The operator's daily retention job deletes a profile and an account by period by the same path as deletion on request, only without a fresh sign-in, and checks the period under the row's lock: otherwise an owner who came back the same day would lose the account. It also completes the deletions of accounts whose steps after the transaction did not go through, and deletes the users of the sign-in service without an account of `api` who for 30 days have not signed in and not refreshed their token. This is beyond П-9 — В32 in table 3 of RUN.md. The date of the last request with the account's token goes into the export: it is data about the owner, not a service field. — R21; RUN.md, T08; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.10.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T30; R40): the region is decided — the USA, one place for all learners: Cloud Run and everything `infra` keeps in a region — `us-east4`, Neon — `aws-us-east-1`, the pair that `api` recommended (R109 spec; П-11 closed; Д127 in table 4 of RUN.md `spec`). The dev project of Neon of `api` is already in `aws-us-east-1` (R39), and it need not be set up again.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T32; R42): the connected modules are the table `connected_modules` of the migration `0004`: the profile ID, the module ID and the time of connection, without a version, and the key is the pair of profile and module. The rows go with the profile by a foreign key, `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so deleting a profile and an account and `api sweep` do nothing new. They will go into the export when epic E4 of `spec` records its format (Д15 in table 4 of RUN.md).

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T33; R43): attempts, lesson runs, the evidence log, the state cache and the engine choices are tables of the migration `0005`, as a skeleton: the keys, the links to the profile and the fields of a row's life. The other fields of the rows of the table of section 3.2 will be added by the tasks of E3 that write them. All the rows go with the profile by a foreign key, so deleting a profile and an account does nothing new (R21).

**R06. The learner model lives in the database of `api` (П-7). What is stored is an append-only evidence log, and the state of the skills is a cache with a mark of the version of `learner-model` and the release of `modules` (Т-22). An engine choice also stores the release of `modules` and the number of the last evidence by which it was made.**
- **What was decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2):
  - П-7 — the learner model is in the database of `api`: several devices and several learners of the account work with it, the subscription too, and the engine computes on the server, as Т-10 recommends. The export is in an open format (R09 spec);
  - evidence — one per finished attempt (concepts 11 spec); an attempt of a task with a rubric gives none in the first slice (О-17). What is in it and how much it weighs is decided by `learner-model` (T04 `learner-model`; architecture 3.8 spec), and `api` stores its type as it is, and columns for itself: the profile ID, the attempt ID, the package digest, the module, the time and the sequence number. Evidence holds no content;
  - the log is only appended to. Evidence on a version that the index marked `wrong_key` is cut off by a filter at recomputation (R63 spec), the transfer by `retired_skills` also goes at recomputation (T04), and the rows stay. The log is deleted only whole — with the profile or by period (R05; T08);
  - the cache is a row per profile: the state, the version of `learner-model`, the pinned release of `modules` and the number of the last evidence taken into account. If the version or the release does not match the ones on which `api` works now, the state is recomputed from the log. New evidence is appended to the cache without recomputing the whole log, and `learner-model` must give by appending the same state as by recomputation. The evidence and the cache are written in one transaction, and the profile's cache row is locked in it: the evidence's number is the next after the number in the cache, and two attempts of one profile on two devices will not get one number. Whether a snapshot of the state is needed is decided by T04 `learner-model`;
  - an engine choice stores what architecture 1 spec names — the versions of `engine` and `learner-model`, the package digests, the seed and the reason code —, and also the release of `modules` and the number of the last evidence. Without them a repeat by the log does not know which prefix of the log to take and which withdrawal marks were in force then;
  - the export — the log, the current state and the choices with their reason codes; the format is epic E4 of `spec` (R76 spec);
  - the recommendations of `api` to П-7 and Т-22 and the addition to architecture 1 spec are В4 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec».

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-02, by the recommendations that the planning of T02 proposed. The database — as in the recommendation of П-7, and the log — as in the recommendation of Т-22. A change of the algorithm recomputes the state from the log rather than carrying the old one over. An engine choice is repeated by the log (architecture 1 spec), and by it, too, the model is recomputed without the evidence `wrong_key` (R63 spec). The cache — so that an engine choice after each attempt and the skill map do not recompute the log from the start. And the mark of the version and the release says when the cache is stale.

— *Rejected:*
- a family file in Google Drive, as in standalone. There the file was the truth, but writes were unconditional: "Whoever uploads last wins, silently", — and an emptied Drive trash erased a profile without a copy. Also `api` would need access to the owner's Drive at every engine choice;
- the device: each device would have its own model, and the engine on the server would not see it. Computing on the device is needed no earlier than stage 5 (Т-10);
- only the state, like the summary by topics and the window of the last 20 answers in standalone: a choice could not be repeated by the log (architecture 1 spec), the model could not be recomputed without the evidence `wrong_key` (R63 spec), and the transfer by `retired_skills` would go over what was already computed;
- only the log, without a cache: every engine choice and every showing of the skill map would recompute the log from the start;
- one table of attempts instead of the log, like progress by the rows of attempts in canvas-api: the model would then read attempts with their content, and evidence is needed without it.

— *Consequence:* T04 carries progress over at the recomputation of the log, without changing it. In T04 `learner-model` the form of the evidence and the condition "the appended equals the recomputed" are checked against this decision, and if they diverge — В4. What goes to `spec` is В4 in table 3 of RUN.md: to П-7, Т-22, the row «Модель ученика» of privacy 4 spec and to architecture 1 spec. — RUN.md, T02, T04; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.2, 2.4 and 3.2.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the state cache is also marked with the window of format versions of `api`: the mark is the version of `learner-model`, the pinned release of `modules` and the window. The current versions of the modules, by whose graphs the transfer by `retired_skills` goes, depend on both the release and the window (R07). Were the window to change without a change of the release — at a move of `api` to a new line of `spec` —, the cache would go stale unnoticed. One more consequence of R07: the evidence of an attempt begun on a previous version of a module and ended after the move names the skills of the previous version, and `learner-model`, appending it to the cache, transfers it the same way as at recomputation — a particular case of the condition "the appended equals the recomputed" (В4). While a deployment is going on, the previous image may serve requests too: a mark newer than its own — by the release or the window — it does not overwrite, and it computes the state for the answer without writing it. Otherwise two images would overwrite the mark one after the other. — R07; RUN.md, T03, T04; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.3.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the sequence number of evidence is the next after the last in the profile's log, not after the number in the cache. The lock of the cache row stays and orders the numbers, and the previous image takes it too, which during a deployment does not overwrite a mark newer than its own. The cache row is created together with the profile, empty, with the number 0: so the first evidence of a profile also has something to lock. The cache remembers the number of the last evidence taken into account. If the mark matches the one on which the image works, and the log has evidence after the one taken into account, `api` first appends this tail to the cache in order — with the transfer (R08) —, and then gives the state or appends the new evidence. The tail is appended both at reading and at appending: otherwise the new evidence would jump over the tail, and the tail would not get into the cache. Otherwise during a deployment two writers would take one number, and evidence that the previous image wrote would not get into the cache under the new mark until the next full recomputation. Rejected: the previous image does not end the attempts of a profile whose cache is newer than its mark — in the middle of a deployment the learner would get a refusal. The author's decision on a finding of an independent check when T04 was planned. — R08; RUN.md, T04; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* an attempt that the learner's leaving or the period without actions closed gives no evidence if it had no wrong answers. This is the second exception from "one per finished attempt", beside an attempt of a task with a rubric. The attempt itself stays as a row with its close reason. A left or abandoned attempt with wrong answers has the outcome "left unfinished", which concepts 11 spec does not name — В11 in table 3 of RUN.md. The author's decision when T05 was planned; the name of the outcome — after the review of T05, so that "not decided" would not mean two different things (R10). — R10; RUN.md, T05; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.5.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* an engine choice also stores its own time. If lesson runs go to the engine as input (R13), a repeat of the choice by the log restores them by this time: the start, the reason and the time of a run end, once recorded, do not change, and which segments had ended by that time is told by the close times of the attempts. This adds to architecture 1 spec — В4 in table 3 of RUN.md. The author's decision together with the plan of T12, on a finding of its independent check. — R13; RUN.md, T12; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the close reason and the close time of an attempt, once recorded, do not change. An attempt of a task with a rubric gives no evidence (О-17), and without it the engine cannot keep the author's order for skills with a rubric: if closed attempts of tasks with a rubric go to the engine as input (В18 in table 3 of RUN.md), a repeat of the choice will restore them by its time, like lesson runs. This adds to architecture 1 spec — to В4. The author's decision together with the plan of T13, on a finding of its independent check. — R14, R15; RUN.md, T13; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.8.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* an engine choice also stores whether the pool was limited to tasks that are checked without AI: otherwise a repeat by the log does not know from which pool the engine chose (architecture 1 spec; concepts 12 spec). So `spec` answered В14 in table 3 of RUN.md (R11). The rest of what a choice stores, and the cache mark, `spec` recorded by В4 — R80 spec —, and the format window in the cache mark and the time of the choice, which it did not know of, went «в реестр spec» as the row В20 (R16). — R16; R80 spec; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07:* an engine choice also stores the grade by which the model computed the starting point of modules without evidence, and the reason code is from the closed list `ReasonCode` (architecture 1 spec; R100 spec). This does not change the log: the grade is a field of the profile, and the choice only remembers it. — R23; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2.

— *Amendment 2026-11-08:* an engine choice also stores the skill chosen on the skill map, the profile's age category at the time of the choice, and a mark of which of the digests are the engine's modules, connected and suiting the learner, and its seed field is empty while the engine takes no randomness (R107 spec; architecture 1 spec; privacy 4 spec). The skill from the map holds until the learner asks for something else, and the record carries it into the next one; the category changes at the yearly restatement and at a change of country, and the digests also name the modules of the log that only `learner-model` gets (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R07). Without these fields a repeat of the choice will not restore the input. Beyond the model the engine gets a summary of issues, which `api` assembles from the records of choices and attempts rather than stores, and a repeat assembles it again from the records before the time of the choice (R104 spec and its 2026-11-07 amendment). This adds no learner's data: `api` has the category and the connections already. — R24; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T33; R43): the database holds the appending of the log too — a trigger refuses any `UPDATE` of an `evidence` row, its `DELETE` on its own, outside the cascade of a profile's deletion, and `TRUNCATE`, so the rows go only with the profile. The evidence's number in the profile's log is the primary key `(profile_id, seq)`, and evidence is one per attempt: `attempt_id` is unique. The cache row `skill_states` is created by `CreateOwnerProfile` and `CreateChildProfile` in the profile's transaction, and for the profiles that existed before the migration the migration itself creates it. While a deployment is going on, the previous image creates profiles without it, so the code that locks it first creates it if it is not there (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`).

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T36; R46): a submission's observation is the column `submissions.observation` of the migration `0007`: `observationv1.Observation` as binary protobuf — what both readers read and `module-kit` joined, in the form from which the same release gives the verdict again; the options chosen by tapping are in the answer of the submission, as the values of their answer slots. The reading of each reader separately and the crop are not stored.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T39; R49): the log and the cache are in code, the package `internal/model` over `learner-model` v0.1.4. Evidence is written by the catch-up of owed evidence: under the lock of the cache row one function appends the evidence of all the closed attempts of the profile that owe it and whose package the image holds, in the order of closing. It is called by the closing in a request in the same transaction, by the skill map and progress before reading, and by the new step `evidence` of the job `api sweep`. So evidence is also got by the attempts that the job, the trigger or the previous image during a deployment closed, and "in one transaction" above holds for the evidence and the cache: the number is the next after the last in the log, under the same lock. The evidence of a version withdrawn `wrong_key` is written to the log but not appended to the cache: so the appended matches the recomputed. An attempt whose package left the image gets no evidence — Д27 in table 4 of RUN.md. The author's decisions when T39 was planned.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T40; R50): the engine choice is in code, and its record is a row of `engine_choices` with the fields of the migration `0010`: the reason code, the skills it gave, the package digest on which it was made, the skill from the map, the age category and the grade of the profile, whether the pool was limited to tasks by tapping, the versions of `engine` and `learner-model`, the release of `modules`, the format window, the number of the last evidence, the digests of all the graphs and, of them, of the engine's modules. There is no seed field: the engine takes no randomness, and this is seen by the version of `engine` in the record (R107 spec). The summary of issues `api` assembles from the records of choices and attempts, as decided: the time a skill was given is by the skills that the record holds, so the summary does not need a package that left the image.

## Module versions

**R07. A new attempt gets the latest published version of a module — the newest by semver version of the pinned index that is not withdrawn, in the format window of `api`, one for all learners, — and a task of a lesson gets the package on which the lesson began. The image holds all the versions of the pinned index in the format window, withdrawn ones too. The configuration is the file `modules.json` with the tag of a release of `modules` and the digest of its index: the index's signature is checked by CI of each pull request, and the release goes only forward. Attempts on a withdrawn version and outside the window are closed "unchecked" by a job after the deployment.**
- **What was decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3; sources — section 4):
  - the current version of a module is the newest by semver entry of the pinned index that the index has not marked `withdrawn` and whose format version is in the window of `api`: while the format is v0, only 0.2 (R44 spec). A mark with any reason, an unknown one too, `api` counts as a withdrawal (R63 spec). This is the same rule by which the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 26, chooses the version of a module that teaches an anchor skill. The current version is one for all learners. It changes with a pull request that moves the configuration to another release of `modules`, or with a change of the window — the move of `api` to a new line of `spec`, — and takes effect with the build of the image (R63 spec);
  - a new attempt outside a lesson is on the current version. A task of a lesson is an attempt on the package on which the lesson began: the lesson holds its digest, and what is recorded about it is decided by T12. On a withdrawn package or a package outside the window the lesson begins no new attempt and ends "unchecked", as an attempt does;
  - whether a module suits a learner, `api` judges by the current version: if it does not suit the learner, while the previous one did, for them the module is like a module without a current version, and `api` does not give them the previous version;
  - a module's connection is the module ID and the date of connection, without a version. `learner-model` and `engine` get the graphs of the current versions of the connected modules. A module without a current version stays connected, its evidence is in the log, but the engine does not get it, and there are no new attempts and lessons on it;
  - the image holds all the versions of the pinned index whose format version is in the window of `api`, withdrawn ones too — only for repeating a verdict (R63 spec; architecture 3.5 spec). A file of the device part lies in the image once, by its own digest. A package leaves the image when its format version leaves the window. Which versions to hold is said by the index, and the build needs no database;
  - attempts and lessons on withdrawn versions and on versions outside the window are closed by an operator job with the image of the new release, after the deployment, and before it, and wherever the job has not reached the attempt, by the check on request: it is mandatory, since a package outside the window is no longer in the image. The verdict is "unchecked" with a neutral phrase (R04 spec), the words of hints are deleted in the same transaction (R05), and the evidence is the outcome "unchecked" (concepts 11 spec);
  - the filter `wrong_key` at the recomputation of the model reads `withdrawn` on each entry of the index — and on the entries outside the window too: they are skipped only by the choice of the current version (R63 spec; [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 26);
  - the files of the device part and the open views of a withdrawn version `api` does not serve, and `app`, on the refusal, deletes the version's open views from the cache (R63 spec; architecture 3.1 spec). How this looks in the protocol is decided by T07;
  - the configuration is the file `modules.json` at the root of the repository: `release` is the tag of a release of `modules`, `index` is the digest `sha256:` of the bytes of its `module-index.json`. It lists no modules and holds no package digests. `api` embeds it in the binary by a Go package at the root of the module — `//go:embed` does not accept `..`, — and the image build reads the same file, without a copy;
  - a CI job of its own, on each pull request and push to `main`, takes the index and its bundle from the release, checks the signature by the recipe `release-verify` (R77 spec), checks the SHA-256 against `index`, rejects a release older than the one pinned on `main` and a pull request after which a module would lose its current version otherwise than by a withdrawal, and prints the current versions of the modules. The image build checks the index only by SHA-256, and the packages are checked by `module-kit` by the chain from it (R62, R77 spec);
  - in the table of section 3.2 there is a new row "connected modules", and the row of an attempt carries the module ID and its version too, so that the export can be read without the index.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-02 by the recommendations that the planning of T03 proposed. Closing by a job after the deployment, the filter over all entries, the refusal for a withdrawn digest, the release only forward and the check on each pull request are proposals of the plan after its independent review, and the author approved them together with the plan.
- **The latest published.** A fix of a key reaches the learner with the next attempt, and a withdrawal goes the same way for everyone — by the signed index (R63 spec). The engine and `learner-model` have one graph per module (R28 spec). A learner's place in a module is their skill model, not a position in a course: what comes next is chosen by the engine by the model (concepts 11, 12 spec), so a new version does not upset the place, and `retired_skills` carry progress over (R49 spec; T04). The state cache is recomputed with a change of release anyway (R06).
- **A lesson on its own package.** A lesson is short, and its segments go in the author's order, and the engine does not reorder them (concepts 5 spec). A new version could remove or reorder a segment in the middle of a lesson. `spec` says nothing about versions within a lesson, and the rule is a decision of `api`.
- **All the versions in the window.** The build does not go to the database, so CI and the image do not touch learners' data, and any begun attempt in the window stays checkable. In v0 the window is one format version, and modules move to a new one all at once (R44 spec; repositories 4 spec): the set of versions in the image is reset with each format version, and unchanged media files of new versions take no space.
- **The release and the digest of the index.** The configuration "a release of `modules` and its index" is required by R63 spec, and the chain of digests begins from the index: index → inventory → parts → files (R62, R77 spec). The digest pins the bytes whose signature CI checked, and from then on it holds the file (R77 spec).
- **Only forward.** A withdrawal is marked only by the index of the next releases (R63 spec): the index of an old release does not have it, and the withdrawn version would go into new attempts again.
- **Each pull request.** A required check whose workflow is skipped by a path filter stays pending and blocks the merge (GitHub documentation, section 4), and a new tag of `spec` may raise the minimum of R77 spec.

— *Rejected:*
- a version pinned to a module's connection, where minor ones come by themselves and a major one by an action of an adult or a learner: the image would hold each major on which anyone has stayed, without a limit, graphs and cache marks would be separate for each profile, and a fix in an old major would require a patch line from `modules`. A withdrawal moves the learner to another version anyway;
- a pinned version that changes only by an action: a fix of a key would not reach the learner until the version is withdrawn;
- a task of a lesson on the current version: a lesson would lose segments in the middle of a run;
- an image only with the versions for new attempts and those that the previous configuration gave: an attempt opened two pull requests back would lose its check, and a withdrawn version would leave the image contrary to architecture 3.5 spec;
- an image by the unfinished attempts from the database: CI or the build would go to the database with learners' data;
- a list of modules in the configuration: while all modules are official, the gate is the release of `modules`. If it is needed, by an amendment;
- package digests in the configuration: the index names them, and they would repeat the chain from it;
- closing only on request: open attempts on packages that are not in the image would stay in the database for long (architecture 3.10 spec), — until someone returns to them or the job for abandoned ones closes them (T05).

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-02 amendment to R06 — the cache mark and evidence on a previous version. T04 carries progress over with a change of the current version, T05 closes abandoned attempts in the same order, T07 carries the connection without a version, the refusal for a withdrawn digest and the module without a current version, T08 deletes connections with the profile, T12 records the lesson's package. What `api` needs from the layout of a release of `modules` is В6 in table 3 of RUN.md, and a rollback of the image with an old release is В7 to Т-19; both «в реестр spec». There is no configuration, CI job or build yet — Д8 in table 4 of RUN.md, and the delay of a withdrawal until the author's pull request is Д9. — RUN.md, T03, T04, T05, T07, T08, T09, T12; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.3, 2.4, 2.7, 2.9, 3.2, 3.3 and 4.
