The commit is the author's (rule 7 of RUN.md). — RUN.md, T07, T08, T09, T14; CLAUDE.md; README.md; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2, 3 and 4.

## Protocol `app` ↔ `api`

**R17. The protocol `app` ↔ `api` is the requests of the first slice by the table of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9. The contract of `spec` carries the messages, the services, the enumeration of causes and the refusals of each method, while the order of checks, the idempotency keys, CORS, the codecs, the interceptors and the serving of files are the code of `api`. A refusal is a Connect error with a standard code and a typed cause, an outcome is a field of a successful answer, and the causes are one enumeration. An action that a repeat must not do twice carries an idempotency key as a field of the message.**
- **What was decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9; sources — section 4):
  - the requests — by R03–R16 and the flows architecture 4.1, 4.2 and 4.5 spec: what comes in, what goes out and what the learner sees. Beyond what T07 named, the table has the profile's fields "ask before showing", the mode and voicing; the return to an open attempt on another device; three variants of beginning an attempt — an engine choice, a lesson's segment and the same task again; the module catalog apart from the connected modules; the request "something else", which calls the engine; an adult's progress. Consent and its status and the quota for the section for adults come "with children and tariffs", by epic E4 of `spec`, and are not decided here (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R11);
  - the boundary. In the contract of `spec` — the messages and the services, the enumeration of causes, the refusals of each method — which causes it returns and with what code —, the idempotency level of the methods and the form of the path of a file of the device part with its answers. In the code of `api` — the order of checks of section 3.1, the rights of OpenFGA, the idempotency keys and their records, CORS, the codecs, the interceptors, the serving of files and the HTTP cache. Section 3.9 speaks at the level of meaning, without the names of messages and fields. This is a recommendation to T62–T64 of epic E3 of RUN.md of `spec` — В25 in table 3 of RUN.md;
  - a learning request and an adult action on a profile carry the profile ID, also when the matter is an attempt, a run or a choice: `api` first checks the right to the profile, and reads them by the pair "profile ID, their ID", so another's and nonexistent ones get one refusal, "not found" (R04). An account is created by one request — the invite code and the owner's statement that they are an adult —, and before it any request of the account gets "no access" (R04, section 3.1);
  - refusal and outcome. If the action did not happen — a Connect error with a standard code and a cause with data: the places of ended segments, the time of the next day, the close reason of the attempt. If the action happened and it changes what the learner sees — a field of a successful answer. An exhausted quota does not refuse a submission — the verdict "unchecked" with the cause "quota" (R11) —, but it does not begin a new attempt or segment, and that is a refusal (R11, R13). "No other words" is an outcome: there was a call, and the right is spent; "already asked" is a refusal: there is no call (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R10);
  - the causes are one enumeration for refusals and outcomes: "quota" is a refusal at the beginning of an attempt or a segment and an outcome on a submission. It also has causes that R04–R15 did not name: "ladder not passed" — showing the solution to a child before the fourth wrong answer, "no showing" — at a task with a rubric, "no rung" — "explain differently" without a rung, "no more words" — the correct answer words a second time or after a new attempt, "not found", "PIN locked", "PIN already set" — "set PIN" when there is one, "segment unavailable" — a segment's attempt out of order, "key not for this profile", "key already used". The list and the Connect codes are the table of section 3.9, as a recommendation to the contract;
  - what `app` does by the code: `unauthenticated` — a re-sign-in, while "fresh sign-in needed" and "account gone" are places for T08; `unimplemented` — only an offer to reload; `unavailable` — a temporary refusal, and `app` repeats it with a growing pause, but not for long, and then shows a failure; the rest `app` shows in its own words by the cause. A cause that its build does not know it reads by the code: in binary protobuf it arrives as a number (R18);
  - the idempotency key is a field of the message, not a header: CORS allows only `Authorization` and the headers of Connect (R18). It is needed by the beginning of an attempt, a submission, the words of a rung and the correct answer words, "explain differently", showing the solution and the request "something else". `api` writes it at the beginning of the request, in the same transaction as the reserve of the quota (R12) or the action itself, with the method and the target — an attempt, a run or, for "something else", the profile; the same key with another method or target is the refusal "key already used". A repeat while the first is going is the refusal "still going"; after it, an answer by what is recorded, and the repeat does not call AI and does not spend the quota a second time. For the words of a rung and "differently", while the attempt is open, these are their words, which the database holds anyway (R05, R10), for a submission with a rubric, how it ended, without content (R15), and for the correct answer words, an answer without words: they are nowhere (R09), and a lost answer is lost words. "Lesson passed", "Done", the beginning of a step and "Hint" repeat by the state, without a key;
  - the key's record — the key, the method, the profile ID, the target, a reference to what is recorded — a submission or a choice —, the state and the time, without content. "Going" lives no longer than the request's time limit: a request that outlived it has crashed, its reserve stays spending (R12), and the repeat goes again. The operator's daily retention job deletes records older than a day, so a record lives no longer than 2 days; the records are deleted with the profile and do not go into the export: this is a service field (R05);
  - the engine choice. The answer by which an attempt outside a lesson ended — "correct", showing the solution or "Done" —, and the answer by which a lesson run ended, carries the next engine choice: by the result of an attempt, evidence, then the engine (architecture 3.5 spec; R13). In a lesson the answer to the end of a segment carries the place of the next segment or "no more segments" (R13). The request "something else" calls the engine itself — the learner asks for another task, chose a skill on the skill map (concepts 12 spec) or did not get an answer with a choice —, and closes the open attempt with the reason "left" (R10, R11). The choice begins nothing (R13): `app` begins an attempt or a run by it;
  - what goes to the device. With a task and in the answers of an attempt — never traps, neither the catalog nor the matched trap: the words of a rung in `api` get it (architecture 4.1 spec; R57 spec). The key and the task's solution — only with an attempt closed by a correct answer or by showing the solution (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05 spec). The slot checks, the skills, the band, the difficulty, the techniques — except the IDs of those given —, the simpler case and the leak phrases — never (R51, R56, R57 spec), the attempt seed neither (R59 spec), and the criteria of the rubric and the marks — not to the learner (R15). `api` does not give an own key back. The marks and the repeating traps to an adult are progress: it names a trap by `description` and `trap_advice`, as [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 16, requires, only by closed attempts. This is В27, and until `spec` has recorded it, progress carries no traps;
  - for a connected module without a current version — for all or for this learner (R07) — `app` gets the mark "no current version", without a digest: the module stays in the list, it can be disconnected, and there are no attempts and lessons on it;
  - the data of the skill map are by profile and module: the state of the skills of the current version, as `learner-model` gives it, and `app` takes the graph and the tiers by the digest of the current version. A skill without a pair and a waiting skill are not on the map (R08);
  - the rights are rows of the table of actions of section 3.1: the module catalog, connecting and disconnecting a module — `can_learn`; the skill map to the learner — `can_learn`, progress to an adult — `can_view`; voicing is written by the learner too, `can_learn`, and only it (Д74 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`); the own key check and the list of providers — `can_manage` of the owner profile of the category `adult`; the request of withdrawn digests — the account from the token.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T07 — refusal and outcome, one task for the whole protocol — and what the plan added after the independent review and the author approved with it: the boundary, one enumeration of causes, the idempotency key as a field, the rows of the table beyond «Сделать». What the plan did not name — the rights to modules, the map and progress, the Connect codes of the causes, the names of the new causes, the term of the key's record, the engine choice in the answer by which an attempt ended, the mark "no current version" — is the reading of the executor of T07, not the author's words.
- **The boundary.** In the contract is what both sides know: `app` builds the request and reads the answer, and the stubs of the services are born in `spec` (R83 spec). `app` does not see the order of checks, the keys and CORS, and their place is the code of `api`. The refusals of a method are in the contract, because by them `app` decides what to show.
- **Refusal and outcome.** The Connect code says what `app` does with any refusal: signs in again, offers to reload, repeats or shows. The cause says what to show. An outcome is in the answer, because the learner sees it as an ordinary screen: "unchecked" is not a failure of the request (R04 spec).
- **The key as a field.** A header that is not among those CORS allows the browser would refuse already at the preflight. A key recorded at the beginning of the request does not let a repeat, while the first waits for AI, spend the quota twice — the independent review of the plan found this. The target in the record — so that a key repeated by mistake with another attempt does not get its answer: the review of T07 found this. R09 does not store the correct answer words, and storing them for the sake of a repeat would mean keeping the words longer than they are needed (privacy 1 spec).
- **The choice in the answer.** Evidence and the choice are one chain in `api` (architecture 4.1 spec), and `learner-model` and `engine` are computations without input and output (repositories 1 spec): the answer to the end of an attempt does not wait for AI, and the learner does not wait for one more request.
- **Traps.** The trap catalog is closed whole: "A trap tells what mistake a task expects, and the learner's app does not get it" ([format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 16). The matched trap in the answer to a submission, as T62 of epic E3 of RUN.md of `spec` names it, goes to В25.
- **Rights.** Connecting is `can_learn`, because "The learner connects the module" (product 5 spec), and on a shared device the server does not tell people apart (R04). Progress is `can_view`, because at stage 5 a teacher reads it (section 3.1).

— *Rejected:*
- "result or refusal" as a field of each answer: it would duplicate the Connect errors; Connect codes without a cause: one code would cover a dozen different refusals;
- the idempotency key as a header: CORS will not let it through; a key on each request: "lesson passed", "Done", the beginning of a step and "Hint" repeat by the state anyway;
- storing the correct answer words for the sake of a repeat: R09 does not store them;
- the matched trap in the answer to a submission: traps do not go to the device;
- the engine choice only by a separate request: an extra request after each attempt, while the card calls the engine by the result of an attempt (architecture 3.5 spec);
- splitting T07 by T62–T64: the common rules are decided once (the author's decision);
- the names of messages and fields in section 3.9: there would be a second source of truth beside the contract.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-02 amendments to R10 — "no other words" is an outcome — and to R11 — the quota on a submission is an outcome, and the request of an adult's quota is E4 of `spec`. The table of actions of section 3.1 gets the rows of modules, the map, progress, voicing and the own key, the table of section 3.2 — the row of idempotency keys. What `api` needs from the contract is В25, an adult's progress — В27 in table 3 of RUN.md, both «в реестр spec». There is no protocol in code — Д14 in table 4. T08 takes the places "fresh sign-in needed" and "account gone". — RUN.md, T07, T08; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.7, 3.1, 3.2, 3.3, 3.4, 3.6, 3.7, 3.8 and 3.9.

— *Amendment 2026-11-02:* the places of T08 are taken (R20). "Fresh sign-in needed" is `unauthenticated` with the allowed age of the sign-in in seconds, "account gone" is `unauthenticated` without data. The "re-sign-in" by the code `unauthenticated` is a sign-in of the same user, without signing out: signing out erases everything of the account on the device (R04 app), and a build that signed in again through signing out would erase the device. `app` reads the cause before any refresh of the token: without a cause, it refreshes the token and repeats the request, and opens the sign-in window only if the refresh failed; on "fresh sign-in needed" it signs in again by the window, and on "account gone" it erases everything of the account and signs out, without refreshing the token: for a deleted user the refresh would end with the SDK's sign-out. The deletion of a profile and of the account carries no idempotency key and repeats by the state: a repeat of the deletion of a profile gets "no profile", a repeat of the deletion of the account — "account gone", and `app` reads both as "deleted already" (R21). The recommendations to the contract are В29 in table 3 of RUN.md. — R20, R21; RUN.md, T08; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.9 and 3.10.

— *Amendment 2026-11-07:* the contract came out — the package `novatrail.api.v1` in `spec` v0.9.4 (R97–R100 spec). The messages, fields, causes and Connect codes are now its own: where the table of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9, says otherwise, the contract is right, and how it differs, "What the contract decided" lists in the same place. Among the differences are the clock offset only for requests to platform AI (the amendment of the same day to R11), a child's profile through "waiting for consent" and `CreateChildProfile`, the reason code of a choice only in progress, the new causes "already exists", "PIN not set" and "answer not current", the idempotency key on the requests of the PIN and of consent, and "Done" with the solved task (the amendment of the same day to R15). The order of checks, the records of keys and how to tell another request under the same key are the code of `api`. — R23; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.9 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T34; R44): the records of keys of an attempt — in code. The record of the beginning of an attempt holds the answer: it has nothing of what the learner sent. The record of a submission holds a reference to the submission, and a repeat gets its recorded answer: the answer of a submission carries the chosen options, while the key's record is without content. The fingerprint of both is SHA-256 (R35). Keys are led by the common helper `rpc.Once`: both `AccountService` and the attempts call it. There is no record "going" yet: requests without AI do everything in one transaction with the record, and "going" will come with the calls of AI (T36).

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T36; R46): the record "going" — in code, for a submission that calls AI: `rpc.Across` writes it in the first transaction, with the checks of the attempt, and commits it before the call, and the second transaction after the call turns it into "done" with the submission. A repeat while it is younger than the request's time limit is "still going"; if older, the request has crashed, and the repeat goes again. A refusal after the call deletes it, so the repeat is checked again. No transaction is open for the time of the call.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T37; R47): the key's record of the words of a rung and of "explain differently" holds the answer without the words — the rung and the outcome —, and a repeat adds to it the words that the attempt stores while it is open: so the words live only with the attempt and go with its closing (R05). A repeat when the wrong answer is no longer the last is "answer not current", and on a closed attempt "attempt closed". For the correct answer words, a repeat gets the answer without words, with the flag "repeat". `rpc.Key.Repeat` is now called for any record of the method that set it, not only for a record with a reference to a submission.

**R18. On the wire is binary protobuf, and the server accepts JSON too, with the proto names. The module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps go without a token, by Connect GET, and a file of the device part by HTTP GET by the package digest and the path, with answers of its own to a withdrawn version and to a deployment. `app` learns of a version withdrawal by a request of its own. CORS is on all paths and answers, and the interceptor's log is the method, the code, the cause and the time.**
- **What was decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9; sources — section 4):
  - the wire is binary protobuf: `app` turns it on in the transport connect-web, which by default sends JSON. A new value of an enumeration — a cause, a close reason of an attempt — that a release of `spec` added to the package `v1`, binary protobuf carries to an earlier build of `app` as a number: a proto3 enumeration is open. In JSON the server writes the value's name, and connect-es skips an unknown name, and the field reads empty: a new close reason would look like "attempt open". The crops of the work are `bytes`, in JSON a base64 string, a third longer, and the crop of a handwriting check goes twice;
  - the server accepts JSON too — for debugging. The standard JSON codec of connect-go writes the names in lowerCamelCase, so `api` sets its own — `protojson` with `UseProtoNames`, as R83 spec requires, and with `DiscardUnknown`, as the standard one;
  - without a token — the module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps (R03): methods with the idempotency level `NO_SIDE_EFFECTS`, and `app` takes them by Connect GET without headers — and without `Connect-Timeout-Ms`, `app` sets no deadline for these calls. Such a GET goes without a preflight, is cached by HTTP and carries no profile ID. The level is decided before the first release of the contract: changing it is a breaking change for `buf breaking` `FILE`. This is a recommendation to T64 — В25;
  - the open view in the contract is the bytes of the format's JSON and the format version beside them: `api` and `app` read it by the format's types, and by the version `app` knows whether it will read the view (R44, R72 spec; «origin `api` и версии» of table 1 of RUN.md of `spec`). A recommendation to T64 — В25;
  - a file of the device part is not a Connect method but an HTTP GET by the package digest and the file's path, without a token and headers: such a `fetch` goes without a preflight and through the HTTP cache. The answers are `200` with the file; `404` — the part of this package has no such path; `410` — the image no longer gives the version: it is withdrawn or outside the window; `503` with `Retry-After` — a digest that the image's index does not know, that is, a deployment is going on (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R07). The form of the path and these answers are the contract's text, to T64 — В25;
  - the HTTP cache of open views and files is a day: by the digest they do not change, and a withdrawal reaches learners only with the author's pull request anyway (Д9 in table 4 of RUN.md);
  - the version withdrawal is a request of its own, "which of these digests does the image no longer give", from the account from the token, without a profile ID. The answer for each digest is "gives", "no longer gives" or "does not know": a digest that the image's index does not know is a deployment, not a withdrawal, as the 2026-11-02 amendment to R07 decided. `app` sends the request at start — with the digests whose open views it keeps —, and after the refusal "version withdrawn" or the answer `410`, deletes the open views of "no longer gives", and asks about the unknown ones later (Д68 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`; the 2026-11-02 amendment to R07);
  - CORS — when `api` is on its own origin: the exact origin of `app` from the configuration, on all paths and all answers, errors too; the methods `GET` and `POST`; the headers — `Authorization`, `Content-Type`, `Connect-Protocol-Version` and `Connect-Timeout-Ms`; `Access-Control-Max-Age` — 7200 s; `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` — `Retry-After`: it is not in the safelist of response headers. On the origin of `app` CORS is not needed;
  - logs: the Connect interceptor writes the method, the code, the cause of a refusal and the time. It writes neither the body, nor the headers, nor the address of the request (R09 spec): in the body are the work and the learner's data, in a header the token, and in a GET's address the message.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T07 — binary protobuf, if the primary sources of connect-es and protobuf-es confirm the arguments, a JSON codec with the proto names, four open views through Connect GET, `410` and `503` for a file, withdrawal by a request of its own, CORS and logs —, and T07 checked the arguments (section 4):
- connect-web — "By default, connect-web clients use the JSON format"; connect-es — "In connect-es, unknown JSON fields are ignored by default", and this mode of protobuf-es also skips "unrecognized enum string representations". In binary form protobuf-es keeps an open enumeration as a number: "Open enums can contain numeric values that are not declared in the generated TypeScript enum". ProtoJSON: "Adding values to an enum is safe if the "Emit enum values as integers" is set on all relevant clients". `bytes` in JSON — "standard base64 encoding with paddings". Both arguments were confirmed, and no question to the author is needed;
- the JSON codec of connect-go v2.0.2 is `protojson.MarshalOptions{Resolver: resolved.resolver}`, without `UseProtoNames`, and the codec's fields can be changed: "Other settings can be configured by mutating the exported fields before use";
- Connect GET — "These requests do not require any special HTTP headers … They are designed to be easy to make and easy to cache in browsers, proxies, and CDNs", and the server of connect-go serves GET only to methods `NO_SIDE_EFFECTS`. The buf rule `RPC_SAME_IDEMPOTENCY_LEVEL` is in the category `FILE`;
- Fetch: `Authorization` is not in the safelist of headers, and a request with it goes with a preflight, and the preflight cache is by the exact address. A `fetch` without a cache mode of its own goes through the HTTP cache.
- The cache for a day, `410` outside the window too, `Retry-After` in `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` and the request of withdrawn ones at start are the reading of the executor of T07, not the author's words.

— *Rejected:*
- JSON on the wire: the arguments are above. JSON with numbers instead of the names of enumerations: the names would not be readable in JSON either, and base64 would stay;
- open views by POST: when `api` is on its own origin, each method comes with its own preflight, and POST has no HTTP cache; open views by token — see the 2026-11-02 amendment to R03;
- the open view as `google.protobuf.Struct`: without types, and the numbers in it are `double`; as messages of its own: beside the format's schemas (R45 spec) there would be a second schema in `.proto`;
- a withdrawal inferred from a refusal to give an open view: `app` takes open views from the cache and does not reach `api` with them (Д68 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`); `404` for a withdrawn version — cannot be told from an error of the path; an answer without CORS — `fetch` would not tell it from a network failure;
- an HTTP cache for a year, as for an immutable file: a withdrawn version would live in the browser's cache longer than a withdrawal takes.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-02 amendments to R03 — `fetch`, CORS and the arguments — and to R07 — withdrawal by a request of its own. В25 in table 3 of RUN.md carries GET and the idempotency level, the open view as the bytes of JSON, the path of a file with its answers and the request of withdrawn ones. — RUN.md, T07; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.3 and 3.9.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T32; R42): the cache and the answers — in code. The answer `200` of an open view and of a file carries `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400`, and a file also an `ETag` — the digest of the file itself —, so a repeat with `If-None-Match` gets `304`. A refusal is `no-store`, and a file's `503` comes with `Retry-After: 30`. A request of a file writes its own row of the log, `package_file` — the status and the time, without the address: the Connect interceptor does not write it, and the request logs of Cloud Run are excluded.

**R19. There is no real-time channel in the first slice (Т-25): the check of handwriting is two requests, the submission with the verdict and then the tutor's words, and an essay is one request. Come back to the channel together with voice, at stage 3.**
- **What was decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9; sources — section 4):
  - the check of handwriting is two requests, as in architecture 4.1 spec: the submission and the verdict, then the words of the rung, for which `app` sends the work once more. A tapped choice is one request, without AI (architecture 4.2 spec). An essay is one request: the reading and the assessment, and the answer is the read text and the feedback (R14);
  - how long the learner waits is an estimate by the measurements of `tutor` be607e5 on Opus 5.5 · `high`, section 3.9. If the readers of the pair go in parallel, on "not yet" the verdict comes in ≈ 3–4 s, and the question ≈ 5 s later; one after the other, the verdict ≈ 2–3 s later. With one answer the verdict would wait for the question too, ≈ 8–9 s. A channel would bring the verdict just the same, and the question earlier only by the overhead of the second request: the token check with revocation, the preflight, if `api` is on its own origin and the entry of its cache has expired, and the crop once more. An essay is ≈ 12 s by one request, and there is nothing to split it into: it has no verdict. The measurement is on the first app (Д14 in table 4 of RUN.md);
  - the first app runs locally: `app` and `api` on one machine, and the preflight is local, while the token check, the database and the AI provider are over the network, as in a deployment;
  - the recommendation for Т-25 is «в реестр spec», В26 in table 3 of RUN.md: decided for the first slice, open until voice (Т-13).

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T07. The author of canvas-api rejected giving everything without a channel and ordered that this not be proposed without new facts (R41 canvas-api). The new facts:
- the tutor's words go out whole, after the leak check (R54 spec): there is no stream of words to `app`, and a channel would carry only events;
- the reading and the analysis are two calls, and the verdict is ready after the reading (R67 spec): the answer of the first request is the early verdict, and a channel would carry one event — the same one;
- the rules of the contracts do not allow streams (R83 spec), and the architecture has no queues or event bus of its own (R11 spec; architecture 7 spec);
- the argument of R23 canvas-api itself — on Cloud Run «фоновая работа после ответа почти не получает процессора», so everything goes inside the request — holds here too: each of the two requests does its work inside itself.

The argument «canvas-api в своём E2 шёл без Centrifugo», which stood in the question to the author, is not a new fact: R60 canvas-api keeps Centrifugo in the stack, and it is absent in E2 «потому, что в E2 нет беты». It was removed from the arguments.

— *Rejected:*
- an event channel, like Centrifugo in canvas-api: a service of its own and a subscription for the sake of one event, which comes anyway as the answer of the first request;
- one answer with the verdict and the words: the verdict would wait for the words, ≈ 5 s;
- a stream in the contract: `UNARY_RPC` does not let it in (R83 spec).

— *Consequence:* in [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 1, there is no channel by decision, not until an answer to Т-25. The recommendation for Т-25 is В26 in table 3 of RUN.md, the measurement of time is Д14 in table 4. — RUN.md, T07; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.7 and 3.9.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T42; Д14): instead of the estimate, a measurement on the first app. `app` and `api` ran on the author's machine, the database was Neon `aws-us-east-1`, and the reading went by the pair Opus 5.5 · `high` and Haiku 5.5 · `medium`. The server's time by the log of `serve`, 10 checks of handwriting:
- the verdict, `SubmitWork` as a whole, p50 9.3 s, 6.3–17.9 s;
- of it, the reading by the pair p50 6.7 s, 3.3–15.2 s, and the rest — the token, the database and the checks — p50 2.7 s;
- the second request without a call, `GetRungWords` with the fallback text, 1.2 s: this is the overhead that a channel would win.

The verdict is three times longer than the estimate, but the argument of the decision holds, by the author's decision of 2026-11-12. A channel would bring the verdict no earlier than the answer of the first request, and the words earlier only by ≈ 1 s of overhead; one answer would make the verdict wait for the words too. Why the reading is longer than the judge of `tutor` that the estimate went by, and which model of the pair holds it back, is В53 in table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md), «в реестр spec»: the time in the log is one for the pair. The overhead is a property of the local run: each request with the database goes from the author's machine to `aws-us-east-1`. The time of the tutor's words is still not measured — `tutor` admits no models for the words (Д25) — and stays in Д14.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T45; Д14; R54): a measurement of the words, on `tutor` v0.1.24, where they are admitted. The server's time, at the author's with `app`:

| What | p50 | Range |
|---|---|---|
| the words of a rung, `GetRungWords`, 3 calls | 10.6 s | 9.8–12.7 s |
| of them, the call of Opus 5.5 · `high` | 7.9 s | 7.4–11.1 s |
| "explain differently", 3 calls | 8.7 s | 7.5–8.9 s |
| the verdict in the same run, 8 checks | 5.3 s | 4.2–6.1 s, the reading by the pair p50 3.0 s |

Opus thought 408–802 tokens, and the estimate by T76 of `tutor` is p50 5.1 s. The words are twice as long as the estimate, and the argument of the decision holds for the same reason as for the verdict: the verdict does not wait for them, and a channel would bring the words earlier only by the overhead of the second request. So the author decided 2026-11-12. Why the words are longer than the measurement of `tutor` is В54 in table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md), «в реестр spec». The verdict in this run is twice as fast as in the first: Haiku · `medium` thought 225–582 tokens against 286–1047, so the time of the reading changes noticeably from run to run.

## Deletion and export

**R20. A fresh sign-in is an `auth_time` no older than 5 minutes: it is required by the deletion of a profile, the deletion of the account and the change of a forgotten PIN. The token check tells apart "expired", "revoked", "no such user", "disabled" and "failure", and "fresh sign-in needed" and "account gone" are refusals with the code `unauthenticated` and a cause of their own.**
- **What was decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.10; sources — section 4):
  - a fresh sign-in is an `auth_time` of a token that passed the check no older than 300 s by the clock of `api`. An `auth_time` ahead of the clock of `api` by no more than 300 s is fresh too: this is the same tolerance that the Admin SDK gives the token's expiry. A fresh sign-in is required by three actions — the deletion of a profile, the deletion of the account and the change of a forgotten PIN. The export and the other adult actions do not require it, and the PIN is not a confirmation (architecture 3.5 spec; spec 12.2 canvas-api);
  - the class of a token check refusal. An expired token and the token of a disabled user are `unauthenticated` without a cause: the SDK will refresh the token or sign out by itself, and `api` does not disable users. A revoked one: `api` reads its UID by `VerifyIDToken`, by Google's keys from the cache, without `GetUser`: if the account is marked "being deleted", this is "account gone", otherwise `unauthenticated` without a cause. No such user is "account gone", and if the UID has an account without the mark — the user was deleted bypassing `api`, for example in the project's console —, `api` deletes the account by the route of R21, without a fresh sign-in and in this same request: step 1, and step 3 is done already. What did not pass the operator's job finishes. Any other failure of the check is `unavailable`. The errors of the SDK are not wrapped before the helpers `Is…`;
  - the order of the checks of section 3.1: the class of the token, then the mark "being deleted" — on any request, the creation of the account and the redemption of a code too —, then steps 1–7, and the fresh sign-in last, after the right;
  - "fresh sign-in needed" is `unauthenticated`, and in the data the allowed age of the sign-in in seconds, 300, as `max_age` of RFC 9470. Freshness is checked only by the three actions above;
  - "account gone" is `unauthenticated`, without data, on any request with a token: when the account is marked "being deleted", when the sign-in service answers that there is no such user, and when the token is revoked and the mark is there. A UID without an account whose user is alive at the sign-in service gets "no access", as before (R04), and a UID whose user is gone gets "account gone": an account could not be created for it;
  - how `app` refreshes the sign-in `app` decides, and the recommendations of `api` are for T63, В29 in table 3 of RUN.md: compare `authTime` with the `issuedAtTime` of the token just refreshed, not with the device's clock, sign in again by the window on a press, and on `auth/user-mismatch` ask `api`;
  - other devices: "account gone" reaches them while their ID token lives, about an hour after `DeleteUser`. Later the Firebase SDK signs out by itself with `auth/user-token-expired`, and the device does not reach `api` — В30.

— *Why:* the author's decision of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T08 — 5 minutes — and what the plan added after an independent check and the author approved together with it. 5 minutes, as R14 canvas-api and the sample check in the Firebase documentation: `< 5 * 60`, otherwise 401. The Connect protocol defines the code `unauthenticated` so: "Caller doesn't have valid authentication credentials for the operation", and for "account gone" "if the user can't be identified" fits too. The code itself tells `app` to sign in again, so a build that does not know the cause does what is needed. RFC 9470 also answers a sign-in that is not fresh enough with 401, with `max_age`. The class of refusal is needed because `VerifyIDTokenAndCheckRevoked` does not give the token on a refusal, and names a deleted user `IsUserNotFound`, which `IsIDTokenInvalid` does not cover: without an analysis of its own, "account gone" cannot be told from an expired sign-in or from a failure. The fresh sign-in is last so that a repeat of a deletion after a lost answer gets "account gone" or "no profile" without a new sign-in window.

— *Rejected:*
- "fresh sign-in needed" by the code `failed_precondition` or `permission_denied`: a build that does not know the cause would not sign in again by the code;
- "account gone" by the code `not_found`: this is the code of "no profile", and `app` must sign out;
- the PIN instead of a fresh sign-in: the PIN is a barrier of the interface (architecture 3.5 spec; R04);
- a fresh sign-in for the export: the same token reads the profile, the progress and the attempts anyway, and the export with deletion in one step goes through a fresh sign-in anyway;
- 10 minutes — the window for other hands on the device is twice as wide; 2 minutes — an owner who paused to think on the confirmation screen would sign in once more;
- deciding for `app` how it refreshes the sign-in: this is its code, and `api` checks only `auth_time`.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-02 amendments to R03 — the class of refusal and the order of deletion —, to R04 — the order of the checks and the irreversible actions — and to R17 — the places of T08 and the "re-sign-in". What this adds to the contract is В29, the other devices are В30 in table 3 of RUN.md, both «в реестр spec». There is no classification in the code — Д15 in table 4. — RUN.md, T08; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.9 and 3.10.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T22; R32): a sixth class of refusal, "invalid". This is a token that the SDK did not accept: broken, empty, of another project or signed not by a Google key — `IsIDTokenInvalid` for a token that is not expired, not revoked and whose user is not disabled. The answer is `unauthenticated` without a cause, as for an expired one. "Failure" is only what kept the check from ending: Google's keys that cannot be got, the network, a 5xx answer of the sign-in service. Its answer is still `unavailable`. Otherwise a forged token would get `unavailable`, and `app` would repeat the request. — R32; RUN.md, T22; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.10.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T42; Д15): a re-sign-in by the window is a new sign-in. The author signed out of `app` and signed in again through Google, and the time of the last sign-in of their user at the sign-in service changed from 2026-11-11 23:51:50 to 2026-11-12 00:35:32 UTC. `auth_time` is "The time when the user authenticated" ([Firebase](https://firebase.google.com/docs/auth/admin/verify-id-tokens), read 2026-11-12). The executor did not see the token of the window itself: `app` has no screens of deletion and of the PIN change yet. So `auth_time` was checked indirectly, and directly it will be by the first deletion from `app`. A fresh sign-in is reachable, and the decision stays. `DeleteAccount` passed on the dev project with a token of the same user, got through a custom token, as in R39: "deleted", and the next request "account gone" with the token class "no such user".

**R21. A profile is deleted separately from the account: by a transaction of the database, then the tuple of OpenFGA. The account by the mark "being deleted" in the same transaction as all its data, then by the tuples, the revocation of tokens and the user at the sign-in service, and last by the account's row; what did not pass the operator's job finishes. Of a child consent a minimal record remains, of an adult's consent nothing. The users of the sign-in service without an account of `api` are deleted after 30 days without a sign-in.**
- **What was decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.10; sources — section 4):
  - a profile is deleted separately from the account (R26 spec), by `can_manage` and a fresh sign-in (R20). The owner profile the same way, and the last profile does not delete the account: the account holds the admission, the tariff and the counters. First one transaction: the account's row `FOR KEY SHARE`, with a check of the mark, the profile's row `FOR UPDATE`, and everything about the profile is deleted — the attempts, the observations, the words of hints, the submissions with a rubric, the lesson runs, the evidence log, the cache, the engine choices, the connected modules, the profile's counters, the idempotency keys and the profile itself. The child consent becomes a minimal record, an adult's consent is deleted whole, and the account's counters stay (R12). After `COMMIT`, the tuple of OpenFGA, and the answer "deleted". If the tuple was not deleted, it is "dangling": there is no row of the profile, and a request by it gets "no profile", and the daily job removes it. A repeat at which the tuple is there and the row is not deletes the tuple and answers "no profile", the same refusal as step 4 of section 3.1;
  - the account, by the account from the token and a fresh sign-in:
    1. one transaction. The account's row `FOR UPDATE`, and on it the mark "being deleted" with the time. Everything about each profile, as above. The child consents into minimal records, the adults' consents and the "waiting" consents without a profile whole. The account's counters and the account's fields: the statement, the PIN, the tariff, the offset, the end of the day, the date of the last request. The binding of the invite code is removed, and the code stays redeemed: SHA-256 without a binding is not personal data (R29 canvas-api), and a new account needs a new code (R12). The account's row keeps the ID, the UID of the sign-in service and the time of the mark;
    2. the tuples of all profiles: `api` reads them by `user:<account ID>` and deletes them in batches, and skips the missing ones (R27 canvas-api);
    3. `RevokeRefreshTokens`, then `DeleteUser`; "no such user" means that the step is done already. The step goes even if step 2 did not pass, and it is done when `DeleteUser` passed: if the revocation did not pass, `DeleteUser` goes anyway, since a refresh token does not serve a deleted user;
    4. only when steps 2 and 3 have passed, a transaction: the cleanup of step 1 once more, for the rows that were written late, and the account's row itself;
    5. the answer "deleted", and `app` erases everything of the account and signs out.

    If steps 2–4 did not pass, the answer is "deleted" anyway: there is nothing about the learners in the database, and the job finishes the rest. A repeat after a lost answer gets "account gone" (R20), and `app` does the same;
  - races. Each write transaction under an account takes its row `FOR KEY SHARE` and checks the mark, and the deletion of the account takes it `FOR UPDATE`: a request that passed the checks before the mark creates under a marked account neither a profile, nor a consent, nor a PIN, nor a counter. The lock is `FOR KEY SHARE`, not `FOR SHARE`: a request that then updates the account's row itself — the date of the request, the PIN counter — would with `FOR SHARE` fall into a deadlock with a neighbouring request of the same account, and `FOR KEY SHARE` does not hinder such an update and still conflicts with the `FOR UPDATE` of deletion. The operator's command that confirms a consent rejects a marked account. A row about a profile that is written after its deletion fails on the foreign key, and an `UPDATE` that did not find the row answers "no profile". The quota reserve of a deleted profile is counted only to the account's counter (R12);
  - the operator's job is the daily job of retention periods (R05). It finishes the marks older than the request timeout: steps 2–4. It deletes by period by the same route, only without a fresh sign-in, and checks the period under the lock of the row: a profile after 12 months without activity, an account after 12 months without requests (П-9). It deletes the users of the sign-in service without an account of `api` who have not signed in or refreshed the token for 30 days: the iteration is `Users` of the Admin SDK, and the time is `LastLogInTimestamp` and `LastRefreshTimestamp` of its `UserMetadata`. And, as before, it cleans the "dangling" tuples and the `changelog` of OpenFGA. A period that depends on the job is called "until the next successful pass of the job"; in the first app the job is an operator's command that the author runs (R76 spec);
  - consent (П-14). A minimal record is only for a child consent: the ID of the consent record, the date, the method of the check and the date of deletion, without the account's and the profile's ID, the pseudonym, the category and the country. It can be linked to the family only through the export file at the owner and the signed form at the operator (П-1). The period is until the lawyer's answer on П-14. In the first slice there are no children, so there are no records either. The withdrawal and the confirmation of consent are not decided here: this is epic E4 of `spec` (П-1);
  - the honest periods are the table "What stays where, and for how long" of section 3.10: the database of `api`, the mark, a "dangling" tuple, the `changelog`, the history of Neon, the sign-in service and its users without an account, the logs of `api`, of Cloud Run and of the hosting of `app`, the AI providers, the eval samples, payment, this device and other devices, the export file, the account's counters, the invite code, the link "Sign in with Google" in the owner's Google account (privacy 1 spec; architecture 4.5, 5 spec);
  - the export. Of a profile by `can_manage`, of the account by the account from the token, without a fresh sign-in (R20). The content is the column "in the export" of the table of section 3.2: for an attempt, the close reason, for a lesson's task, the run ID and the segment place, for a child's profile, "ask before showing", the lesson runs, the submissions with a rubric, the counters — the profile's in the profile's export, the account's in the account's export —, the consents whole, the date of the last request. Without secrets and service data: the UID, the PIN hash, its counter and lock, the binding of the code, the seed, the idempotency keys and the tuples (R05). The format of the export is epic E4 of `spec` (R76 spec), and "export and delete in one step" (privacy 7 spec) is at `app`: a fresh sign-in, the export, the deletion.

— *Why:* the author's decisions of 2026-11-02 in the planning of T08 — the mark and the job, a record only for a child, 30 days for the users without an account — and what the plan added after an independent check and the author approved together with it. First the database, then the tuples, as R27 canvas-api: in the reverse order a failure after the tuples made a repeat impossible. The mark and the job close what was a hole in canvas-api: there only the client led a repeat, and a failure after the database left the Firebase user without a period, and after the revocation of tokens a repeat required a new sign-in. The mark keeps the UID, without which the sign-in service cannot be finished, and answers the other devices "account gone". Step 3 goes even without step 2, because the sign-in service holds the identity data, for up to 180 days after deletion too, and the tuples only IDs. The minimal record is the recommendation of П-14 "keep a minimal record — the date and the method of the check, without the child's data", and the record's ID is there so that it can be checked against the signed form at the operator. An adult's consent has no form, there is nothing to check the record against, and keeping it would mean keeping it "with no use at all" — the argument of R05. A user of the sign-in service without an account appears for everyone who signed in without an invite code, and for one who signed in again after deletion: the SDK gives them a new UID. Without the job nobody would delete them, and the 180 days of Firebase begin only with deletion. 30 days are enough to get a code.

— *Rejected:*
- as in canvas-api, without the mark and the job: a failure after the database would leave the user of the sign-in service without a period;
- the sign-in service first: on a failure of the database the owner could not sign in to repeat, and the data would stay without an owner until the period of 12 months;
- deferred deletion with a window to cancel: the data would live longer, and the fresh sign-in protects against an accident anyway;
- deleting the account together with the last profile: the account holds the admission, the tariff and the counters, and a new profile would then require a new code;
- deleting the child consent whole: against the recommendation of П-14;
- a minimal record for an adult's consent too: there is nothing to check it against;
- a record with the account's ID: a link to the deleted family without need; a record without an ID of its own: it cannot be checked against the form either;
- keeping the users without an account 12 months, as an account: for a year the Google account of a person whom the platform never admitted would be kept; not deleting them at all: data without a period.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-02 amendments to R03, R04, R05 and R17. In the table of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.2, the periods after deletion and the date of the last request in the export, and in section 3.10 the steps, the races, the job and the honest periods. «В реестр spec»: В29 for T63 of epic E3, В30 for architecture 4.5 spec, privacy 1 spec and `app`, В31 for П-14 and В32 for П-9 in table 3 of RUN.md. There is no deletion or export in the code — Д15 in table 4. — RUN.md, T08; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 2.8, 3.1, 3.2, 3.6, 3.7, 3.8, 3.9 and 3.10.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T30; R40): the period of the minimal record of a child consent is decided: 5 years after the deletion of the account or the profile, and then the record erases itself; an adult's consent and a "waiting" consent are deleted whole (R117 spec; П-14 closed; Д148 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). The content of the record — the record's ID, the date, the method of the check and the date of deletion, without the account's and the profile's ID, the pseudonym, the age category and the country — is the one that `child_consent_records` already holds. It is erased by the daily job — the 2026-11-09 amendment to R38. The operator holds the scan of the signed form outside the database of `api` for the same period (R116 spec). "Until the lawyer's answer" is no more: the platform has no lawyer, and the author decides legal questions by Claude's analysis (R112 spec).

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T43; R52): a learner's own consent is withdrawn by the learner themselves: `WithdrawOwnConsent` deletes the profile, as the deletion of a profile does, with the minimal record of consent (R132 spec). The deletion of a profile and of the account leaves the problem reports until their period, without the account's, the profile's and the attempt's ID (R123 spec), and the daily job erases the scan of the form of a waiting consent on the day of deletion, of a confirmed one after 5 years. The export is in the code, `api.zip` by the part `export/` of `spec`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T42; Д15): the deletion of the user at the sign-in service does not remove the link "Sign in with Google" in the owner's Google account. This was checked on the dev project: after `DeleteAccount` there is no such user, and `novatrail-api-dev.firebaseapp.com` stayed among the author's connected apps. Revoking the Google token can remove it: "Revocation removes all OAuth 2.0 scopes previously granted to a project" ([Google](https://developers.google.com/identity/protocols/oauth2/javascript-implicit-flow), read 2026-11-12). This token is got by `app` at a sign-in by the window, and `api` does not hold it. The author's decision of 2026-11-12: `app` revokes the Google token at the deletion of the account, after a fresh sign-in — В52 in table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md), «в реестр spec». Until `app` does so, the table "What stays where" of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.10, says that the link stays and the owner removes it.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14* (T75; R70): the deletion of a profile and of the account takes the profile's stored works in its transaction, by `ON DELETE CASCADE` of the table `works`; switching their storage off erases them in its own. They leave nothing in "What stays where" but Neon's history, within its window.

## Result of the detailing

**R22. The detailing of E1 is finished: the thirteen items "To decide when detailed" of the card are decided — R03–R15 and R17–R21 —, and [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3, describes the service: the decisions on the items are sections 3.1–3.10, the service as a whole is section 3.11. What of the decisions changes the card and other places of `spec` the rows В33 and В35 of table 3 of RUN.md carry. Two epics of code are proposed next: E2 — the skeleton, the database, sign-in and deletion —, which needs only the author's dev projects, and E3 — the first app — by row 6.5 of the cross-repository plan of `spec` (R76 spec). Starting E2 before row 6.5 starts the code of `api` is the recommendation of row В34.** Task T09 of epic E1, 2026-11-02.
- **Section 2** — the result at its start: the items are decided, the subsections stand under their former numbers, and what is deliberately deferred is named there with when to come back. "What is known" remained only in subsection 2.8: tasks T01–T07, T12 and T13 removed their own, carrying the facts over into section 3. The result removed it too — the facts were already in "What the decision rests on" of section 3.10, and what was not there the result added: the fresh sign-in and the check with revocation in canvas-api, and that by П-14 a lawyer is asked together with П-1.
- **Section 3.11** — a summary, not a new design: the parts of the service, where each is decided, what it needs from outside and whether it is in the first app; what the sections left to the epic of code; the order on which E2 and E3 stand.
- **Section 3 is reconciled with the amendments.** An independent reconciliation found places where the text lagged behind the amendments to the decisions. The result brought them in line with the decisions, without changing the decisions:
  - the quota at the start of an attempt and of a segment and for "explain differently" without a reserve is a refusal, and for the submission and the words an outcome (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R11): step 7 of section 3.1 and "Exhausted" of section 3.6;
  - "no other words" is an outcome, not a refusal (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R10): section 3.5;
  - the port of the sign-in service also gives the class of refusal and iterates the users for the job (R20, R21): section 3.1;
  - there is no evidence either for an attempt closed "left", "abandoned" or "not suitable" without wrong answers (the 2026-11-02 amendments to R06 and R10), or for an attempt with a rubric closed "unchecked" on a withdrawn version or out of the window (R14): sections 3.2, 3.3 and 3.5;
  - the target of the idempotency key for "something else" is the profile (R17): section 3.2;
  - the right of step 4 is `can_view` too (R04, R17), "done" ends a segment too (R14), and after showing the solution in a lesson the answer carries the place of the next segment, not the engine choice (R13, R17): sections 3.1, 3.5 and 3.7;
  - progress carries the repeating traps once `spec` records В27 (R17): section 3.9;
  - all the jobs and commands of the operator are in section 1 and in the operator's row of section 3.11, and everything the daily job does is in section 3.10; the users of the sign-in service without an account are "30 days without a sign-in and a token refresh" everywhere (R21);
  - the sign-in service processes the user agent, and keeps IP addresses in its logs: so its privacy page says (section 4), not "in the logs — the user agent" (section 3.1);
  - the record of the sources of canvas-api names the sections where its quotes are now — 3.1, 3.2, 3.6, 3.9 and 3.10.

  One finding of the reconciliation was not confirmed. It asked for a refusal on the deletion of the last profile, but "the last profile does not delete the account" means that the account stays without profiles too, not that the last profile cannot be deleted (R21, "Rejected").
- **Reconciliation with the card** (architecture 3.5 spec) and with the other places of `spec` where it speaks about `api`. The tasks wrote «что выбрано, туда внесёт T09». The card does not mark as decided sign-in and permissions, the module version, the lesson and deletion, and the database is not named. The end of a lesson "only early or "unchecked"" and the engine choice after each attempt, in a lesson too, diverge from R13. The list of the operator's jobs is incomplete. The rows of architecture 3.10 spec and repositories 4 spec pin in the configuration of `api` the package digests, which R07 rejected. All this В33 carries. The items about the rubric В17 carries, the protocol and the channel В26, the rest about the lesson В15 and В16.
- **Reconciliation with privacy spec**, the whole document. The decisions of `api` break no hard rule: there are neither identifiers nor content in the logs, neither an ID nor an adult's data go to AI, the picture, the sound and an own key live in the request's memory, and every place of storage has a named period. The divergences are carried already by the rows В17, В21, В30, В31 and В32, and the new ones by В35. These are the register of `api` in privacy 4 spec, the country in the account, the trap from what is stored for "explain differently", and the periods of an open attempt and of the records of keys in П-9. The clock offset with an own key diverges from П-23, and this is a question to me — В36.
- **The rows «в реестр spec».** `spec` recorded В1–В14, and `api` closed them by its move to v0.6.6 (R16). В15–В23, В25–В27 and В29–В32 stand in table 3. `spec` has not recorded them yet: in table 5 of RUN.md of `spec` on `main` 5d8765e — this is the tag v0.6.6 — there are only В1–В14 from `api`, read 2026-11-02. The new ones are В33, В34 and В35. В24, В28 and В36 are questions to me.
- **The next epics** — table 2 of RUN.md:
  - E2 — the skeleton, the database, sign-in and deletion. This is a Go service by the skeleton of canvas-api (repositories 7 spec) and the database of `api` with the OpenFGA store, migrations and tests on `embedded-postgres`. With them, sign-in and permissions, the fresh sign-in, the deletion of what is already there and the operator's commands over it. It needs no library and no release of `modules`, only the Firebase and Neon dev projects (Д5, Д6): the attempts, the lessons, the learner model and the job after a deployment, with their periods and deletion, come with E3. The Connect handlers come with the contract of epic E3 of `spec`: the account and the profiles in E2, if T63 of `spec` comes out in a release before its end;
  - E3 — the first app. These are the module packages, the attempt and the check of handwriting, the hint ladder, the lesson, the evidence and the choice, and the protocol. E3 waits for rows 1.6–1.9, 3.3, 3.6, 4.6 and 5.4 of the cross-repository plan.

  The rest — the quota in code, Apple, the jobs in a deployment, the path of a task with a rubric, an own key, the storage of essays and the tariffs — is outside the first app, and table 2 has no epics for them yet (section 3.11).

— *Why:* so T09 orders: section 2 is empty or names what is deliberately deferred, section 3 describes the service, and what changes the card goes «в реестр spec». Section 3 was written by ten tasks, each about its own, and none described the service as a whole — hence section 3.11. The tasks put the amendments into their own sections, and into the sections of earlier tasks not always, and the builder would stumble over different rules in two places. Two epics instead of one, because almost half of the service waits for nothing of what row 6.5 waits for, and as of 2026-11-02 its rows 3.3, 3.6, 4.6 and 5.4 are not started. The skeleton, the database, sign-in and permissions are needed by the first app in any case, and deletion goes with them (Д15).

— *Rejected:*
- **renumbering section 2 or removing the subsections** — the references of the log and of the tasks to 2.x would point to the wrong place;
- **leaving "What is known" in 2.8 or carrying it over into 3.10 word for word** — the first would diverge from how the other nine subsections were done, and the second would repeat "What the decision rests on" almost word for word;
- **one epic of code after all the rows that 6.5 waits for**, as the cross-repository plan stands now. The skeleton, the database, sign-in and deletion would wait for libraries they do not need;
- **E2 with handlers by a draft of the contract** — the service stubs are born in `spec` (R83 spec), and `api` would write its own anew;
- **proposing now the epics of the first deployment too** — what goes first will be seen by the end of E3, and Д3, Д9–Д12 name when to come back anyway;
- **fixing the clock offset with an own key at once** — this changes R11 and R17, and the author changes decisions (В36).

— *Consequence:* [architecture.md](architecture.md) — the header, sections 1, 2, 2.8, 3, 3.1–3.3, 3.5–3.7, 3.9, 3.10 and the new 3.11, the record of the sources of canvas-api in section 4. RUN.md — T09, table 1, table 2 (E2, E3), table 3 (В33–В36 and an addition to В15), table 4 («Когда вернуться» for Д5, Д6, Д8, Д9, Д11, Д14 and Д15). README — that the detailing is finished.

## The `spec` tag: v0.9.4

**R23. `api` stands on `spec` v0.9.4. The tag records the answers of `spec` to the rows «в реестр spec» В15–В23, В25–В27 and В29–В35 of table 3 of RUN.md, all by the recommendations of `api`, and the author's answer to the question В36, and the contract `app` ↔ `api` — the package `novatrail.api.v1` (R97–R100 spec), which took almost all of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9, and in a few places decided otherwise. With it came the module format 0.3, the admission table for AI and the rules of Starlark and of module versions. The skeleton, the database, sign-in and deletion are a row of their own, 6.10, of the cross-repository plan: E2 waits for nothing but the author's dev projects.** Task T15 of epic E4, 2026-11-07; the move in one task is the author's decision of the same day.
- **What the tag brings to `api`** (`git -C spec diff v0.6.6 v0.9.4`, read 2026-11-07; table 5 and Д82, Д83 and Д89 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec` v0.9.4; rows 6.5 and 6.10 of `COORDINATION.md` of `spec`):
