## Deployment facts and the admission rung

**R25. The deployment facts for the AI admission function and the lowest rung of the models' admission rule are held by `infra`, beside the rollout list, and the deployment passes them to `api`; they enter neither the image nor the git of `api`. For the first app, a local file of the author outside the repository, with the rung "author".** The author's answer of 2026-11-08 to В24, by its recommendation — option (a). Task T16 of epic E5.
- **What was decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1):
  - the facts file is the one that the [admission table](../spec/admission/README.md), section 5, names: a fact at a provider for the categories the operator named. Its type and how to read it are `tutor`'s (R57 tutor). It lies in the repository `infra`, beside the rollout list (R106 spec), and a pull request of `infra` changes it;
  - the lowest rung that `api` passes to `Table.Select`, "author" or "full" (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R76 spec; architecture 3.6 spec), is a value of the same place. "Author" only in the first app: any deployment, even where only the author learns, passes "full";
  - the deployment of `infra` passes both values to the Cloud Run revision, and `api` reads them at start. How exactly — by an environment variable or a file — T02 of `infra` and the epic of code of `api` decide. There are no secrets in them;
  - no facts file: no fact holds, and platform AI is closed, while an adult's own key is open; a fact unknown to the vocabulary is an error at start ([admission table](../spec/admission/README.md), section 5). No value of the rung: "full": so it is closed by default, as for the admission function (architecture 3.9 tutor). What is missing `api` writes at start to the log for the operator, without identifiers;
  - self-hosting holds its own facts and its own rung in its own deployment;
  - the first app is a local file of the author outside the repository, not in the folder `/workspaces/api`, like the key of the Firebase service account: in it the facts, if the table opens with them the cell of platform AI for `adult`, and the rung "author". The first app has no deployment and no `infra` (R76 spec; row 6.9 of `COORDINATION.md` of `spec`).

— *Why:*
- the facts and the rung are a property of the deployment, not of the build: the operator states them about their contracts and about who learns, and the image of `api` is one for all — in it only the binary and the module packages (R105, R106 spec; RULES.md, Production images);
- self-hosting writes its own facts and does not edit `api` for that: `tutor` designed the file just so — in git, by pull request, not built into the image (architecture 3.9 tutor);
- `modules.json` is another matter: the packages are built into the image at the build, and this is a property of the release of `api` (R07);
- removing a fact means closing at once the cells that it opened ([admission table](../spec/admission/README.md), section 5): the deployment of `infra` does this without a release of `api`.

— *Rejected:*
- in `api`, beside `modules.json`: the platform's facts would become the facts of everyone who builds `api`, and a change of a fact would wait for a release of `api`;
- deciding with the first deployment: the admission task in E3 reads the values at start, and it needs to know from where.

— *Consequence:* the card of `infra`, architecture 3.11 spec, names neither the facts file nor the rung — В39 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec». There is no code — Д18 in table 4 of RUN.md. — RUN.md, T16; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T30; R40): a deployment states the fact `minor_safeguards` only when the provider's list of measures is fulfilled and the author decided by analysis that they are enough ([admission table](../spec/admission/README.md), version 0.2; R112, R123 spec; Д98 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). The facts file is still held by `infra`; version 0.2 opened and closed no cell, and the vocabulary of facts did not change. The variables are `API_ADMISSION_FACTS_FILE` and `API_ADMISSION_RUNG`, and the deployment calls the configuration check before the migrations (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R106 spec; Д129): T35 writes them.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T35; R45): the values are read by the variables `API_ADMISSION_FACTS_FILE` and `API_ADMISSION_RUNG`, at the start of `serve` and by the command `api check`. The path to the file is absolute and outside the git working tree, as for a secret file. The rung is one of the codes `tutor`, `author` or `full`; without a value, `full`. The rule ""author" only in the first app" is held by the code: `author` with `K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is a configuration refusal, so a deployment that was passed "author" does not start and does not pass the check before the migrations. What is missing the row `startup` says by the fields `facts_file` and `rung_set`, without providers, facts and paths.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T36; the author's decision): in the first app the author states `minimal_retention` for `adult` too, although their organization in the Claude Console has retention turned on — 30 days, without zero data retention (read 2026-11-10). Why: only the author, an adult, learns, and Anthropic keeps only their own work; the author will not request zero data retention. The exception is only for the first app: a deployment states a fact only when it holds. For `spec` — В46.

## Skeleton

**R26. The skeleton of `api` is a Go service after the model of canvas-api: one binary with the commands `serve` and `version`, configuration from the environment, logs by `log/slog` in JSON under a table of events, errors and panics in the log by kind, and the version is set by the linker. The differences from the model are recorded here, as R46 canvas-api recorded its own, and the next reconciliation with the model does not roll them back.** The author's decisions of 2026-11-08 in the planning of T17: the table of events, the prefix `API_`, the modules of `spec` in `go.mod` through a test. Task T17 of epic E2.
- **The model** is canvas-api at the commit `0901af0` of its `main`, read 2026-11-08 and cloned into a temporary folder (RULES.md, Citations). Its skeleton is `internal/` from standalone (R11 canvas-api; repositories 7 spec).
- **What was taken:**
  - the configuration, `internal/config`: only the environment, only the declared names, exactly by case, and an empty value is not set. A refusal wraps `ErrInvalid` and names the variable. The port in canonical digits from 1 to 65535. The shutdown time is split: ¾ for draining the requests, ¼ for closing, and in a deployment, where `K_SERVICE` is set, it is shorter than 10 s — the pause of Cloud Run before stopping an instance;
  - start and shutdown, `cmd/api` and `internal/server`: `signal.NotifyContext` on SIGINT and SIGTERM, and a second signal ends the process at once; the port is taken before the row `startup`; an error is written once (`errLogged`); a shutdown during the start is not a failure; a drain that did not fit is a warning `stopped` with `cut_short`;
  - a panic and an error in the log by kind, not by text: a runtime failure in the runtime's words, the rest by type, as `PanicValue` and `errorsField` of canvas-api;
  - the version is `Version`, `Commit` and `Date` of the package `internal/version`, which `-ldflags -X` sets, and tests that the linker sets them and that the build names this path;
  - the health check `GET /health`, without `z` at the end (R30 canvas-api).
- **How it differs from the model:**
  - the logs are `log/slog` of the standard library, not zap; the keys of Cloud Logging are the same — `severity`, `message`, `time`;
  - the ban on direct identifiers, IP addresses and content (R09 spec; privacy 4 spec) is held by the table of events, `internal/logging/events.go`. The message of a row is the name of the event, the event names its fields, and a field has a rule: a number, a flag, a duration, a word of a closed set, a string in the form of the build — a version, a commit, a date —, or the kind of an error and of a panic, which only the package itself makes. There is no free text. The guard handler writes only this: a field that the event does not name, or a value not by the rule, is dropped, and the row says how many were dropped — `dropped_fields`; an unknown event loses its message and is written as `unregistered_event`; groups are dropped. A new event or field comes with the code that writes it, as a row of the table, and its test holds each row of the rules. The libraries in the process get the same `*slog.Logger`, and their rows pass the same filter. canvas-api has no such design — the linter's bans on `zap.Any` and `zap.Reflect` and the closed sets of the method and the route —, and the table is only declared in its CLAUDE.md, for E2;
  - one binary with commands — `serve` and `version`, and later `migrate` and the operator's commands, because there is no shell in the image (R105 spec). canvas-api has one command, and `cmd/migrate` and the others are separate binaries in the plan;
  - the standard library instead of viper, gin and errgroup: the configuration reads only the environment, and the Connect handler is an `http.Handler` for `http.ServeMux` (T23). The code has no third-party modules but the modules of `spec`;
  - the prefix of the variables is `API_`, and `PORT` and `K_SERVICE` are names of Cloud Run. There are no variables for the environment's name or the log format, like `CANVAS_ENV` and `CANVAS_LOG_FORMAT`: the log is always in JSON, and a deployment is recognized by `K_SERVICE`;
  - a secret is the type `Secret`, which prints as `[redacted]` both in `fmt` with any verb and in the log. The value is from the variable `X` or from a file by an absolute path in `X_FILE`. Both at once is a refusal, and a file in the git working tree too: a key in it is one commit away from the repository (RULES.md, Hard rules). canvas-api has no secrets. The first secret variables will come with T20 and T22;
  - the error format `{code, message}` of the package `apierror` is not taken: the refusals of `api` are Connect errors with `Refusal` (R97 spec), T23 builds them. An unknown path is 404 without a body, and so is a path that the mux would redirect to its clean form: the redirect would repeat the path. A panic is 500 without a body;
  - there is no dependency container, `internal/app/container.go`: there is nothing to close yet, it will come with the database;
  - the recipes of the checks — `ci-lint` (gofmt and `go vet`), `ci-test` (`-race` and the profile `coverage/go.out`), `ci-vuln` (govulncheck v1.9.0) and `ci-licenses` (go-licenses v2.0.1) — by the agreement of the 2026-11-01 amendment to R30 spec, without golangci-lint. In canvas-api `ci-lint` is golangci-lint, and the profile is `coverage.out`. `ci-licenses` takes the list of licenses from `ALLOWED_LICENSES`, which the shared `go.yml` gives, and without it from `just -f spec/justfile licenses-allowed`;
  - `build` builds with `CGO_ENABLED=0` and `-trimpath` (RULES.md, Production images). In canvas-api `CGO_ENABLED=0` is set only by the `Dockerfile` and the release recipe;
  - Go is 1.27.3 from the devcontainer: in `go.mod`, `go 1.27.3`, without a line `toolchain`.
- **The modules of `spec`.** `github.com/altnovatrail/spec` and `github.com/altnovatrail/spec/contracts` are v0.9.11, from the Go proxy, without `replace` (R64, R83 spec). The skeleton does not call them yet, and `go mod tidy` would remove what is unused, so they are held by the test `internal/version`: it imports both and checks by `debug.ReadBuildInfo` that they have one tag number. They do not enter the production binary: `spec.Format` embeds about 20 MB of format files, and it is needed by the tests of E3 ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3). The test does not read the submodule `spec/`: the shared `go.yml` does not take it.

— *Why:*
- the model is what repositories 1 and 7 spec order to carry over, and differences without a record the next reconciliation would roll back — for this R46 canvas-api recorded its own;
- the table holds the rule of the logs not by the memory of the author of each row but by one place, which has a test: without the guard the log test fails on a field with an email address and a UID;
- the guard stands under `*slog.Logger`, not over it, so the rows of the libraries pass the filter too.

— *Rejected:*
- a closed list of fields without events: any event would carry any allowed field;
- a function for each event instead of `*slog.Logger`: stricter, but the libraries that expect `*slog.Logger` would bypass the filter, and each event would become a new method;
- the modules of `spec` and of the contracts with the first code that calls them, T23 and E3: `go.mod` would diverge from the task, and nobody would check the one tag number of both;
- the contracts in the binary, so that `version` prints their version: for the sake of a line, code that is not needed before T23;
- viper, gin and cobra: dependencies for the sake of what the standard library gives;
- golangci-lint: the license is GPL-3.0, and it would enter only as a named exception (R12 spec), and `spec` rejected it for itself. `api` can come back to it by a decision of its own, if `go vet` is not enough;
- the prefix `NOVATRAIL_API_`: unambiguous, but long, and the configuration reads only the declared names anyway.

— *Consequence:* the Go CI, Dependabot `gomod` and the Go badge are T18; the `Dockerfile` and the check of its `-X` path are T19. — RUN.md, T17; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T21): `PORT=0` outside a deployment asks the kernel for a free port, and the row `startup` names it; in a deployment, where the platform names the port, `0` is still a refusal. So the tests of `serve` do not share a port with the tests beside them and with the Postgres of `embedded-postgres`, which takes a free one too: a free port found in advance another could take first.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T31; R41): the root module of `spec` enters the production binary too — by the package `types/formatv0`, through `module-kit` —, and `spec.Format` with the format files does not enter it: the binary does not import the root package `spec` (`go list -deps ./cmd/api`). The binary grew by 2.1 MB, from 53.9 to 56.0 MB (`-s -w`, linux/amd64). The module `spec` is now held in `go.mod` by code as well, and the test `internal/version` still checks that both modules have one tag.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T35; R45): the root package `spec` enters the production binary too, and with it `spec.Format`, — through `tutor/admission`, whose admission table is `spec.Admission`, and `spec` embeds `format/`, `admission/` and `deployment/` by one `embed.FS`. The binary grew by 1.4 MB, from 56.7 to 58.1 MB (`-s -w`, `CGO_ENABLED=0`, linux/amd64): the "about 20 MB" of this entry is the size of the folder on disk, while the bytes of `format/` themselves are 5.9 MB, and the linker apparently keeps identical vector files once. The module `tutor` goes into `THIRD_PARTY` and into the `ignore` of Dependabot, like `module-kit`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T43; R52): in `go.mod`, `go 1.27.4`, following the Go of the devcontainer (the 2026-11-11 amendment to R30).

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T59; sources 10.6): a path that the mux would redirect is not found, by the mux's own answer: `Routes` asks `mux.Handler`, and a handler of the type of `http.RedirectHandler` is answered 404 without a body. Besides the clean form, this is the root of a subtree without its slash — `/novatrail.api.v1.AccountService`, since Connect mounts a subtree for each service, and `/packages/<name>/device`, of the pattern `{path...}` —, which until T59 was answered 307, with the path and the query in `Location`. `cleanPath`, which repeated the cleaning of the mux, is removed. The seeds of the fuzz test `FuzzRoutes` found it (the 2026-11-13 amendment to R27). No handler of `api` answers by a redirect. One 404 has a body, as before T59: a request for no method of a service that is not a POST of Connect's protocol gets Connect's own `http.NotFound`, the fixed text "404 page not found", which repeats nothing of the request (sources 10.6).

## Go CI and coverage

**R27. Go code is checked by the shared `go.yml` of `.github` v0.1.21 — "Go / lint", "Go / test", "Go / vuln" and "Go / licenses" —, its CodeQL reads Go too, and coverage goes to Codecov by OIDC. The modules of `spec` and of the contracts are held at the tag of the submodule by `ci-lint`: it checks the versions that the build chooses against the commit of the submodule. The merge is held by these checks and `codecov/patch`, which goes into the ruleset when it first arrives.** The author's decisions of 2026-11-08 in the planning of T18: the check as in module-kit, OpenSSF Best Practices later, the required checks are set by the executor on the author's confirmation. Task T18 of epic E2.
- **The calls of `.github`** (architecture 3.3 .github, by the release v0.1.21 — commit `706f4067a66c91b0442033f82a7924eeb8b60497`, out 2026-11-08; read 2026-11-08):
  - all the calls — secrets, DCO, documents, CodeQL, Scorecard and the new job "Go" — at v0.1.21, by SHA with the version in a comment (R04 .github). The names of the checks have not changed since v0.1.0, and there are no new required inputs. The documents check of v0.1.21 expects `spec` no lower than v0.2.17 (R08 .github), and `api` has v0.9.11;
  - the job "Go" gives `contents: read` and `id-token: write`: the nested coverage job asks for an OIDC token, and a called workflow only lowers permissions (the 2026-11-04 amendment to R30 spec). There is no input `same-bits`: it is for `learner-model` and `engine` (R107 spec);
  - CodeQL — the input `languages: go`. It builds Go by autobuild with the Go of the stage `toolchain` of the devcontainer (R09 .github), and there is no Makefile or `build.sh`, which autobuild would run first, in the root of `api`;
  - Dependabot — the ecosystem `gomod`, with a pause of three days (R10 .github), and the modules `spec` and `spec/contracts` in `ignore` by name (R64 spec and its second 2026-11-02 amendment).
- **The modules of `spec` at the tag of the submodule** — after the model of `ci-lint` of module-kit (R20 module-kit). The commit of the submodule is taken from the git index, so the check goes in CI too, where there is no submodule. The versions of both modules are the ones that the build chooses, `go list -m`, and their commit is `Origin.Hash`, which the Go proxy gives. All three commits must match, and the versions be one. A library that requires a `spec` newer than the submodule — `tutor`, `module-kit` — will raise it by minimal version selection, and "Go / lint" will turn red. It is fixed not by an edit of `go.mod` but by a move to the tag in a separate task. Such a library, once it enters `go.mod`, goes into the `ignore` of Dependabot too, as R68 tutor: its new patch may require a `spec` newer than the submodule ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.11). The test `internal/version` stays: it holds both modules in `go.mod` (R26).
- **Coverage.** `.github/codecov.yml` is the text of the template of architecture 3.3 .github: changed lines no less than 90%, `codecov/project` only reports (R48 spec). There is no `after_n_builds`: in `spec` it is 2, because there are two uploads, and `api` has one upload. The Codecov app is installed at the organization for all repositories, and there is no secret (R16 .github). The Codecov API on 2026-11-08 knew `api` with `active: false`, and `learner-model` and `engine` Codecov turned on by their first upload (R13 learner-model; R12 engine) — the author's step "connect Codecov" is not needed.
- **The order of the ruleset of `main`.** "Go / lint", "Go / test", "Go / vuln", "Go / licenses" and "CodeQL / analyze (go)", with `integration_id` 15368, go in after the first green run on the task's pull request (the 2026-11-01 amendment to R30 spec). `codecov/patch`, with `integration_id` 254, only when it first arrives: in `learner-model` an early requirement held the merge without end (R13 learner-model). "Go / coverage" does not go into the ruleset.
- **SonarCloud** reads the Go tests — `*_test.go` and the programs under `testdata/` — as tests, not as code: `sonar.tests` and `sonar.test.inclusions`, as in `spec`, and the submodule `spec/` is excluded both from the code and from the tests. The template of `.github` gives only `sonar.exclusions=spec/**`.
- **README** — the Go version badge, and the coverage badge after the first upload (repositories 2.3 spec). OpenSSF Best Practices later, Д19 in table 4 of RUN.md.

— *Why:*
- the check in `ci-lint` goes in CI on every pull request, without the submodule, and catches both a forgotten side of a move to a tag and a library that raised `spec`;
- `codecov/patch` in the ruleset before it arrived holds any merge, as it was in `learner-model`;
- part of the Best Practices criteria are about releases and their signature, and T19 will give the release: answers written earlier would have to be rewritten.

— *Rejected:*
- the check by a Go test, as `TestSpecVersion` of tutor: there is no submodule in CI, and the test would check it only on the author's machine (R20 module-kit);
- relying on the review of a move to a tag: a move that forgot one of the sides would pass green;
- `same-bits`: `api` computes nothing where identical bits on all builds matter (R107 spec);
- `after_n_builds: 2`, as in `spec`: with one upload Codecov would wait for a second without end;
- `codecov/patch` in the ruleset at once: see "Why";
- Best Practices now: see "Why".

— *Consequence:* the group `shared-workflows` in Dependabot is an edit of the session of `spec` (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R04 .github). The open Dependabot pull requests on v0.1.10 Dependabot will close itself when the references become newer. — RUN.md, T18; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T31; R41): `github.com/altnovatrail/module-kit` entered `go.mod` — v0.3.0, on `spec` v0.12.0 — and went into the `ignore` of Dependabot `gomod` beside the modules of `spec`: its new release may require a new tag of `spec`, and `api` takes it by a task of its own.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T58; Д19): the item of README above, "OpenSSF Best Practices later", is half done. The answers to the 67 criteria of the level passing lie in `.bestpractices.json` in the root. They were started from the files of `spec`, `module-kit` and `app`, which the site accepted, and checked against the facts of `api` (repositories 2.3 spec; the 2026-10-26 amendment to R48 spec). The site offers them in the project's questionnaire when the author creates it, and the author saves them. The badge goes into README with the level passing — T60. The differences from `app`:
- `crypto_password_storage` and `crypto_random` — Met: the PIN is stored by R35, and the salt, the invite codes (R34) and the consent request codes are given by `crypto/rand`;
- `dynamic_analysis` — Unmet. The tool of this criterion must vary the input or be a test suite with branch coverage no lower than 80%: "the dynamic analysis tool needs to vary the inputs in some way to look for various kinds of problems or be an automated test suite with at least 80% branch coverage" ([bestpractices.dev criteria](https://github.com/coreinfrastructure/best-practices-badge/blob/main/config/locales/en.yml), read 2026-11-12). `api` has no fuzz tests, and Go measures statement coverage, not branch coverage. The criterion is SUGGESTED, and it does not hold the level passing. The answer will become Met with the fuzz tests of T59, and they will also close the Scorecard finding `Fuzzing`. R33 spec explains this finding only by code that reads no outside input, and `api` reads such input.

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T59; Д19): the readers of foreign input have fuzz tests of Go, and `dynamic_analysis` of `.bestpractices.json` is Met. Each test checks that its reader does not panic, and what holds of what it accepts:
- `FuzzRead` of `internal/invite` — the code a person types (R34): written in the other case, with O for 0 and I or L for 1, or with dashes and white space between its symbols, it reads to the same hash, and it has 16 symbols; dashes and white space never change whether a text is read;
- `FuzzFromName` of `internal/packages` — the package's name in the path of a file of a device part (R42): a digest whose `Name` is that name;
- `FuzzUnpack` of `internal/packages` — the archive of a package from a release (R41), through `packages.Unpack` of `export_test.go`, since the seeds come from `packagestest`, which imports the package: regular files alone, of the package's parts, by clean paths, of no more bytes than the tar holds, and an archive with a link refused. The fuzzer varies the tar, and the test gzips it: the gzip is the standard library's, the rules are `api`'s;
- three more readers that the task found: `FuzzCursorOf` of `internal/learning` — the page token of `GetProgress`, which `app` gives back (R49): a cursor whose token reads back to it, between 1970 and the year 9999; `FuzzRoutes` of `internal/server` — the path and the query of any request, on the mount of `serve`, `rpc.Mount` of every service and the pattern of the files (R26): no answer is a redirect, and a 404 has no body but the fixed text of Connect; `FuzzParseConfig` of `internal/packages` — `modules.json`, which a pull request may change and `cmd/modules-check` reads (R41): a release and a digest of their form, which read back from themselves.

  No fuzz test is given to the rest. The body of a request is parsed by Connect and protobuf, and its fields are then checked in one pass, without decoding: `pin.Valid`, `countries.Valid`, `idem.ValidKey`, `store.CanonicalID` — `uuid.Parse` and its own writing back —; the path of a file of a device part is a key of a map. The operator's files and environment — a secret of `_FILE`, read whole and stripped of one newline, the service account's key, read by `encoding/json` and the SDK, the deployment's facts, read by `table.ReadFacts` of `tutor` (R25), the durations and numbers of `config` by `strconv` and `time` — come from the operator, not a stranger, and their readers are the standard library's and the libraries'. `proto.Unmarshal` of records reads what `api` wrote.
- **The corpus and the run.** The seeds are `f.Add` and `testdata/fuzz/<test>/` of the package, and `go test` runs them without fuzzing, so `ci-test` runs them on every pull request (sources 13.1). The recipe `just fuzz [fuzztime] [test]`, 1m by default, fuzzes each test in turn, or the one named: Go fuzzes one test of a package at a time. It minimizes a new input for 2 s, not 60: the minimizing stops the fuzzing (measurements 5). An input that fails is written to `testdata/fuzz/`, stays in the corpus and fails `ci-test` until the code is fixed. Fuzzing runs in a task that changes a reader (`.claude/rules/go.md`), not in CI: it is not deterministic, and a pull request would fail by a finding it did not make.
- **What was found.** The seeds of `FuzzRoutes` found that `/novatrail.api.v1.AccountService`, without the slash of the subtree that Connect mounts for each service, and `/packages/<name>/device`, of the pattern `{path...}`, were answered 307, with the path and the query in `Location` (sources 10.6); the 2026-11-13 amendment to R26 fixed it. A probe of the database for `FuzzCursorOf` found that a page token of a time before 4713 BC was answered `unavailable`, by the database's refusal; the 2026-11-13 amendment to R49 fixed it. Fuzzing itself, `just fuzz 5m` on 2026-11-13, failed no test (measurements 5); but `FuzzUnpack` stood still, and the cause was an entry of PAX sparse: 3,584 bytes of tar that `unpack` unfolded to 500 MB of zeros, under the limit of R41 (sources 4.5). The 2026-11-13 amendment to R41 refuses it, `FuzzUnpack` checks that what is unpacked is no more bytes than the tar holds, and the input stays in `testdata/fuzz/FuzzUnpack/`.
- **Scorecard.** Its check `Fuzzing` looks for fuzz functions of Go in the repository (sources 13.1); the finding is expected to close after the merge, with the score 10.

The author created the project by the repository's address, and the site read `.bestpractices.json` from `main` itself, as for `spec` and `app` (the 2026-10-27 amendment to R48 spec; sources 13.2):
- all 67 statuses of the file are saved, and 63 justifications. For `floss_license`, `floss_license_osi`, `repo_track` and `repo_distributed` the site wrote its own, as for `spec` and `app`. They say what the file says — Apache-2.0 is approved by the OSI, the repository is git on GitHub, and git tracks who changed what and when —, so no link with `overrides` was built: the file's wording adds a link to `LICENSE` and the sign-off under the DCO, which other answers of the file name too;

The test is in `internal/export`, since the unpacking it holds is that package's own, and `.claude/rules/go.md` names the package among those of fuzz tests. `just fuzz 5m FuzzStoredWork` — 3.89 million runs in 5 minutes, no failure.

## Release

**R28. A release of `api` comes out by itself, by the shared `release.yml` of `.github` v0.1.21, after green CI on `main`: the static binaries `api_<version>_linux_amd64` and `api_<version>_linux_arm64`, the `Dockerfile` by which the deployment builds the image, the licenses the binaries come with, and `SHA256SUMS`, each file with its signature. The binary takes its commit and that commit's time from a file that `git archive` writes, so the build is reproducible. CI's job "Image" checks the `Dockerfile`: it builds the image the same way as the deployment does, and from the source code. The first release is v0.1.0, with the merge of T19.** The author's decisions of 2026-11-08 in the planning of T19: the first release now, the commit and the time through `export-subst`, a CI job of its own checks the image, and the executor sets GitHub's settings once the author confirms. Task T19 of epic E2.
- **How a release comes out** (R18 .github; architecture 3.3 .github, by release v0.1.21 — read 2026-11-08):
  - `.github/workflows/release.yml` is the template of architecture 3.3 `.github`, at v0.1.21; the shared `release.yml` has not changed since v0.1.16. `RELEASE_LINE` is `0.1`, the number is the next patch of the line (R31 spec), and a release comes out only when `paths` change: the list of the kind `go` without `testdata/`, and with it `Dockerfile`, `.gitattributes` and `internal/version/archive.txt` — what whoever deploys receives;
  - the shared `release.yml` sets the tag itself: a tag set by hand, with no release behind it, stops every following run ("Nobody tags by hand"). The item of T19 «тег первого релиза ставлю я» did not match this;
  - the recipe `release-files <version>` writes the files into `dist/`, in the `toolchain` stage of the commit being released, on the tree of `git archive` without `.git`, submodules and the token, and the shared `release.yml` signs each with cosign without keys, `<file>.sigstore.json`, and verifies the signature before publication. `just -f spec/justfile release-verify api <tag> <file>` accepts the files (R77 spec);
  - the condition of the signature is immutable releases and the tag ruleset "v-tags", `update`, `deletion` and `non_fast_forward` with no bypass, before the first release (R77 spec). The executor set them once the author confirmed, 2026-11-08: immutable releases by `PUT immutable-releases`, and "v-tags" as ruleset 26814634 (RUN.md, T19).
- **What is in the release:**
  - binaries for `linux/amd64` and `linux/arm64`, as canvas-api has (R52 canvas-api), `CGO_ENABLED=0`, `-trimpath`, `-buildvcs=false` and `-s -w`, after module-kit (R07 module-kit). There are no other OSes: `api` is a service, and it runs in an image on Linux;
  - the `Dockerfile` — the same as in the repository;
  - `LICENSE`, `LICENSE-go` — the notice of Go's standard library — and the licenses of the modules the binary is built from, by the list `THIRD_PARTY` of the `justfile`, as module-kit has (R38 module-kit). The list is empty now: the modules of `spec` enter only the tests (R26), and `release-licenses`, which `ci-lint` calls too, fails on the first module that enters the binary without its license. This is the answer to the question «Открыто» of T17;
  - `SHA256SUMS` over all the files.
- **The commit and the time.** `.gitattributes` gives `export-subst` to the file `internal/version/archive.txt`, `$Format:%H %ct$`, and `git archive` writes the full commit and its time into it. `release-files` passes them to the linker, the time in UTC RFC 3339, and refuses without a file that `git archive` filled in. The binary's date is the commit's time, not the build's: two builds of one commit give the same bytes, and the job "Image" checks this.
- **The `Dockerfile`** — after canvas-api (commit 0901af0; R105 spec):
  - `build` is `golang:1.27.3-trixie` by digest, with the Go of `go.mod`; `runtime` is `gcr.io/distroless/static-debian13:nonroot` by digest, uid 65532, `ENTRYPOINT ["/api"]` and `CMD ["serve"]`, so the same image runs the operator's commands too. The digests were read from the registries 2026-11-08, and the digest of distroless is the same that R105 spec recorded;
  - `release` is the runtime around the release's binary from the build context `binary`, the file `api`: the deployment builds it, `docker build --target release --build-context binary=<folder>`. The last stage is the same image from the source code, for the checks;
  - the test of `internal/version` holds the tag of `golang` equal to the version of `go.mod`, both images by digest, and the `-X` path the same in the `Dockerfile`, `build` and `release-files`;
  - Dependabot `docker` raises the digests of the root `Dockerfile`, but not the version of `golang`: Go is raised by hand, together with the devcontainer and `go.mod`;
  - there are no module packages in the image yet — E3 adds their stage (Д8).
- **The job "Image"** on a runner that has Docker is `.github/image.sh`: `release-files` twice from `git archive`, a comparison of the bytes, then the image `--target release` around the `linux/amd64` binary and the image from the source code. Each has `version` with the version and the commit, user 65532, `/health` and exit 0 on SIGTERM. The check is required in the ruleset of `main`.

— *Why:*
- the first release now: the check of T19 waits for it, the Best Practices criteria (Д19) and the deployment of `infra` — it needs a release on which the list can be tried (T02 `infra`). From then on each change of the code gives a patch;
- an image built the same way as the deployment will build it catches an error of the `Dockerfile` before the deployment, not during it;
- the line `startup` and the command `version` name the commit in the binary, and the commit's date keeps the build reproducible.

— *Rejected:*
- the tag of the first release by hand: the shared `release.yml` does not accept such a tag;
- `publish: false` until the first deployment: the check `release-verify` would not have taken place, and `infra` would have had nothing to try;
- Commit and Date "unknown" in the release: the binary would not name its commit;
- only the build of `infra`: an error of the `Dockerfile` would be found at the deployment;
- hadolint and an image scanner: the image is not published, and the base is distroless, whose signature can be verified (R105 spec);
- publishing the image: R105 spec forbids it.

— *Consequence:* how the deployment builds the image — the stage `release`, the context `binary`, the binary `api_<tag>_linux_amd64` — is В40 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec». Д8 is without the `Dockerfile`. — RUN.md, T19; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 3.3 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T31; R41): the `Dockerfile` has two new stages, `release-packages` and `source-packages`. Each puts a binary into the runtime — from the context `binary` or from the stage `build` — and calls it with the command `packages` in exec form, into `/tmp/packages`; the stages `release` and the last one copy the folder to `/packages` with root as owner. The stage `release` still reads no main context, and the build needs the network. `modules.json` entered the release's `paths`: a pull request that changes only it puts out a release. The job "Image" checks that both images read the packages of the pinned release, and prints their size.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T41; R51): the release's README names the commands that the deployment of `infra` runs as jobs of the same image, by the roles of its record `api_jobs`: `api check`, `api migrate` and `api after`, and `api sweep` on a schedule. What each reads is the table of R51.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T43; R52): the stage `build` is `golang:1.27.4-trixie`, digest `sha256:b54b2639de8c1bfe538ec5ffc45f29b1932235ac5f6d63c059853646677112d9` read from Docker Hub 2026-11-11 (the 2026-11-11 amendment to R30).

## Database and migrations

**R29. `api` goes to Neon with the driver pgx v5.12.0: the requests of `serve` through Neon's pooler, and the migrations directly, goose v3.29.0, by the command `api migrate`, embedded in the binary and forward only, by the rule "add first, remove later", which a test holds. The connection strings are secrets outside the repository, both with `sslmode=verify-full`. The tests with the database are `embedded-postgres` v2.0.1, Postgres 17: one server per package and a database of its own for each test. A write under an account goes in a transaction that holds its row `FOR KEY SHARE` and checks the mark "being deleted".** The author's decisions of 2026-11-08 in the planning of T20: goose, the pooler for `serve` and a direct connection for `migrate`, a test of the migration rule and a database per test. Closes in Д6 the migration tool, the pool and the connection string. Task T20 of epic E2.
- **The driver** is pgx v5.12.0, MIT, and the pool is `pgxpool`. Not below v5.12.0: in v5.9.0–v5.11.0 `channel_binding=require` was bypassed without `sslmode=verify-full` ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4).
- **The pooler and the direct connection.**
  - Neon's pooler is PgBouncer in transaction mode: it supports the protocol's prepared statements, `max_prepared_statements=1000`, and those are what pgx uses in its default mode. It does not support `SET`, `LISTEN`, SQL `PREPARE` and session locks. A request of `api` is in one transaction, and row locks — `FOR KEY SHARE`, `FOR UPDATE` — live in it, so `serve` goes through the pooler: otherwise the instances of Cloud Run would run short of direct connections.
  - The migrations go directly: goose holds the session lock `pg_try_advisory_lock`, so that two runs do not go at once, and Neon names migrations work for a direct connection. OpenFGA goes to its database the same way: the requests by a pgx pool in the default mode, the migrations by goose (T21).
  - So there are two strings, and both are secrets of R26: `API_MIGRATE_DATABASE_URL`, the direct one, from this task on, and `API_DATABASE_URL`, the pooler's, from the first request of `serve` to the database (T23): a variable comes with the code that reads it. The configuration takes the string from the variable or from a `_FILE` file outside git's working tree and checks not the string but what pgx makes of it: every address that pgx will try — the first one and the fallbacks — is either on this machine, the tests' loopback or a socket, or is reached over TLS that checks the server, that is, with `sslmode=verify-full`. A check of the string by parsing the URL could be bypassed: pgx reads the host from `?host=`, the last of two `sslmode` and `ssl=true` as `require`, and pgx's `require` without `sslrootcert` does not check the certificate. A refusal names the variable, not the string.
- **The migrations** are goose v3.29.0, MIT, by a provider without goose's global registry, on top of `database/sql` of the pgx driver:
  - the files `internal/store/migrations/NNNN_*.sql` are embedded in the binary (`//go:embed`), each goes in its own transaction, and the table `goose_db_version` holds the version;
  - forward only, without Down: `api` is rolled back only by rolling forward (R106 spec);
  - "add first, remove later": while a new revision rolls out, the old one still serves and reads the schema as it was. So `DROP`, `RENAME`, `TRUNCATE`, `ALTER … TYPE` and `SET NOT NULL` go only in a migration with the line `-- contract: <reason>`, where the reason is that nobody reads the old schema any more. A test holds this rule, the numbering from 0001 without gaps and the absence of Down;
  - the command `api migrate` writes to the log the event `migrated` — how many were applied and the schema version —, and a failure as the event `failure` with the moment `migrate`, by its kind. Who runs it at the deployment is В38.
- **The schema of E2** is the migration `0001_accounts.sql`, by [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.2 and 3.10:
  - `accounts` — the UID of the sign-in service, the only unique field, which does not change; the owner's statement with its time; the PIN's hash, the counter, the start of the count and the lock; the tariff; the offset in minutes, from −720 to 840 and a multiple of 15; the end of the day; the date of the last request; the mark "being deleted". All the fields but the ID and the UID may be empty: after the mark, only the ID, the UID and the time of the mark stay in the row (R21). The fields that a request updates are in no unique index: `FOR KEY SHARE` lets through only an `UPDATE` that touches no keys;
  - `invite_codes` — the SHA-256 of the code, the tariff, the time of redemption and the binding to an account. Redemption and binding are stored apart: deleting the account removes the binding, and the code stays redeemed (R21);
  - `profiles` — the fields of section 1 and of the contract's `Profile`, the codes those of the contract; the owner profile is one per account and an adult, and an adult does not ask before showing;
  - `consents` — the adult's consent and the child consent, "waiting" without a profile or "confirmed"; what else the record writes — who gave it and how it was checked — T26 will specify with its migration;
  - `child_consent_records` — a minimal record without the ID of the account and of the profile (R21; П-14);
  - the rows about a profile refer to it, and the profiles and the consents to the account, `ON DELETE CASCADE`: a row about a profile written after its deletion fails on the foreign key (R21).

  T25 will add the idempotency keys, T26 the list of countries, E3 the tables of attempts, lessons and the learner model.
- **A write under an account** is `store.WriteUnderAccount`: a transaction, the account's row `FOR KEY SHARE`, no row — "no account", a mark — "being deleted"; an error of the write rolls the transaction back. The other side is `store.MarkDeleting`, the first step of deletion: the row `FOR UPDATE`, then the mark, in the deletion's transaction. `FOR UPDATE` exactly: a plain `UPDATE` of the mark takes `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, which does not conflict with `FOR KEY SHARE` and would let through a write that does not touch the account's row. A test holds both cases — a write that does not touch the account and a write that updates it: the mark waits for both, and the next write sees it.
- **The tests with the database** are `internal/store/storetest`:
  - the package's `TestMain` calls a helper, and it starts one `embedded-postgres` v2.0.1 at the first test that needs a database, Postgres 17, and the Neon dev project of T29 is created with the same one, — on a free port; the migrations go once into a template database, and each test gets its own database — its copy, `CREATE DATABASE … TEMPLATE` — and can run in parallel;
  - the helper builds the supervisor with `go build` from the module's source code and passes `Config.Supervisor` (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05);
  - `embedded-postgres` is a dependency of the tests, it does not enter the binary, and has no dependencies of its own;
  - Codecov does not count the helper's lines: what it does not cover are failures of the environment, no `go` or Postgres did not start, and a test will not cause them.
- **The release's licenses** — `THIRD_PARTY` names pgx, `pgpassfile`, `pgservicefile`, `puddle`, goose, `interpolate`, `go-retry`, `multierr`, `x/sync` and `x/text`, with the files `LICENSE` or `LICENSE.txt` (R28). All are MIT, Apache-2.0 or BSD-3-Clause.

— *Why:*
- goose: OpenFGA requires it too, so it comes in any case, and the provider takes embedded files and holds the lock;
- the pooler for `serve`: Neon's direct connections go by the size of the compute, and the instances of Cloud Run grow with the load; a request of `api` keeps no session state;
- a server per package and a database per test: initdb and the start of Postgres take seconds, and a copy of the template milliseconds, and the tests' databases do not see each other.

— *Rejected:*
- tern: it works on pgx directly, but pulls in sprig and a dozen of its dependencies;
- a migrator of our own: code that would have to be maintained, when goose comes with OpenFGA anyway;
- golang-migrate: its module requires the drivers of a dozen databases and cloud SDKs;
- everything direct: the connections would hit Neon's limit as the instances grow;
- everything through the pooler, with goose's table lock: Neon names migrations work for a direct connection outright;
- Down migrations: `api`'s rollback is forward only, and a Down that nobody runs is not tested;
- only the rule and review: a violation would pass CI;
- a Postgres for each test: seconds per test.

— *Consequence:* Д6 is only the Neon dev project, T29. The phrase of section 3.2 "Postgres for each test" is replaced: Postgres per package, a database per test. — RUN.md, T20; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.11 and 4.

## Devcontainer

**R30. The `toolchain` stage of the devcontainer of `api` installs the package `libnss-wrapper` from a snapshot of the Ubuntu archive, and the helper of the database tests supplies through it a name for a uid that the image's `/etc/passwd` does not know. This is the first difference of the devcontainer of `api` from the container of `spec`.** The author's answer of 2026-11-08 to the failure of "Go / test" of pull request #30, task T20 of epic E2.
- **The failure.** The shared `go.yml` of `.github` runs the `toolchain` stage under the runner's uid — `docker run --user "$(id -u):$(id -g)"` (R16 .github) —, and the image has no such user. `initdb` looks up the name of its uid and stops: "could not look up effective user ID 1001: user does not exist". In the devcontainer the tests run as `vscode`, whom the image knows, and there is no failure.
- **What was done:**
  - `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, the `toolchain` stage: `libnss-wrapper` from the snapshot of the Ubuntu archive `UBUNTU_SNAPSHOT`, which is raised by hand, like a version (R40 spec), the same way as `repo-template` of `spec` installs the browser's libraries;
  - `internal/store/storetest`, only when the uid is not in `/etc/passwd`: writes `passwd` and `group` with this uid into its temporary folder and sets `LD_PRELOAD`, `NSS_WRAPPER_PASSWD` and `NSS_WRAPPER_GROUP`; `embedded-postgres` passes the environment to the processes of Postgres. The production image has no such library: the image is the distroless one of R28.

— *Why:* the database tests run in the same "Go / test" as the others, and their coverage goes to Codecov with the same upload; the task does not wait for another repository.

— *Rejected:*
- a change of `go.yml` of `.github` — mount the runner's `/etc/passwd` into the container: it would fix all the Go repositories, but T20 would wait for a release of `.github`, and the shared workflow is a change of another repository;
- an `/etc/passwd` open for writing in the `toolchain` stage, to which the helper appends its line: no package, but the devcontainer image becomes less strict;
- a CI job of its own for the database tests, and a skip in "Go / test": the database's coverage would leave the upload of "Go / coverage".

— *Consequence:* the devcontainer of `api` now differs from the container of `spec` by this package and `ARG UBUNTU_SNAPSHOT`; a move to a new tag of `spec` compares the Dockerfile with the template and keeps this difference. — RUN.md, T20.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T43; R52): **the second difference from the container of `spec` is Go 1.27.4 instead of 1.27.3 in the `toolchain` stage.** The author's answer of 2026-11-11: in the pull request of T43. The required check "Go / vuln" failed on `GO-2026-6859`: "HTTP/1 server crash due to MaxBytesReader header race in net/http", fixed in 1.27.4 ([the record GO-2026-6859](https://vuln.go.dev/ID/GO-2026-6859.json), read 2026-11-11). T43 did not bring the vulnerability in: the vulnerability database was extended after the green CI of `main` on 2026-11-10. go1.27.4 came out 2026-11-10, the sums of the archives `linux-amd64` and `linux-arm64` are from [go.dev/dl](https://go.dev/dl/?mode=json&include=all) and [Release History](https://go.dev/doc/devel/release), read 2026-11-11. `GO_VERSION` and the sums are in `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, `go 1.27.4` in `go.mod` (an amendment to R26), the image of the `build` stage in the `Dockerfile` (an amendment to R28). The author rebuilds the container. Raising Go in the template is the row «В реестр spec» В49 of RUN.md; a move to a tag of `spec` with Go 1.27.4 or newer removes this difference. — *Rejected:* a separate pull request before T43 — the author's decision; waiting for a tag of `spec` — T43 would not have been merged before it.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T53; R60): **the second difference is removed** — `spec` v0.15.1 raised the template's Go to 1.27.4 (the answer to В49, the 2026-11-11 amendment to R64 spec), and `GO_VERSION` and the sums of the `toolchain` stage of `api` match the container of `spec` line for line: the move required no change of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`. Beyond the template of the kind `go` — the gopls step, its lock file and the Go extension (R102 spec) — the devcontainer of `api` now differs from the container of `spec` by the package `libnss-wrapper` of this entry and the volume of R39. `go 1.27.4` in `go.mod` and the image of the `build` stage of the `Dockerfile` are as before (the 2026-11-11 amendments to R26 and R28).

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T72; R67): **the third difference is the volume `novatrail-dev-token`**, which `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` mounts at `/home/vscode/.novatrail-token` and the Dockerfile makes as `vscode` with the mode `700`, beside the folder of the volume of R39: the custom token of the test account `dev-app` for the local build of `app`. A move to a new tag of `spec` compares `.devcontainer` with the template and keeps the three: the package of this entry, the volume of R39 and this one.

## OpenFGA in the process

**R31. Rights are OpenFGA v1.22.1, as a library in the process of `api`: the package `pkg/server` on the Postgres storage in the database `openfga`, and the storage's schema is run by OpenFGA's own files through goose with a session lock, like the migrations of the database of `api`. The command `migrate` creates the store `api` and the model of section 3.1, and `serve` only checks them. The port `internal/authz` checks `can_learn`, `can_manage` and `can_view`, writes and deletes the tuple `owner` and reads the account's tuples.** The author's decisions of 2026-11-08 in the planning of T21: connection strings of its own for the database `openfga`, and the store and the model in `migrate`. Task T21 of epic E2.
- **The packages** are only `pkg/server`, `pkg/storage/postgres`, `pkg/storage/sqlcommon`, `pkg/logger` and `assets` of OpenFGA and `transformer` of `openfga/language` v0.3.2. The server is `server.NewServerWithOpts` with the storage `postgres.New`, and its methods — `Check`, `Write`, `Read`, `ListStores`, `CreateStore`, `WriteAuthorizationModel` — `api` calls directly, with the requests of `openfgav1`, without gRPC. `Close` closes the storage too ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4).
- **The storage's migrations are not `RunMigrations`.** Its package `pkg/storage/migrate` brings `go-sql-driver/mysql` under MPL-2.0 into the binary — outside the allowed list (RULES.md, Licenses), and it itself works on goose's global state and without a lock. The migration files of OpenFGA — `assets.EmbedMigrations`, `migrations/postgres` — are run by a goose provider with `WithSessionLocker` over a direct connection, as R29 runs the migrations of the database of `api`. That the MySQL driver is not in the binary is held by `release-licenses`: the module is not in `THIRD_PARTY`, and `ci-lint` fails if it enters. The helper with the lock is one for both databases, `store.MigrateFiles`.
- **The store and the model.** Store names in OpenFGA are not unique, and a session lock cannot be taken through the pooler, so instances of `serve` starting together would create two stores or two models. Therefore:
  - `api migrate`, after the schema, under the session's `pg_advisory_lock` on the direct connection, finds or creates the store `api` and writes the embedded model only when no model of the store says the same. Models are compared without the ID, by the schema, the types and the conditions. Two stores `api` are a refusal, for both `migrate` and `serve`;
  - `serve` at start — `authz.Open`, from T23 on — finds the store and, among all its models, the one that says the same as the embedded one, and keeps to its ID. No store or no such model — it does not start: this means `migrate` did not run before the revision (В38). Any such model is looked for, not the last one: while a new revision rolls out, its `migrate` has already written the new model, and the old revision still starts on cold instances — on its own; and a rollback to the old revision finds its model, and `migrate` does not write it a second time. So the model, like the schema, changes by the rule "add first, remove later": a new model does not remove what the old revision rests on;
  - the model is `internal/authz/model.fga`, the block of [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.1, word for word; a test holds one to the other.
- **The connection.** The storage's pool is its own, `store.Open`, checked by `Ping` under the start's context: a stop that was asked for while the database wakes up does not wait; the storage closes it along with itself.
- **The connection strings** are its own for the database `openfga`, as for the database `api` (R29): `API_MIGRATE_OPENFGA_DATABASE_URL`, the direct one, for `migrate`, from this task on, and `API_OPENFGA_DATABASE_URL`, the pooler's, for `serve`, from T23 on. Both are secrets of R26 with the check of R29: every address is on this machine or over TLS that checks the server. The OpenFGA storage keeps no session state, and it works in Neon's pooler.
- **The port `authz.Rights`:**
  - `Can(account, right, profile)` is `Check` by the model found at opening, with `HIGHER_CONSISTENCY`. OpenFGA turns on no check caches by default, and there is no secondary database, so a check sees a write before it. Another's profile and a profile that does not exist give the same "no" (section 3.1);
  - `WriteOwner` and `DeleteOwner` — a repeat is harmless (`OnDuplicate` and `OnMissing` are `ignore`); `DeleteOwned` — in batches of 100, as many as OpenFGA takes; `OwnedProfiles` — all pages of `Read` by `user:<account ID>`, the relation `owner` and the type `learner` (R21);
  - the model's objects are `user:<account ID>` and `learner:<profile ID>`.
- **"Before `COMMIT`".** OpenFGA has its own database, and there is no one transaction over both. The tuple is written from inside the open transaction of the database of `api` — in the `fn` of `store.WriteUnderAccount` —, after the profile's rows: if it was not written, the error of `fn` rolls the transaction back; if it was written and `COMMIT` failed, a "dangling" tuple without a profile row remains. It gives no rights to anything: a request by it finds no profile, and the daily job removes it (R21; T28). A test shows both outcomes.
- **The log.** OpenFGA's logger is noop: OpenFGA writes through zap, with the IDs of the store, the model and the request in fields that the log's guard would drop (R26). `migrate` writes the event `migrated` for each database — the field `database`, `api` or `openfga`, and for `openfga` also `store_created` and `model_written`.
- **Cost.** 74 third-party modules enter the binary — OpenFGA, grpc, otel, prometheus, cel-go, viper, zap and others, all under licenses of the list —, and `THIRD_PARTY` names each; five have a NOTICE, and grpc's is `NOTICE.txt`, and `release-licenses` carries it too. The binary grew from 11.9 to 33.8 MB (measured 2026-11-08). The package `pkg/server` registers metrics in Prometheus's global registry at load; `api` does not serve them.

— *Why:*
- `migrate` already goes over the direct connection and once per deployment (R29; В38): under its lock the store and the model are not doubled, and `serve` has nothing to apply;
- strings of its own for `openfga` allow keeping the rights storage under a Neon role of its own or, in self-hosting, on another server;
- OpenFGA's migration files through a provider of our own give the same lock as the database of `api` has, and bring no MPL-2.0 license into the binary.

— *Rejected:*
- `RunMigrations`: MPL-2.0 in the binary, goose's global state and no lock at all;
- the store and the model at the start of `serve`, as R28 canvas-api: a race of instances and a spare store that would have to be cleaned up;
- an `openfga` string derived from the string of `api`: the rights storage would be tied to the same server and role;
- OpenFGA's log through the guard: its lines would become `unregistered_event` without fields;
- a check cache: a write of rights and a check after it would diverge for the cache's lifetime.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-08 amendment to R04 — `migrate` applies the model, and `serve` checks it. — RUN.md, T21; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.2 and 4.

## The sign-in service port

**R32. The port of the sign-in service is `internal/authn`: the check of the ID token with revocation and the refusal class, deleting a user at the sign-in service and iterating over the users. The adapter is the Admin SDK for Go v4.22.0 whole, and the adapter's tests run on the real SDK against a stand-in Google over TLS, without the emulator. The service account's key is the secret `API_FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS`, only outside a deployment; in Cloud Run, the service's credentials, without a key.** The author's decisions of 2026-11-09 in the planning of T22: a stand-in Google, a secret of its own, the class "invalid" and the SDK whole. Closes in Д5 the emulator and the key. Task T22 of epic E2.
- **The port `authn.Port`** — the operations of R03:
  - `Verify` gives a `Token` — the UID and `auth_time` — or a `*Refusal` with a class: "expired", "invalid", "revoked", "no user", "disabled" or "failure" (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R20). For "revoked" and "no user", the refusal has the UID too: `VerifyIDToken` reads it, by the signature, without a request. The SDK's errors come to the helpers `Is…` unwrapped, and the order matters: `IsIDTokenInvalid` is true for an expired token, for a revoked one and for the token of a disabled user alike;
  - `RemoveUser` is step 3 of deleting an account (R21): `RevokeRefreshTokens`, then `DeleteUser`, even if the revocation failed. "No user" means that the step is done already;
  - `Users` iterates over all users, with the time of the last sign-in and of the token's refresh, for the operator's job (R21);
  - `Fresh` and `FreshFor` are the fresh sign-in of R20: `auth_time` not older than 300 s and not ahead of the clock of `api` by more than 300 s. The SDK does not check `auth_time` at all: it gives a tolerance of 300 s only to `iat` and `exp`. So freshness is entirely the code of `api`.

  The port gives only the UID and the time. The code reads no email, name, phone or other claims, and a test holds this (R29 canvas-api). The fake port for the tests of T23, T27 and T28 is `authntest.Fake`.
- **Errors without data.** The texts of the SDK's errors name the UID — "no user exists with the uid: …". So an error of the port says only the class or the operation that failed, and the SDK's error is its segment. The log names this segment by its type (R26), and it does not get into the answer to `app`.
- **The Admin SDK whole.** An `auth.Client` is built only through the root package `firebase.google.com/go/v4`: `auth.NewClient` takes an `internal.AuthConfig`, and an internal package cannot be imported from another module. The root package pulls in Firestore, Cloud Storage and Monitoring. When `serve` takes the port (T23), 32 modules enter the binary, all under Apache-2.0, MIT or BSD-3 and without NOTICE, and it grows from 33.8 to 51.8 MB (a trial of 2026-11-09, a build with `-s -w`). govulncheck and `go-licenses` are green on the trial. In T22 the binary does not take the port yet, and `THIRD_PARTY` does not change.
- **The tests: a stand-in Google** (Д5). A `httptest` server with TLS answers for Google's keys for the ID token and for the calls of Identity Toolkit that the SDK makes: `accounts:lookup`, `accounts:update`, `accounts:delete` and `accounts:batchGet`. The test signs tokens with its own RS256 key, and the real SDK checks the signature, the expiry, the project and the revocation.
  - The SDK takes Google's keys with a client that cannot be passed from outside: it is a clone of `http.DefaultTransport` at the moment the client is built. So the package's `TestMain` replaces `http.DefaultTransport`. The new transport connects only to the stand-in server and trusts only its certificate, so the test does not go out to the network. And if the SDK stops taking this transport, the tests fail rather than pass it by.
  - The calls of user management go through `option.WithHTTPClient`.
  - The devcontainer does not change.
- **The key** is a secret of R26: `API_FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS` or `API_FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS_FILE`.
  - A file in git's working tree is rejected, as for the connection strings.
  - Only a key of the type `service_account` is accepted. The adapter checks the type, because the library checks it only at the first call. The key is passed through `option.WithAuthCredentialsJSON` with the type: `WithCredentialsJSON` and `WithCredentialsFile` are deprecated "because of a potential security risk".
  - In a deployment, when `K_SERVICE` is set, the key is rejected, and outside a deployment it is required. Without a key, the adapter takes the service account's token only from the metadata server, `google.ComputeTokenSource`, with the scopes the SDK itself asks for. The deployment gives the service account its role. The adapter never takes the default credentials: they would read `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` too, and the file of `gcloud auth application-default login` — a user's credentials, perhaps of another project.
  - The project is `API_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`, and it is required: the Firebase project may differ from the Google Cloud project where `api` runs, and `api` does not let the SDK guess it from the environment.
  - With `FIREBASE_AUTH_EMULATOR_HOST` set, the adapter is not built: the SDK then does not check the token's signature and goes to the host of the variable over HTTP.

  `serve` reads the variables from T23 on.
- **The class in the log and the time of a call.** The log names an error by the types of its segments (R26), and all refusals have one type, `*authn.Refusal`. So the interceptor of T23 writes the class into the log line, as a separate field with the set of values of `Class`. The SDK repeats a call up to 4 times — on a network error and on 503 — and waits up to two minutes, so the port's calls on a request's path go under the request's deadline (T23).

— *Why:*
- a stand-in Google checks the same path as the deployment: the signature, the revocation and the UID of a revoked token without a second request. The emulator cannot do this: in its mode `VerifyIDToken` checks the revocation too (`c.isEmulator || checkRevokedOrDisabled`), so the UID of a revoked token and of the token of a deleted user cannot be read through it, and it does not check signatures;
- a secret of its own keeps the key under the same check "outside the working tree" as the connection strings, and picks up no other credentials of the machine;
- "invalid" apart from "failure": a forged token is not a failure of the service, and with the class field that T23 adds, the log tells a forgery from an expired token;
- the SDK whole: the check of revocation and the parsing of the token stay the SDK's code (R20).

— *Rejected:*
- the Firebase Auth emulator: Java 21 and `firebase-tools` in the devcontainer and CI, tokens without a signature, and the path of the UID of a revoked token, which it would not check;
- a stand-in emulator through `FIREBASE_AUTH_EMULATOR_HOST`: the same trouble with the UID of a revoked token, and the signature is not checked;
- `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` and the default credentials in general: the path to the key goes past the check of the working tree, and the machine's credentials, gcloud's of another project included, would be picked up by themselves;
- a user's credentials through `gcloud auth application-default login`: gcloud is needed in the devcontainer, which is a difference from the container of `spec`, and Identity Toolkit requires a quota project with them;
- a JWT check of our own and REST calls without the SDK: the binary would be 18 MB smaller, but `api` would hold a copy of the SDK's logic, which R20 rejected;
- "invalid" as "failure": a forgery would get `unavailable`, and `app` would repeat the request.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-09 amendments to R03 and R20. Д5 is narrowed to the dev project (T29). In T23: the variables, 32 modules in `THIRD_PARTY` and the license name of `cloud.google.com/go`: by the rule of `THIRD_PARTY` it would be `go`, and `LICENSE-go` in the release is already the license of Go. — RUN.md, T22; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.10, 3.11 and 4.

## The Connect server and the checks

**R33. `serve` answers by the contract `novatrail.api.v1` on Connect v2 — `connectrpc.com/connect/v2` v2.0.2, on which the contracts module is built —, and before the handler each request passes the checks of section 3.1 by an interceptor, by the table of methods: the token class, the mark "being deleted", the account, the right to the profile of its message and fresh sign-in last. The request's deadline is 30 s, the date of the last request is written once a day, and a user deleted bypassing `api` puts the mark on their account. CORS only to the exact origin of `app` from `API_APP_ORIGIN`.** The author's decisions of 2026-11-09 in the planning of T23: 30 s, once a day, an interceptor by the table, the mark. Task T23 of epic E2.
- **The server.** `apiv1connect.Register<Service>Handler` registers all six services of the contract on the `connect.Server`, and `connecthttp.Mount` puts their methods on the same mux as `/health`. A handler that does not exist yet is `Unimplemented<Service>Handler`: the method answers `unimplemented`, but after its checks. The codecs are binary protobuf and JSON with proto names (`UseProtoNames`); Connect itself drops unknown JSON fields (R18; [contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), sections 6 and 9).
- **The table of methods** (`internal/rpc/methods.go`) says for each method of the contract: whether a token is needed — only four open views of `PackageService` go without it; whether an account is needed — only `CreateAccount` goes without it; which right is checked on the message's profile — by the table of section 3.9; whether fresh sign-in is needed — `DeleteProfile`, `DeleteAccount` and `ResetPin` (R20). A test holds the table equal to the server's methods, a right on those and only those methods whose request carries `profile_id`, and "no token" on those that are `NO_SIDE_EFFECTS`. The server does not serve a method without a row. Showing the solution and the skill map have a double right — `can_learn` for the learner, `can_manage` or `can_view` for an adult: the table checks `can_learn`, which in the first slice the owner always has, and the handler of E3 checks the right that its request names.
- **The interceptor of the checks** — in the order of R20 and section 3.1:
  1. the token from `Authorization: Bearer`; if there is none, `unauthenticated` without a cause;
  2. `authn.Port.Verify` and the refusal class by the table of section 3.10. A revoked token of a marked account and the token of a user that does not exist are "account gone"; a failure is `unavailable`; the rest is `unauthenticated` without a cause;
  3. the account by the UID: a mark is "account gone" on any method, `CreateAccount` too; a UID without an account is "no access", `permission_denied`, but for `CreateAccount`;
  4. the date of the last request;
  5. the right — on the message, when the wrapped `Receive` has received it, before the handler and before any reading by the profile ID: an ID that is not a UUID in the form the database writes it — lowercase, with hyphens —, OpenFGA's "no" for the right and a profile row that is not there under the account are one refusal, "no profile", `not_found` (section 3.1). The interceptor reads the profile row after the right — so that a dangling tuple (R21) gives no right to anything, and fresh sign-in stays last;
  6. fresh sign-in last: "fresh sign-in needed" with `max_auth_age_seconds` 300.

  Who passed the checks — the UID, the sign-in time and the account ID — the handler takes from `rpc.CallerFrom`. A refusal is `rpc.Refuse`: a Connect error with a code and the detail `Refusal`, and the text is the cause's name. A failure of a port or the database is, to the client, a code without a text, and to the log, the error's kind. Step 5, "consent given", is the helper `rpc.RequireConsent` for the handlers of the learning requests of E3: if the profile has no confirmed consent, "no consent", `failed_precondition`.
- **"No user, and the account has no mark"**: the user was deleted bypassing `api`. The interceptor puts the mark on the account with `store.MarkDeleting` and answers "account gone"; if it was not put, `unavailable`, and a repeat puts it. Full deletion at this point is T27, and the job of T28 completes it.
- **The request's deadline** is `API_REQUEST_TIMEOUT`, 30 s by default, less than `API_HTTP_WRITE_TIMEOUT`: the answer of a request cut off by the deadline still has time to go out. The client's deadline from `Connect-Timeout-Ms` is taken if it is shorter. The calls of the ports obey it — the Firebase SDK repeats a call up to 4 times and waits up to two minutes (R32) —, as do the record "going" of an idempotency key (T25) and the job's completion of the marks (T28).
- **The date of the last request** is `store.TouchAccount` on a request that passed the class and the mark, when the date read together with the account is not today by UTC: the other requests of the day make no write, and the `UPDATE` itself, with `last_request_on IS DISTINCT FROM` today's date, writes the row no more than once a day even when two requests race. The `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` of this `UPDATE` does not hinder writes under the account, and it conflicts with the `FOR UPDATE` of the mark, as it should (R21).
- **CORS** is a wrapper of the whole handler: on all paths and all answers, errors, `404` and the `500` of a panic too, the headers are set at the moment the answer begins, so the clearing of the answer's headers on a panic does not take them away. The origin is exact, `API_APP_ORIGIN`, in the form a browser writes it: the host lowercase, without the default port, without a path and `*`, and in a deployment only `https`; if it is not set, `api` stands on the origin of `app`, and CORS is not needed. Preflight is `204`, the methods `GET` and `POST`, the headers `Authorization`, `Content-Type`, `Connect-Protocol-Version` and `Connect-Timeout-Ms`, `Max-Age` 7200, and the answers have `Expose-Headers: Retry-After` (R18; [contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15).
- **The log** is the event `request` of the log's table: the method is the contract's procedure name, the Connect code, the cause is the name of the `Cause`, the token's refusal class, the time in milliseconds, and for a failure the error's kind. No body, no headers, no address, no UID, token or ID: the values of the method and the cause are forms of the contract's names, the code and the class are closed sets, and a test compares them with the contract, Connect and `authn`. A panic of a handler leaves a line too — with the code `internal`, next to the line `panic`. There is no line for a request that Connect rejects before the interceptors — a wrong `Content-Type`, a GET of a method with side effects, a broken GET: such a request has no method yet.
- **`serve`** reads the variables of `config.ServeFrom`: `API_DATABASE_URL` and `API_OPENFGA_DATABASE_URL` — the secrets of the pooler's strings with the check of R29, `API_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`, `API_FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS` — required outside a deployment, a refusal in a deployment (R32), and `API_APP_ORIGIN`. Before the port it opens the pool of the database of `api` with `Ping`, `authz.Open` and the port `authn`; if one did not open, a start failure with the field `database`. It closes them in the share of the exit left for closing: a request that did not fit in the drain still holds a connection, and the pool's `Close` would wait for it longer than the platform's grace period.
- **The check of the image.** `serve` no longer starts without the databases and the sign-in service, so the job "Image" (R28) starts PostgreSQL on the runner — `postgres:17.11-bookworm` with a digest, the same version 17.11 as the tests have; Dependabot does not see the script, and it is raised by hand —, migrates both databases with the command `migrate` of the release image and starts each image with a service account key made for this run: the key belongs to no project, and `serve` sends it nowhere. Then the same `/health` and the stop on SIGTERM.
- **Cost.** The Admin SDK with its modules, Connect and the contracts module entered the binary: `THIRD_PARTY` is 108 modules, all under licenses of the list. The license names of `cloud.google.com/go` and `google.golang.org/api` would by the rule be `go` and `api` — as for the licenses of Go and of `api` itself in the release —, so they keep the host, and `release-licenses` refuses a repeated name and these two. The `linux/amd64` binary with `-s -w` is 53.3 MB against 33.8 for v0.1.3 (measured 2026-11-09).

— *Why:*
- the checks in one interceptor keep the order of R20 in one place: a check forgotten in a handler would be a refusal nobody notices, and the table and its test do not let a method be forgotten;
- the right on the message, not in the handler: "before any reading by the profile ID" by itself;
- 30 s hold the AI calls of E3 — the p50 of an essay call is 11.9 s, of a hint 5.1 s (section 4) — and free the handler long before the two minutes of the SDK's repeats;
- once a day: the date is needed to the day, and a write on each request would load the account's row, which the writes under it hold;
- the mark on the path "no user" at once answers other devices "account gone" and closes the writes under the account, and a UID without a user does not hold it indefinitely until T27.

— *Rejected:*
- checks by helpers in each handler;
- writing the date on each request;
- on the path "no user", only the answer, without the mark;
- 60 s: a handler stuck on the SDK's repeats would hold on for a minute; 15 s: an essay call at the tail of the distribution would not fit;
- `rs/cors` and `connectrpc.com/cors`: a module and rules that ten lines of our own do not need, and they would not keep the headers on the answer of a panic.

— *Consequence:* T24–T27 build the handlers of `AccountService` on `rpc.CallerFrom` and `rpc.Refuse`, and T25 the records of keys under the request's deadline. — RUN.md, T23; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.2, 3.9, 3.11 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T36; R46): the limit of a request's message is 4 MiB, `rpc.MaxMessage`, through `connecthttp.WithReadMaxBytes`; more is `resource_exhausted` before parsing. This is also Connect v2's default limit, so Д22 was wrong: a message of any size was not read into memory, the limit simply was not named anywhere. The largest message of the contract is a crop of the sheet: by the rule of the crop, no more than 1568 px a side, grey, 8 bits, that is, less than 2.5 MB, even if PNG compressed nothing.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T43; R52): the limit of a request's message is 11 MiB, not 4: a scan of the consent form is up to 10 MiB ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15). `LegalService.GetLegalTexts` goes without a token, like the open views, with `Cache-Control` for an hour.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14* (T74; R69): the `Cache-Control` of `GetLegalTexts` is an hour at most, and no longer than until 00:00 UTC of the nearest day of a next version it gives.

## Account and invite codes

**R34. An invite code is 16 characters of Crockford's base32, 80 bits from `crypto/rand`, in four groups of four; it is read as a person will type it, and the database holds the SHA-256 of the code in this reading. The code is created by the operator's command `api invite` with a tariff — by default `closed_slice`, "closed slice" —, and the command prints it once. `CreateAccount` is one transaction: "already exists" before the code, then the redemption of the code, its binding and the tariff into the account, and the owner's statement with its time. `GetAccount` reads the account in one snapshot.** Task T24 of epic E2, 2026-11-09; the documents decided the task without planning, and the executor chose the form of the code and the command.
- **The code** (`internal/invite`) — Crockford's alphabet, digits and letters without I, L, O and U: `0123-4567-89AB-CDEF`. Reading a code, `api` ignores case, hyphens and whitespace characters — the end of a line from a terminal, a non-breaking space from an email —, and reads O as 0, I and L as 1, as Crockford prescribes. The hash is of the 16 characters of this reading, so every spelling of one code has the same hash. What is not 16 characters of the alphabet is "code not accepted", without a request to the database.
- **The command `api invite [-tariff closed_slice]`** — one code per run. Only the code goes to stdout, and the log to stderr: a failure with the stage `invite` and the field `database`. The connection string is `API_DATABASE_URL`, the pooler's, as for `serve`: writing one row holds no session (R29). The tariffs are the list `invite.Tariffs`, so far with one entry, `closed_slice`; the command rejects any other tariff. The tariffs' caps come with the quota (Д11).
- **`CreateAccount`** (`internal/account`, `store.CreateAccount`):
  1. the account exists — `CAUSE_ALREADY_EXISTS`, `already_exists`, before the statement and before the code, so a repeat after a lost answer gets it, not "code not accepted" (the contract, `CreateAccount`). A marked account is stopped already by the interceptor (R33). A request not in form looks for the account once more before refusing: one created by a neighbouring request after the checks is "already exists" too, and a marked one is "account gone";
  2. the owner's statement is not `true` — `invalid_argument` without a `Refusal`;
  3. the code is not in form, not found or redeemed — `CAUSE_CODE_NOT_ACCEPTED`, `invalid_argument`;
