  4. the transaction: the account's row with the UID, the time of the statement in `adult_stated_at` and today's request date — `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (auth_uid) DO NOTHING`; then one query — `UPDATE invite_codes … WHERE redeemed_at IS NULL RETURNING tariff` in a `WITH` and `UPDATE accounts` by it — redeems and binds the code and gives its tariff to the account. If there is no code, the transaction rolls back, and no account remains.

  Races are safe. Two requests of one UID: the second waits on the unique index of the UID and finds the first one's account — "already exists". Two accounts for one code: the second waits on the code's row and finds it redeemed — "code not accepted". If the first rolled back, the second goes on.
- **`GetAccount`** — the account ID, whether a PIN is set, the profiles — the owner's first, then by the time they were created — and the child consents without a profile, waiting and confirmed. The read is one transaction `REPEATABLE READ, READ ONLY`: the device erases the databases of the profiles that are not in the list (R04 app), and the profiles and consents in it are of one moment. There are no open attempts and runs until E3. An account marked or deleted after the checks is "account gone".

— *Why:*
- 80 bits cannot be brute-forced either through `CreateAccount` or from a stolen table of codes, so a hash without salt and without a secret is enough, as R04 decided, and there is no code in plain form in the database;
- Crockford's alphabet and its reading forgive what a person confuses copying a code from a screen or from an email: case, O and 0, I, L and 1;
- one code in stdout: the operator passes it on as it is, and the log does not hold it;
- one transaction in the contract's order leaves neither an account without a code nor a code redeemed without an account.

— *Rejected:*
- shorter, 8 characters, 40 bits: `CreateAccount` has no request quota, and brute force would become a matter of time;
- HMAC with a secret instead of SHA-256: a second secret of the deployment, which 80 bits do not need;
- several codes per run: the first app needs one, and output on one line does not mix codes up;
- a direct connection, as for `migrate`: the command needs no session, and the operator would need a third connection string;
- the code in a log line: in a Cloud Run job the log is stored, and the code is a secret until it is redeemed.

— *Consequence:* in a Cloud Run job stdout goes to the log too, so `api invite` is run from one's own terminal — locally, for the first app, the author runs it (section 3.1). Changing the tariff by a command and the flag "platform AI closed" come with the quota (Д11). — RUN.md, T24; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 3.6.

## PIN and idempotency keys

**R35. The PIN hash is argon2id at OWASP's minimum: 19 MiB, two passes, one lane, as a PHC string. Failures are counted over the last 15 minutes, and the fifth closes input for 15 minutes from itself. An idempotency key lives in the space of its account, and the record recognizes another request by the request's fingerprint: the SHA-256 of the request without the key, and for a request with a PIN or an age, argon2id with the record's salt. A PIN request writes the action and the key's record in one transaction.** The author's decisions of 2026-11-09 in the planning of T25: OWASP's parameters, the target "account", a slow fingerprint for secrets, 15 minutes from the fifth failure. A sliding window instead of a window from the first failure is a finding of T25's review: at the junction of windows from the first failure up to eight failures got through. Task T25 of epic E2.
- **The hash** (`internal/argon`):
  - argon2id from `golang.org/x/crypto/argon2`: m = 19456 KiB, t = 2, p = 1, a salt of 16 bytes, a hash of 32 bytes. This is OWASP's minimum: "m=19456 (19 MiB), t=2, p=1" (OWASP Password Storage Cheat Sheet, read 2026-11-09).
  - The hash is stored as the PHC string `$argon2id$v=19$m=19456,t=2,p=1$<salt>$<hash>`, and the check goes by the parameters the string names: they can be raised without a migration, and old hashes are checked by their own.
  - The comparison is in constant time.
- **The count of failures** (`internal/pin`):
  - the account holds the time of each failure of the last 15 minutes — the new column `pin_failed_at` —, and the count is the failures younger than 15 minutes;
  - the fifth such failure closes input for 15 minutes from itself, so in any 15 minutes there are no more than five failures, at the junction of windows too;
  - a correct PIN resets the count. Nobody reads the columns `pin_failures` and `pin_failures_since` of migration 0001 any more: a migration with the mark "-- contract:" will remove them;
  - `SetPin`, `ChangePin` with the correct old PIN and `ResetPin` write the new hash and open input.

  The time is the clock of `api` to the microsecond, as in the database: a recorded answer and a refusal read later name the same instant.
- **A PIN request** (`internal/account`):
  1. the form — a PIN of 4–6 digits and a key of 1 to 128 bytes; otherwise `invalid_argument` without a `Refusal`;
  2. one transaction under the account (R29): the account's row `FOR KEY SHARE` with the check of the mark, then `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`. The second conflicts with itself, and not with `FOR KEY SHARE`, so the PIN requests of one account go one after another, and the other writes under the account do not wait for them;
  3. the key's record:
     - there is one, of the same method and request, "done" — the recorded answer, and nothing is counted;
     - of another method or request — `CAUSE_KEY_ALREADY_USED`;
     - "going" — `CAUSE_STILL_GOING`;
  4. the action and its refusals — "PIN already set", "PIN not set", "PIN locked" with the end of the lock;
  5. the new state of the PIN, the answer and the key's record "done" — in the same transaction.

  A refusal is not recorded: the transaction rolls back, and a repeat is checked again. A PIN failure is an answer, not a refusal: it is recorded, and a repeat does not count it. A repeat next to the first request waits for the account's row and finds the first one's record, not "still going".
- **The key's record** (migration 0002) — the account, the key, the method — the contract's procedure —, the profile ID, the fingerprint, the state "going" or "done", the answer — the contract's binary protobuf — and the time. The primary key is `(account_id, key)`. A request of the account itself has no profile; the targets of E3 — an attempt and a run — come as columns of their own with their tables. The PIN answer is whether the PIN matched, how many tries are left and the end of the lock: there is no content in it.
- **The fingerprint** (`internal/idem`) — of the request written by protobuf deterministically, without `idempotency_key`:
  - for most requests, SHA-256;
  - for a request with a PIN — the PIN requests — and with an age — `RequestChildConsent` in T26 — argon2id with the record's salt, with the same parameters.

— *Why:*
- against brute force of the PIN through `api`, the count of failures protects, not the hash: a PIN has 10⁶ values. The hash is needed against whoever reads the database. More memory per hash is shared among the requests of an instance, and adds almost nothing to the protection against brute force from the database;
- 15 minutes from the fifth failure in a sliding window hold "no more than 5 in 15 minutes" in any window;
- the key in the account's space: another account's key says nothing about someone else's action and does not hinder one's own;
- a fast hash of the request would give away the PIN — 10⁶ values — and the age — 18 values —, and by privacy 3.3 spec the age is stored nowhere. A slow fingerprint protects them no worse than the PIN hash and needs no new secret;
- one transaction for a short request: the action and its record do not diverge, and "going" is needed only by a request that waits for AI.

— *Rejected:*
- the second option of RFC 9106, section 4, t = 3, p = 4, 64 MiB: three times the memory per request. OWASP's option of 46 MiB with t = 1 is the same in the other direction;
- a lock until the end of a window from the first failure: at the junction of windows there would be up to ten failures in 15 minutes. A window from the first failure with a lock from the fifth too: at the junction up to eight got through;
- a shared space of keys: the same key of another account would be "key already used";
- an HMAC of the fingerprint with a secret of the deployment: a new secret for the deployment and for the first app;
- a fingerprint without the PIN and the age: the same key with another PIN would get the earlier answer, though the contract says to refuse "another request".

— *Consequence:* a PIN request has up to three argon2id hashes: the fingerprint, the check of the PIN and the hash of the new one, for `ChangePin`. The fingerprint and the hash of the new PIN are computed before the transaction, so no more than one hash holds the account's row and the pool's connection — the check of the PIN or the fingerprint of a repeat. The two phases of the record — "going", then "done" — and the deadline of "going" E3 builds with the first request that waits for AI. The job of T28 deletes records older than a day. The deterministic protobuf encoding is stable within one build; a repeat across the deployment of a new version may get "key already used", and this is an accepted cost. — RUN.md, T25; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.2, 3.9 and 3.11.

## Profiles, the country before the DPIA and consent

**R36. The owner profile is created in the category `adult` by the owner's statement, with a consent — a row given by this statement — and the tuple `owner` before `COMMIT`. A child's profile is created in two steps: a consent "waiting" with the category and the country, then the profile by the confirmed consent. The countries are a list of the allowed ones in the database of `api`, empty at the start, which the command `api countries` keeps; it does not open the EU countries with their regions, the EEA and the United Kingdom before the DPIA. A child's category is by the boundary of 13 years.** The author's decisions of 2026-11-09 in the planning of T26: a list of the allowed, the boundary 13 in code, the adult's consent as a row, one task in two commits. Task T26 of epic E2.
- **The list of countries** (`internal/countries`, migration 0003, `api countries list|allow XX|remove XX`):
  - empty at the start: a country where the law requires more than a statement of age (П-6) is closed until the operator opens it;
  - 42 codes are never opened before the DPIA (П-2):
    - the 27 EU countries — "EU countries" ([european-union.europa.eu](https://european-union.europa.eu/principles-countries-history/eu-countries_en), read 2026-11-09);
    - the EU's outermost regions with codes of their own — MQ, YT, GP, GF, RE and MF: "five French overseas departments – Martinique, Mayotte, Guadeloupe, French Guiana and Réunion; one French overseas community – Saint-Martin". Madeira, the Azores and the Canary Islands have no codes of their own ([European Parliament, "Outermost regions (ORs)"](https://www.europarl.europa.eu/factsheets/en/sheet/100/outermost-regions-ors-), read 2026-11-09);
    - "the three EEA EFTA States – Iceland, Liechtenstein and Norway" ([efta.int](https://www.efta.int/eea/eea-agreement), read 2026-11-09);
    - the United Kingdom — UK GDPR;
    - out of caution, until the lawyer's answer on П-2: the Åland Islands — AX, a part of Finland with a code of its own — and the codes by which the closed countries are called otherwise: UK instead of GB, EL instead of GR, IC — the Canary Islands, EA — Ceuta and Melilla;
  - a removed country accepts no new profiles, and the ones created stay;
  - the country is checked by `CreateOwnerProfile` and `RequestChildConsent` — the requests that name it. `CreateChildProfile` takes the consent's country, and the contract has no refusal about the country for this method.
- **The owner profile** (`CreateOwnerProfile`):
  - the form;
  - then in a transaction under the account: "already exists", before the country, so a repeat gets it even if the country was removed since; then "country not accepted";
  - the rows of the profile and the consent and the tuple `owner` — from inside the open transaction, after the rows, before `COMMIT` (R04, R31). If the tuple was not written, the transaction rolls back.

  The adult's consent is a row of `consents`: confirmed now, the method `owner_statement` — the owner's statement with the account's date (П-6), the category `adult`, the profile's country; "who" is the account itself. The date the profile's age was stated is the date of the statement.
- **The child consent** (`RequestChildConsent`):
  - the age in years, from 0 to 17, required;
  - the category: 0–12 — `under_13`, 13–17 — `from_consent_to_18`. `from_13_to_consent` is empty while the EU and the United Kingdom are closed: for the accepted countries the boundary is 13 (privacy 3.1 spec), and the DPIA brings the table of the ages of digital consent;
  - the row "waiting" is only the category and the country;
  - the age lives in the request's memory, and the key's fingerprint is argon2id (R35).
- **A child's profile** (`CreateChildProfile`):
  1. the consent by the canonical UUID, under the account, `FOR UPDATE`; another's and a nonexistent one are one refusal, `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND`;
  2. the consent has a profile — "already exists", before the state;
  3. "waiting" — `CAUSE_NO_CONSENT`;
  4. the profile of the consent's category and country, the date the age was stated is the date of the request, and the tuple before `COMMIT`.

  There is no confirmation of consent until epic E4 of `spec` (R98 spec; П-1), so the tests check this path, not the first app.
- **The settings** (`UpdateProfile` by `can_manage`, `SetVoicing` by `can_learn`) — the fields that came:
  - the pseudonym — 1–30 code points after trimming spaces at the edges, without control characters: the database does not hold NUL, and an end of line is not part of a name;
  - the avatar — up to 64 printable ASCII characters;
  - the language — a valid BCP 47 tag: subtags of 1–8 letters and digits joined by hyphens, no longer than 64 characters, which `golang.org/x/text/language` parses. A subtag the library does not know is valid too, and an underscore instead of a hyphen, which the library forgives, is not;
  - the grade — 1–12, and 0 clears it;
  - "ask before showing" for `adult` — `invalid_argument`.

  The defaults of a new profile are by the contract: "by steps", voicing on for `under_13` and off for the others, normal speed, and for a child "ask before showing" on.
- **The profile's activity date** — a request with `profile_id` that passed all checks, fresh sign-in too, writes today's date by UTC, once a day, like the account's date (R33). The dates of a new profile — of activity and of stating the age — are by UTC too, not by the session's time zone: by it the profile is deleted after 12 months without activity (П-9; T28).
- **The idempotency key** — the helper of T25 is generalized: the account's row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, the key's record and an action over the transaction; the PIN and the consent are its actions. The fingerprint of both is argon2id: the fast one comes with the first request of E3 that has neither a PIN nor an age. The profile and the consent share the check of the canonical UUID, `store.CanonicalID`.

— *Why:*
- closed by default: a country of П-6 that nobody named the operator opens themselves, rather than finding it open;
- 42 codes in code, not in the database: the EU cannot be opened before the DPIA even by mistake, nor through a region with a code of its own;
- the boundary 13 holds because the EU is closed: no accepted country needs another;
- the adult's consent as a row: privacy 4 spec keeps consent for each learner, and the helper "consent given" (R33) reads one table for everyone;
- "already exists" before the country and the state: a repeat after a lost answer learns that the profile exists and reads the account.

— *Rejected:*
- a list of forbidden countries: a country of П-6 would turn out open;
- the list in the deployment configuration: the operator's command keeps it (section 3.1), and a change of the configuration is a deployment;
- the age of digital consent as a column of the list: only a lawyer gives the values, and before the DPIA it is 13 for all accepted countries;
- the adult's consent without a row, only the account's statement;
- two tasks instead of one.

— *Consequence:* for the first app, `api countries allow` for the author's country, then `CreateOwnerProfile`. The confirmation, withdrawal and removal of consent, the yearly update of the category and the change of the country are epic E4 of `spec`. Deleting the profile and the consents is T27, deletion by the activity period T28. — RUN.md, T26; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1 and 3.2.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T30; R40): an account has no more than 10 consents "waiting", by the constant `waitingConsentsMax` in code. A request beyond the limit is `invalid_argument` without a `Refusal`, and a repeat with the same key is the same consent even at the limit: the key's record answers before the count (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R98 spec, the answer to В41; Д144 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). The count goes under the account's row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, which every request with a key takes already (R35), so requests side by side do not exceed the limit. The number is the author's decision in the planning of T30: a family needs a few consents, not hundreds, and it does not depend on the tariff, like the boundary of 13 years. With the release of epic E4 of `spec` the refusal becomes the cause `CAUSE_TOO_MANY_CONSENTS` with the code `resource_exhausted` (Д21 in table 4 of RUN.md). The basis and the method of consent were decided by R116 spec: under 18, consent; verifiable parental consent is a signed form whose scan the operator keeps outside the database of `api`, and before the public launch a card transaction is added to it; above the age of digital consent the learner gives consent themselves, in their own profile. The methods of confirmation, withdrawal, removal of a waiting consent and the learner's own consent are written by task T103 of E4 (П-1 closed). The statement "I am 18" with its date and the method of consent as confirmation of adulthood at a child's profile are R118 spec (П-6 closed), and the list of countries before the DPIA is R119 spec (П-2 closed).

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T43; R52): the category is computed by the table of the ages of digital consent `spec.ConsentAge`, not by the boundary of 13 years: below the country's age, a parent gives consent by a signed form by the consent request code, and from it the learner themselves, in their own profile, which is created at once and waits. The country is one of the 249 codes of the part's schema. The limit of waiting consents without a profile is the earlier 10, and the refusal beyond it is `CAUSE_TOO_MANY_CONSENTS` with the code `resource_exhausted` (Д144). The consent requests name the current versions of their texts (R136 spec).

## Deletion in code

**R37. Deleting a profile and an account is the package `internal/deletion` in the order of R21: the database, then the tuples, then the user at the sign-in service, and the account's row last. What failed after the database is "unfinished": the answer is "deleted" all the same, a log line names the failure, and the mark or a dangling tuple is left to the job. A repeated deletion of a profile that has a tuple and no row deletes the tuple. The path "no user" deletes the account whole by the same package.** Task T27 of epic E2, 2026-11-09; the documents decided the task without planning, and the executor chose the design in code.
- **A profile** (`DeleteProfile` by `can_manage` and fresh sign-in):
  1. a transaction under the account: the account's row `FOR KEY SHARE` with the check of the mark, the profile's row `FOR UPDATE`;
  2. a child consent goes into the minimal record `child_consent_records`: the ID, when given, how checked, when deleted. An adult's consent goes whole;
  3. the profile's row, and with it, by the foreign keys, the consent and the records of idempotency keys;
  4. after `COMMIT`, the tuple. If it was not deleted, "unfinished": there is no row, and the tuple gives nothing.

  The account does not delete its last profile.
- **A dangling tuple.** `DeleteProfile` has a mark in the table of methods: when the right is there and the profile's row under the account is not, the interceptor deletes the tuple and answers "no profile", as section 3.10 says — only with fresh sign-in: without it the tuple stays, and the answer is the same. If it was not deleted, `unavailable`, and a repeat deletes it. The other methods do not touch a dangling tuple: the job removes it (T28).
- **An account** (`DeleteAccount` by fresh sign-in):
  1. one transaction:
     - the account's row `FOR UPDATE` and the mark;
     - the minimal records of the confirmed child consents — and of those whose profile is not created yet;
     - the profiles, the consents, the records of keys;
     - the account's fields — the statement, the PIN, the tariff, the offset, the end of the day, the request date;
     - the code's binding — the code stays redeemed;
  2. the tuples by `user:<account ID>`, in batches;
  3. `RemoveUser` — revocation and deletion, "no user" is done. It runs even when step 2 failed;
  4. only when 2 and 3 went through — a transaction: the clearing once more and the account's row.

  If step 1 failed, `unavailable`, and nothing changed. Steps 2–4 failing is "unfinished". A repeat gets "account gone" from the interceptor.
- **"No user", an account without a mark**: the interceptor deletes the account with the same `Account`, without fresh sign-in, and answers "account gone". Step 3 then finds the user deleted. If step 1 failed, `unavailable`, and a repeat deletes it. An account that a neighbouring request of the same user deleted first is "account gone" too.
- **`Finish`** — steps 2–4 for a marked account. `Account` calls it, and the job of T28 will call it for the marks that requests left.
- **The log.** An "unfinished" deletion answers with the code `ok` — and on the path "no user", "account gone" —, and its line `request` is of the error level, with the failure's kind (`rpc.NoteFailure`): the operator sees that there is something to complete. One failure of steps 2–4 is on its own, so that its kind is visible; two are together.
- **The export** — `ExportProfile` and `ExportAccount` answer `unimplemented` until epic E4 of `spec` records the format.

— *Why:*
- one package for the four paths — `DeleteProfile`, `DeleteAccount`, "no user" and the job of T28 — keeps the order of R21 in one place;
- "unfinished" is a separate kind of error: the caller tells "deleted, and the rest will be completed" from "not deleted";
- the interceptor, not the handler, deletes a dangling tuple: it reads the profile's row itself, after the right, and a request without a row does not reach the handler.

— *Rejected:*
- deletion on the path "no user" by the mark alone (R33): the learners' data would live until the job's pass;
- a refusal on an "unfinished" deletion: there is nothing about the learners in the database, and a repeat after the refusal would already get "account gone" — `app` would erase everything and sign out all the same (R21);
- a dangling tuple only for the job: section 3.10 requires a repeated deletion to delete it.

— *Consequence:* the job of T28 completes, through `Finish`, the marks older than the request timeout and removes dangling tuples. Д15 is narrowed. — RUN.md, T27; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.10.

**R38. The daily retention job is the command `api sweep`: one pass per run, seven steps in order, with no state between passes. A pass is bounded by its own period, `-for`, 9 minutes by default; the 12-month period is checked under the lock of the row; a dangling tuple is one older than the request timeout and without a row of the profile at its account; a user of the sign-in service without an account is one 30 days after the latest of the sign-in, the token refresh and the creation. The log is counters by steps; a pass that did not reach its end, or with a refusal, exits with code 1.** Task T28 of epic E2, 2026-11-09; the decisions on the pass's period, the dangling tuple, the user without a sign-in and the command's name were the author's, in planning.
- **The steps** (`internal/sweep`), each choosing its work again by a query:
  1. marks "being deleted" older than the request timeout — `Finish`, steps 2–4 of R37;
  2. accounts without requests for more than 12 months — by the same route as `DeleteAccount`, without a fresh sign-in (`deletion.IdleAccount`);
  3. profiles without activity for more than 12 months — by the same route as `DeleteProfile` (`deletion.IdleProfile`);
  4. users of the sign-in service without an account of `api` — `RemoveUser`;
  5. dangling tuples — `DeleteOwned`, by accounts;
  6. records of idempotency keys older than a day (R17);
  7. OpenFGA's `changelog` older than a day (R05; R27 canvas-api).

  Steps 2–3 come before the tuples and `changelog`, so that the pass itself picks up what it left. A failure of an element is counted, and the row `failure` names its kind and step; the step goes on, and the following steps do in any case.
- **Batches and an interrupted pass.** Accounts, profiles and tuples go by 100. Accounts and profiles are chosen by the primary key, in ascending order of ID after the last one seen: a failed element is not chosen again in the same pass. Users go one at a time, and the account of each is checked right before the deletion: a list of the sign-in service of many pages is minutes out of date by its end, and a user who came back in those minutes and created an account keeps it. Keys and `changelog` are deleted by 1000 rows per `DELETE`; `changelog` has no index by time, but the job keeps no more than two days of records in it. There is no state between passes: the next pass picks up an interrupted one by itself, because each step chooses its work again.
- **The pass's period** is the flag `-for`, 9 minutes by default: less than the 10 minutes that Cloud Run gives a job's task by default. When it runs out, the pass stops between elements, the further steps do not begin, and their rows say `finished: false`. Time is not counted as a failure.
- **12 months** is strictly more: the date of the last request or activity is earlier than the same date a year ago, in UTC. The period is checked again under the lock: an account by `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` with a condition on `last_request_on`, a profile the same way on `active_on`, under `WriteUnderAccount`. Postgres waits for the request that writes today's date and reads the row again, so an owner who came back on the day of the pass loses no data. A profile of an account that was marked after the choice is "nothing done": it goes with the account. The owner's profile is deleted by period the same way as a child's profile: its date of activity is moved only by requests with its ID, not by the account's requests and not by requests on the children's profiles (П-9).
- **A dangling tuple** is a tuple `owner` of an account on a profile, older than the request timeout (the time of the write is given by OpenFGA), whose profile has no row, or a row of another account. The job does not read tuples of the model's other kinds. A young tuple is not touched: it is written before `COMMIT` (R31), and the transaction of its profile may still be going.
- **A user without an account** is a UID that no account has, marked or not, and the latest of whose three times — the sign-in, the token refresh, the creation — is older than 30 days. The creation is `CreationTimestamp` of the Admin SDK, the new field `authn.User.Created`: a user just created in the console is not deleted on the same day.
- **`changelog`** is deleted by SQL through the storage's pool: OpenFGA cannot do this, and `authz.Rights` holds the pool, which it opens itself.
- **The configuration** is `config.SweepFrom`: the databases `api` and `openfga` through the pooler, the Firebase project and key. The log level, the request timeout and the time to close are `config.LoadFrom`, as for `serve`: the deployment gives the job the same variables as the service, and their `API_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` must match. The deployment is recognized by `CLOUD_RUN_JOB`: with it a service account key is forbidden, and outside a deployment it is required (R32). Cloud Run sets `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` for a job's task — «The name of the Cloud Run job being run», and «By default, each task runs for a maximum of 10 minutes» (the container contract and task timeout of Cloud Run, read 2026-11-09; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4).
- **The log.** For each step, a row `swept`: the step, `done`, `failed`, `finished`. Neither ID nor UID: the list of the event's fields does not let them through (R09 spec). The exit is 0 only after a clean pass: a period that depends on the job is "until the next successful pass" (R21).

— *Why:*
- with no state between passes there is nothing to repair when a pass was interrupted: the next one chooses its work again;
- an own period of the pass gives the result row of each step on a break too; without it the platform would stop the pass in the middle of an element silently;
- the 12-month period under the lock of the row is what section 3.10 prescribes, and it rests on the same `FOR UPDATE` as deletion on request;
- a young tuple without a row is usually a profile whose transaction has not yet ended;
- the creation time protects a user who has neither a sign-in nor a token refresh.

— *Rejected:*
- a pass without its own period: on a break by the platform the log would not name where it stopped;
- any tuple without a row being dangling: the job would delete the tuple of a profile being created right now, and that profile would be left without the right;
- only the sign-in and the token refresh: a user without both would be deleted by the very first pass, even one created an hour ago;
- a cursor of the pass in the database: the steps choose their work by a query anyway, and the cursor would have to be maintained and deleted by hand.

— *Consequence:* Д15 is narrowed to the checks on the dev project (T29) and the export. The job's schedule is given by the deployment of `infra`. Closing abandoned attempts and lesson runs, and the quota counters of E3 and the quota epic, will add their own steps to the job themselves. — RUN.md, T28; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.10, 3.11 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-09* (T30; R40): the pass has eight steps — `consent_records` comes before the records of keys. It erases the minimal records of child consents for children deleted more than 5 years ago by the calendar, in batches of 1000, and a younger record stays (R117 spec; the 2026-11-09 amendment to R21). The period runs from the date of deletion in the record itself: there is no ID of the account or of the profile in it.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T33; R43): the pass has ten steps — `abandoned_attempts` and `abandoned_runs` come after `idle_profiles`: an attempt without actions for 7 days is closed "abandoned", a lesson run without actions for 30 days ends "abandoned" (R10, R13). They go after the deletion of profiles by period: a deleted profile has nothing to close.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T41; R51): the pass has thirteen steps — `gone_attempts` and `gone_runs`, the steps of the job after a deployment, come after `idle_profiles`. They close "unchecked" the attempts and lesson runs on packages that the image no longer gives, before `abandoned_attempts` would close them "abandoned". The step `evidence` of T39 already stands after `abandoned_attempts` (R49).

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T43; R52): the pass has fifteen steps — before `keys` come `consent_forms`, which erases the scans of forms after their period through the storage port, and `problem_reports`, which deletes the reports handled more than a year ago. `api sweep` reads the forms' storage, as `serve` does.

**R39. The first run on dev projects is `just dev` and `just dev-check` with the configuration from a file in a Docker volume outside the repository. The ID token is a custom token of the placeholder user `dev-author`, signed by the service account key and exchanged at the sign-in service, with no new Go code. The country of the author's profile on the dev project is the placeholder `US`. The volume `novatrail-api-dev` is a difference of the devcontainer of `api` from the container of `spec`.** Task T29 of epic E2, 2026-11-09; the decisions on the token, the file, the country and the check were the author's, in planning.
- **The configuration file** is lines `KEY=VALUE` in `/home/vscode/.novatrail-dev/dev.env` or at the path `API_DEV_CONFIG`. The recipe refuses a file in a git tree — by the same rule as the binary for a secret file: `.git` in the directory or its parents —, takes only the names `API_*`, reads the lines as they are, without executing them, and prints no value. No secret goes into the process's command line: only through the environment, files and pipes. In it:
  - the pooler's strings and the direct strings of the databases `api` and `openfga`;
  - the Firebase project and the path to the service account key, `API_FIREBASE_CREDENTIALS_FILE`;
  - the Web API key, `API_DEV_WEB_API_KEY`, and, if needed, `API_DEV_UID` — only the recipe reads them, the binary does not know them.
- **The volume.** `.devcontainer` mounts the named volume `novatrail-api-dev` at `/home/vscode/.novatrail-dev`, and the Dockerfile creates this directory, like the directories of the other volumes, with the mode `700`: the volume belongs to `vscode` and survives a rebuild of the container. This is the second difference of the container of `api` from the container of `spec`, after the package `libnss-wrapper` of R30 (CLAUDE.md, Environment): a move to a new tag of `spec` checks `.devcontainer` against the template and keeps both the volume and the line of the Dockerfile.
- **The ID token** (`scripts/dev.sh`):
  - a custom token with Firebase's claims — `alg` `RS256`, `iss` and `sub` the service account's email, `aud` Identity Toolkit, `iat`, `exp` in an hour, `uid` — is signed by `openssl` with the key from the file;
  - REST `accounts:signInWithCustomToken` exchanges it for an ID token;
  - the key, the Web API key and the token go through files, pipes and the configuration of `curl` on stdin, not through the command line.

  The sources are [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4.
- **`just dev-check`** is the whole path:
  1. the build;
  2. `migrate` of both databases;
  3. `countries allow US`;
  4. `serve` on a free port;
  5. the ID token;
  6. `GetAccount`: if there is no account — `api invite` and `CreateAccount`; if there is no owner profile — `CreateOwnerProfile`;
  7. `GetAccount` is checked by `jq`: the account and the owner profile of the category `adult`;
  8. the log of `serve`: each line is JSON, and there is neither UID, nor email, nor connection strings, nor the Web API key, nor the token.

  A repeat skips what was created. The recipe does not go into CI: it needs real projects. The file loader and the custom token are checked in CI by the test `scripts/dev_test.go`: it calls the script's functions in bash, and checks the signature by the public key.
- **Checked 2026-11-09** on the projects `novatrail-api-dev` — Firebase on the Spark plan and Neon on the Free plan in `aws-us-east-1`, Postgres 17: `just dev-check` went through the whole path and the repeat, and the log is clean. The Neon strings are with `sslmode=verify-full`, and pgx understands `channel_binding=require` from Neon's string. The Web API key is «Browser key (auto created by Firebase)» in Google Cloud's Credentials.

— *Why:*
- a custom token gives a real ID token of the sign-in service — the same route of the check with revocation as `app` has —, without `app` and without code that would get into the binary;
- a placeholder UID separates the test account from the author's sign-in through Google: that one will come with `app`;
- the volume keeps the key outside any git tree and does not vanish on a rebuild, and nothing is needed on the host.

— *Rejected:*
- MathTrail's working project `mathtrail-canvas-prod` instead of a new one: `api sweep` would delete its users without an account of `api`;
- waiting for `app` in E3 — the path with a real token would stay unchecked until the first app;
- a small web application for the sign-in by the window — that is the work of `app`;
- a Go program that issues the token: a second adapter of the sign-in service beside the binary, while `jq`, `openssl` and `curl` are in the container already;
- a host folder — it has to be created before the container starts, otherwise the container will not start;
- the home folder in the container — it vanishes on every rebuild;
- the author's real country — the data of the dev account are synthetic, as its UID is.

— *Consequence:* Д5 and Д6 are closed, Д15 is narrowed to the sign-in by the window through `app`; epic E2 is done. — RUN.md, T29; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.2, 3.11 and 4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T42): the run together with `app`.
- `dev.env` also names `API_PACKAGES_DIR` — the folder into which `just packages` puts the packages of the pinned release —, `API_APP_ORIGIN=http://127.0.0.1:5173` — the origin of `app` on loopback, whose server proxies the paths of `api` (Д177 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`) — and `API_CONSENT_FORMS_DIR`.
- The Firebase dev project has the Web App `app` and `127.0.0.1` among the authorized domains (Д178 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). Their key is the same `API_DEV_WEB_API_KEY`.
- `just dev serve` listens on port 8080, and `app` in its container, attached to the network `novatrail-api` (R19 app), goes to `http://<address>:8080`; the address is `hostname -i` in the container of `api`, while there is no alias (Д31).

This is how the author walked the path of the first app on 2026-11-12.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T52): `just dev-check` checks the platform AI quota on the database itself, without a paid call; the author's decision in planning.
- **The key.** The check's server gets the platform AI key `sk-ant-dev-check-refused`, which Anthropic refuses, and `API_ANTHROPIC_KEY_FILE` from `dev.env` is overridden by an empty value — empty means "not set". The real key does not get into the check.
- **The quota step.**
  - The export `ExportAccount` — before and after.
  - Between them, an attempt on a task that AI reads. This is the engine's choice of a task, or the first unfinished segment of the lesson it gave; a task by tap, or one not admitted, the check skips and asks the engine again, up to 8 times.
  - A submission of a synthetic PNG of one white pixel.
  - The reading reaches Anthropic, which answers 401 before the model, for free. The outcome is `CAUSE_KEY_REFUSED`. There is no record of the spending, and the reserve remains spending (R58).
  - The check: the profile and the account have spent more than before, and the reserve holds as much as before.
- **The flag.** `api reopen` goes before the server: the flag "platform limit", if a run with `app` left it, would answer the reading with "limit" without a call.
- **The logs.** No log has either the key from `dev.env` or the check's key. The log of `serve` has a row `ai_usage` of the submission with the outcome `key_refused`, and none with a model's answer.
- *Why:* this way the reserve and the settlement go the whole path on Neon — the first transaction, the call, and the settlement by a transaction of its own — without a route of its own for the check in the code and without money.
- *Rejected:*
  - steps without a call: the reserve and the settlement are not seen on the database;
  - `dev-check` without edits;
  - substituting the provider's address through `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`: the Anthropic SDK reads it, and the adapter of `tutor` sets no address, but this is a hole, not a route for the check (В58).

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T71; R66): `just dev` runs on the stub of `tutor`. `scripts/dev.sh run` unsets the dev file's key of platform AI, `API_ANTHROPIC_KEY` and `API_ANTHROPIC_KEY_FILE`, and sets `API_TUTOR_STUB=1`, so a run calls no provider. The models are `just dev-ai`, `run-ai`, with the key of the dev file, for a task that checks the words of a model under the cap its text names (rule 4 of RUN.md). `just dev-check` stays on the key Anthropic refuses, as the 2026-11-12 amendment above has it, and unsets the switch.

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T72; R67): `custom_token` and `id_token` take the UID, and `dev-check` gives them `API_DEV_UID` or `dev-author`; `exchange` swaps a custom token on stdin for an ID token. The steps of the legal texts, of the account and its owner's profile, of the migrations with the country, of the packages, of `api reopen` and of the start of the server are functions that `dev-check` and `just dev-app` share: the latter keeps `api` up for the local build of `app` with a custom token of the account `dev-app` in the volume `novatrail-dev-token`. What `dev-check` does is the same, and it passed after the change.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14* (T74; R69): the container of `api` is `api` in the network `novatrail-api`, by `--network-alias=api` of its devcontainer, so `app` goes to `http://api:8080`, not to the address `hostname -i` of the 2026-11-12 amendment above, which a rebuild changes; Д31 is closed.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14* (T75; R70): after the reading of handwriting, `dev-check` switches the storage of the owner's works on to the current storage-consent, puts a synthetic work of the attempt — a history `[]` and the pixel the reading sends —, lists it, reads it back as an adult, finds it in the export, erases it, puts it again and switches the storage off, after which the list is empty: nothing of it is left.

## The `spec` tag: v0.12.0

**R40. `api` stands on `spec` v0.12.0. The tag records `spec`'s answer to the row «в реестр spec» В41 — `api`'s own limit of "waiting" consents until the release of epic E4 —, format 0.4 with the leading question's fields in the contract, Identity Platform in production, the region, the basis and the method of consent, the period of the minimal record of consent, "report a problem" and the fact that the platform has no lawyer. The code of `api` builds on the tag without edits, and the code that reads the module packages waits for a release of `module-kit` on the line of `spec` 0.10 or newer: `module-kit` `v0.2.6` does not build beside `spec` v0.12.0.** Task T30 of epic E3, 2026-11-09. The move is by the author's instruction of the same day, to the latest release instead of v0.11.0, which the task named; one task with the code, the order of E3 and the limit of 10 consents are the author's decisions in the planning of T30.
- **What the tag brings to `api`** (`git -C spec diff v0.9.11 v0.12.0`, read 2026-11-09; Д98, Д108, Д121, Д125, Д127, Д129, Д132, Д135, Д137, Д138, Д139, Д141, Д144, Д145, Д148 and Д150 in table 4 and table 5 of RUN.md of `spec` v0.12.0; the rows 3.8, 3.9, 5.7, 5.8, 6.5, 6.8 and 6.10 and the section «Устарело в RUN.md репозиториев» of `COORDINATION.md` of `spec`):
  - the contract — two fields of the leading question, `GetHintResponse.leading_question` and `OpenAttempt.requested_leading_question` (v0.10.0; R108 spec; the 2026-11-08 amendment to R82 spec). There are no new methods, messages or causes, `account.proto` and `cause.proto` have not changed, and the `go.mod` of the contracts module has the same connect v2.0.2 and protobuf v1.36.12. The code of `api` builds and passes the tests without edits;
  - format 0.4 (v0.10.0; the 2026-11-09 amendment to R44 spec; R111 spec) — the check kind `time`, the leading question, examples and the page of a workbook. The vectors of `spec.Format` are only 0.4, and in the types `formatv0` two fields changed type. Hence the 2026-11-09 amendment to R07;
  - the 2026-11-09 amendment to R82 spec: the leading question in evidence is only a field of `learner-model`, after its release with this field (Д138). Hence the 2026-11-09 amendment to R10;
  - the answer to В41 — the 2026-11-09 amendment to R98 spec: `api`'s own limit of "waiting" consents and the refusal `invalid_argument` without `Refusal` until the release of E4, and with it `CAUSE_TOO_MANY_CONSENTS` (Д144). Hence the 2026-11-09 amendment to R36;
  - R116–R118 spec (Д148): the basis of processing by age, the verifiable parental consent being a signed form whose scan the operator keeps outside the database of `api`, the learner's own consent above the age of digital consent; the minimal record — 5 years, then erasure; "I am 18" with a date; the clock offset is data of the account. П-1, П-6, П-14, П-22 and П-23 are closed. Hence the 2026-11-09 amendments to R21, R36 and R38;
  - R112 spec: the platform has no lawyer — legal questions are decided by the author on Claude's analysis, and the former "a lawyer decides" is read so;
  - R109 spec (Д127): the region — Cloud Run `us-east4`, Neon `aws-us-east-1`; П-11 is closed. Hence the 2026-11-09 amendment to R05;
  - Identity Platform in the platform's production project and the role `signInUsers` of the service account of `api` (architecture 3.11 spec; Д141, Д145). Hence the 2026-11-09 amendment to R03;
  - R113 spec: artifacts on the server only if the owner turned on storage for the profile, off by default; П-8 is closed. This changes Д12, but not its deadline;
  - R123 spec (Д150): "report a problem" at a tutor's answer — `api` takes the adult's report and passes it to the operator, without content of the work beyond what is attached. There is no method in the contract — В43 and Д20 in RUN.md;
  - R115 spec: the operator's texts — the policy, the consents and the operator's name — `api` serves as copies without a token, and the platform serves the texts of the part `legal/` of `spec`. The request for them comes with epic E4 ([the text of the contracts](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15) — Д21 in RUN.md;
  - R114, R119, R121 and R122 spec: development agents get no learners' data (П-12 is closed); the DPIA comes before the first child (П-2 is closed); processor agreements and zero data retention at the AI providers (П-3 is closed); an own key only for `adult` (П-4 is closed), as `api` already does (R04);
  - the admission table 0.2 (R112, R123 spec; Д98): `minor_safeguards` only by the author's decision on the provider's measures; no cell opened or closed, and the dictionary of facts is the same. Hence the 2026-11-09 amendment to R25;
  - the part `deployment/`, format 1 (v0.11.0; the 2026-11-09 amendments to R85 spec): `/deployment.json` is read by `app` and by the deployment of `infra`, and not by `api`. The origin of `app` in the CORS of the platform's deployment is `<project>.web.app`, without `firebaseapp.com` (Д132);
  - the 2026-11-08 amendments to R106 spec — Д125 and Д129: the commands that the deployment runs with the image of `api`, and the admission variables. T35 and T41 take them;
  - `learner-model` and `engine` `v0.1.3` through the gate of `sim` (Д137) — T39 and T40 take them;
  - the template of the kind `go`: gopls from the lock file `.devcontainer/gopls/` by the step `GOPLS_VERSION` in the stage `devcontainer`, and the settings of the Go extension (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R102 spec; Д108, Д139). RULES.md, the container of `spec` and `docs-check-repo` did not change between the tags;
  - amendments to R11, R24, R31, R45, R47, R51, R64, R72, R73, R77, R95, R103, R114 and R115 spec, and with them R110 and R120 spec — on `canvas`, eval, `infra`, `sim`, `tutor`, self-hosting copies and `spec` itself. They do not change the documents of `api`.
- **`module-kit` on the new tag.** There is one Go module `spec` per build, and MVS gives it the tag of `api`, so the library that `api` takes is built with the types of `spec` v0.12.0. `module-kit` `v0.2.6`, the latest release, on `spec` v0.9.9, reads them the old way. A build of an empty module in a temporary folder, 2026-11-09, `go build` of the package `modulepackage`: «module/tasks.go:236:14: cannot use part.Picture (variable of type *formatv0.TaskPicturePath) as *formatv0.PicturePath value in assignment» and «module/tasks.go:290:11: formatv0.ExplanationPart does not satisfy formatv0.StatementPart | formatv0.DemoPart». `observation`, `verdict` and `openview` build, and `module`, `modulepackage`, `moduleindex` and `moduleversion` do not. `tutor` `v0.1.21` with `observation` and `verdict` of `module-kit` builds. So T31 (its part with `module-kit`), T32 and T34, and through them T36–T40, wait for a release of `module-kit` on the line of `spec` 0.10 or newer — row 3.8 of `COORDINATION.md` of `spec`; T33 and T35 do not wait. Row 6.5 of `COORDINATION.md` does not say this — В42 «в реестр spec».
- **What changed here:**
  - `spec/` — at v0.12.0, commit 5b4640a: `gh release verify v0.12.0 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed an immutable release. The Go modules are `v0.12.0` and `contracts/v0.12.0`. The tag is in CLAUDE.md, README.md and RUN.md;
  - `.devcontainer/` — the step of gopls, the lock files `gopls/go.mod` and `gopls/go.sum` and the settings of the Go extension — as a copy of the recipe of the template of the kind `go` at the tag, with the differences of `api` R30 and R39;
  - the code: the limit of "waiting" consents — the constant `waitingConsentsMax` = 10, the count `store.WaitingConsents` and the refusal in `RequestChildConsent`; the step `consent_records` in `api sweep` and `store.DropConsentRecords`; the comments that named the closed П-1, П-2, П-6 and П-14 and the lawyer now name the decisions of `spec` that closed them. The applied migrations are not touched;
  - the 2026-11-09 amendments to R03, R05, R07, R10, R21, R25, R36 and R38;
  - [architecture.md](architecture.md) — the status, sections 1, 2, 3.1–3.3, 3.5, 3.6, 3.8–3.11 and 4: the closed open questions П- by the decisions of `spec`, "lawyer" by R112 spec, the window 0.4 and the build of `module-kit`, the leading question, the calls of Identity Toolkit, the region, the period of the minimal record, "report a problem", the limit of consents;
  - README.md — the sign-in in production and the calls of Identity Toolkit, the CORS origin, the step of `api sweep`, the status of E3;

— *Why now:* the author's instruction of 2026-11-09 — «вышла новая версия v0.12.0». v0.12.0 is the latest release on that day, and immutable. The section «Устарело в RUN.md репозиториев» of `COORDINATION.md` of `spec` names T30: the tag carries `api`'s Д144, Д145, Д148 and Д150. E3 builds the attempt, the hints and the evidence on format 0.4, and the first app is on format 0.4 (the section «Первое приложение» of `COORDINATION.md`).

— *Rejected:*

- staying on the line 0.9 — v0.9.16, where `module-kit` `v0.2.6` builds: the window would be 0.3, while the first app and the modules are on 0.4, and there would be neither the leading question nor the answer to В41;
- the window 0.3 on the line 0.12, until a `module-kit` with 0.4 is out: `spec` keeps one current version of format v0 (R44 spec), and `module-kit` `v0.2.6` does not build on this tag anyway;
- the code of Д144 and R117 spec as a separate task T43, as the executor proposed: the author decided on one task;
- the limit as a number in the configuration: it does not depend on the tariff, and an extra variable would fall on the deployment of `infra`;

— *Consequence:* T31 is split in its planning: the part without `module-kit` can go before row 3.8. `module-kit` and `tutor` on the line 0.10 are their moves, not `api`'s. — RUN.md, T30; CLAUDE.md; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1–4.

## Module packages in code

**R41. The module packages are put into a folder by the binary's command `api packages <folder>`: it takes the embedded `modules.json`, downloads the index of the pinned release of `modules` and checks it by digest, and each package in the window — withdrawn ones too — it downloads as an archive from the release of its record, strictly unpacks in memory, reads by `module-kit` along the chain from the digest, and lays out by digests: the index, the package's three texts under the package digest, a file of the device part once, under its own digest. `serve` at start reads the folder `API_PACKAGES_DIR`, `/packages` by default: the index by digest, the packages' texts by the same chain, and the files of the device parts by the size that the list names; a foreign index or a broken package is a start failure. The index's signature is checked by the CI job "Modules" — `release-verify`, and then `cmd/modules-check` by the rules of R07. `modules` v0.1.4 is pinned, and its window 0.4 is empty.** Task T31 of epic E3, 2026-11-09; pinning v0.1.4 now, one task, and removing В42 are the author's decisions in the planning of T31.
- **What was decided:**
  - `modules.json` in the root is `{"release": "v0.1.4", "index": "sha256:a244c0ba…edb2a"}`, and it is embedded by the package `github.com/altnovatrail/api` in the module's root, `modules.go`. The parsing is strict: two members, a tag `vX.Y.Z` and a digest in lower case (`internal/packages`, `ParseConfig`);
  - `module-kit` v0.3.0 gives `modulepackage.Read`, `moduleindex.Read`, `moduleindex.Newest` and `module.ReadsFormat`, but neither downloads nor unpacks: that is `internal/packages`. The address is `https://github.com/altnovatrail/modules/releases/download/<release>/<file>`, without a token; the timeout of a file is 2 minutes;
  - the folder into which `Lay` lays out is created by `os.Mkdir` with the mode 0755 and a random name beside the target one, not by `MkdirTemp` and `Chmod`: this way the nonroot of the image reads it, and SonarCloud (go:S2612) flagged `Chmod` as permissions to be reviewed;
  - unpacking — only regular files and the folders of `device/`, paths clean and without `..`, only `inventory.json`, `server.json`, `device.json` and `device/…`, no path twice. A link, another type of entry, an extra path is the refusal `ErrArchive`. The limits are: the index 16 MB, the archive 256 MB, the unpacked content 512 MB: a package of the first slice is a few MB, and a wrong file will fill neither the disk nor the memory of the build;
  - the layout is `module-index.json`, `packages/sha256-<hex>/{inventory,server,device}.json` and `files/sha256-<hex>`, as R07 prescribes: an unchanged media file of a new version takes no space. The folder is assembled beside, in a temporary one, and moves into place by renaming as a whole; the command does not touch a non-empty folder. The modes are 0755 for folders and 0444 for files: the image copies the folder with root as owner, and `serve` runs as nonroot;
  - `serve` reads the folder before it opens the databases: the index's digest against the embedded one, the texts of each package of the window by `modulepackage.ReadParts`, and each file that its list names by size under its digest in `files/`. Media files are not taken into memory and are not hashed again at each start: `Lay` put them under the digest after `module-kit` read them along the chain, and the image does not change. The set — the packages' texts and the folder — is put into `rpc.Deps` for T32, and the row `startup` names the release of `modules` and the number of packages;
  - the log — the event `packages_laid` with the release and the numbers of packages and files, and the failure moment `packages`;
  - the `Dockerfile` — the stages `release-packages` and `source-packages` (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R28);
  - the CI job "Modules" — `.github/modules.sh` in the stage `toolchain`, with the submodule and the history of `main`. The recipe `just ci-modules` downloads the index and its signature, calls `release-verify`, then `go run ./cmd/modules-check` against the `modules.json` of `main`, if it is there. The program is outside the release binary: the release build takes only `./cmd/api`. On every pull request and push, without a path filter. A required check is an edit of the ruleset, on the author's confirmation;
  - the first app — the recipe `just packages`, by default into `/home/vscode/.novatrail-dev/packages`, the dev volume outside git, and `serve` with `API_PACKAGES_DIR` of this folder. `just dev-check` lays the packages out again for its run;
  - licenses — five new modules of the binary in `THIRD_PARTY`: `module-kit`, the root `spec`, `jsonschema/v6` (Apache-2.0), `go.starlark.net` (BSD-3-Clause) and `yaml/v4` (Apache-2.0 and MIT, with NOTICE). `go.starlark.net` stands on the pseudo-version that `module-kit` pinned: it is exact, and starlark has no tags.
- **Checked 2026-11-09:** `release-verify modules v0.1.4` — «signed by the shared release.yml of .github v0.1.15»; the signature of v0.1.3 for the index of v0.1.4 is refused — «Source Repository Digest … is 8cf1659…, and modules v0.1.4 is c23ed62…»; a `modules.json` with another digest is refused by `modules-check`. `just ci-modules` prints «no current version» for `novatrail/english-essays`, `novatrail/grade-3-math` and `novatrail/olympiad-math`. The first CI run, pull request #45: the job "Modules" is green, the image's stages laid out v0.1.4 — «packages»: 0, «files»: 0 —, and both images are 58,359,952 bytes. "Modules" became a required check of the ruleset of `main` by the author's instruction of the same day.

— *Why:*
- one command for the image and the first app is what the 2026-11-08 amendment to R07 prescribes: the build stage and the recipe call one code, and `modules.json` is one, embedded;
- `module-kit` does no input or output (R34 module-kit), so the downloading and the unpacking are in `api`, and the check is still its: a package that `api` let through, `module-kit` read along the chain;
- an archive from a release is not a trusted file until the chain has read it, and unpacking into memory by strict rules does not let it write outside the folder;
- `serve` checks the folder at start, not at the first request: an image with the wrong release is a failure of the deployment, not of learners' attempts;
- the CI checks are by a Go program, not by shell: the rule of the current version is the same that `module-kit` gives an attempt (`moduleindex.Newest`), and tests hold it;
- v0.1.4 now is the author's decision: the code and CI are ready before E7 of `modules`, and the 0.4 packages will come in one pull request with `modules.json`.

— *Rejected:*
- a folder per package with a copy of all its files: each version would repeat the media files, while R07 puts them by digest, once;
- `serve` reading the packages whole, `modulepackage.Read` — a finding of the review of T31: each instance would hold in memory all the media files of all versions and would hash them at each cold start;
- loading the packages by `serve` itself at start: the image has no network at the deployment, and each revision would go to GitHub;
- the CI checks in shell and `jq`: the rule of the current version would diverge from what `api` gives an attempt;
- waiting for the release of E7 of `modules` — the proposal "wait for row 3.9": the packages code and CI would stand idle;
- splitting T31 into T31 and T43 — the executor's proposal: the author decided on one task.

— *Consequence:* В42 is removed — `module-kit` v0.3.0 removed the wait for row 3.8. The size of the image with 0.4 packages is measured again when they are pinned (Д8). The job after a deployment is T41. — RUN.md, T31; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 1, 3.3 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T44; R53): `modules` v0.2.5 is pinned, `sha256:cebf23cf…aec6a`. `just ci-modules`, after `modules-check`, lays out its packages by `api packages` and runs the test of the first app's path on them, `TestThePinnedReleaseIsLearnedOn`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T54): the packages are read by `module-kit` v0.3.1. Its Go code is the same as that of v0.3.0, to the byte — checked 2026-11-12 on the Go modules of both versions —, and its `go.mod` names `spec` and `spec/contracts` v0.15.0, which the build of `api` raises to v0.15.1 anyway. The set of the binary's modules did not change, nor did `THIRD_PARTY`. R52 did not take it: v0.3.0 reads the packages. Epic E7 raises it so that the `go.mod` of `api` names the neighbours' latest releases. `just ci-modules` with the release test is green.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T57; R61): `.github/modules.sh` takes the recipe as an argument — `ci-modules` for the job "Modules" and `ci-withdrawals` for the scheduled job "Withdrawals" —, and `cmd/modules-check` got the flag `-latest`, the index of the newest release. The job "Modules" does the same as before.

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T59; sources 4.5): unpacking also refuses a sparse file of PAX — an entry with a record whose key begins with `GNU.sparse.` —, before it reads the entry. A package holds no sparse file, and the reader of `archive/tar` unfolds one into the zeros of its holes: 3,584 bytes of tar became a file of 500 MB. Memory stays bounded by the limit of 512 MB, as before — a regular file of zeros reaches it from about 0.5 MB of gzip too (measurements 5) —; the refusal holds an archive to regular files whose bytes it holds, which `FuzzUnpack` checks, and the old sparse format of GNU, the type `S`, was refused already as another type of entry. The fuzz test `FuzzUnpack` led to it (the 2026-11-13 amendment to R27).

— *Amendment 2026-11-14* (T76; R71): `modules` v0.2.7 is pinned, `sha256:6c46a1a9…2083a`; the test of the pinned release runs its subtest "the board module" in the dev mode, and `ci-modules` still looks for the line of the test's pass.

## Catalog and package in code

**R42. `PackageService`, `ModuleService` and the files of the device parts are answered by the package `internal/catalog`, from the set of packages that `serve` read at start. The open views are built once, at start, and a package whose views do not build is a start failure. A `200` answer of an open view and of a file is cached for a day, a file also by an `ETag` of its digest, a refusal is cached by nobody, and a `503` of a file comes with `Retry-After: 30`. The connected modules are the table `connected_modules`: the profile's ID, the module's ID and the time of connection, without a version.** Task T32 of epic E3, 2026-11-09; the table in T32 and not in T33, and one task, are the author's decisions in the planning of T32.
- **What was decided:**
  - the set of packages, `packages.Set`, on reading the folder also builds the status of each digest of the index — "gives", "no longer gives" or "does not know" (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R07) —, and a map of the files of each package's device part by their paths in `device/`. `Current` gives the package of the module's current version by the function `moduleindex.Newest` of `module-kit`;
  - `catalog.New` builds the open views of the module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps of each package of the window by the functions of `openview` of `module-kit`, and holds them as bytes of JSON with the format version from the module manifest. A part the module does not have, the view does not have either: the answer goes without it. The skill tiers are taken from the canonical view, `skill_tiers`;
  - open views: a digest of the wrong form is `invalid_argument` without `Refusal`; "no longer gives" is `CAUSE_VERSION_NOT_GIVEN`; "does not know" is `CAUSE_PACKAGE_UNKNOWN`. `CheckPackages` names each digest of the request in its order, and a digest of the wrong form refuses the whole request;
  - the catalog is the modules of the index whose current version suits the profile by audience: the profile's age category is in `age_categories` of its module manifest. A module ID by the format's schema is lowercase ASCII, so the order of bytes is the order of UTF-16 units that the contract asks for;
  - `ConnectModule`: the module is not in the index — `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND`; it has no current version, or the version does not suit — `CAUSE_NO_CURRENT_VERSION`; otherwise `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` under the account (`store.WriteUnderAccount`, R21), and a module connected already keeps its time. A repeated connection of a module that is connected already gets no refusal, even if the module now has no current version: the request is repeated by the state of the profile. `DisconnectModule` deletes the row, and a module that is not connected changes nothing. The answer of both is the connected modules, read in the same transaction as the change;
  - each method of `ModuleService` reads the profile by one query — the age category and whether the consent is confirmed (`store.LearnerOf`); a consent not confirmed is `CAUSE_NO_CONSENT`, as `rpc.RequireConsent` refuses;
  - the list of connected modules is in the order of connection, and those connected in one instant by the bytes of the ID (`COLLATE "C"`). A module without a current version for the learner comes with an empty digest;
  - the path of a file is `GET /packages/sha256-<hex>/device/<path>`, on the same mux as Connect, under the same wrapper of CORS. A digest of the wrong form and a path that is not in the part's list are `404`; "no longer gives" is `410`; "does not know" is `503` with `Retry-After: 30`. The file is served by `http.ServeContent` — with HEAD, ranges and `304`; `Content-Type` is by the extension for the four formats of `media/`, `text/plain; charset=utf-8` for `LICENSE` and `NOTICE`, and with it `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. Refusals have no body;
  - caching (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R18): `public, max-age=86400` for `200`, `206` and `304`, and `no-store` for everything else, the errors of `http.ServeContent` — a range outside the file, a file that cannot be read — too. They have no body: the text of such an error is the server's, not the contract's. A `500` is written into the log as an error;
  - the form of a digest and the name `sha256-<hex>` are one for `internal/packages` and `catalog`: `packages.IsDigest`, `packages.Name` and `packages.FromName`, and the rule of the window is one function for the layout and for the status;
  - the log — the event `package_file`: the status, the time and, on a failure, the kind of error, without the address, the digest and the path;
  - the table `connected_modules` of the migration `0004` (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R05); the module ID in it is from 1 to 128 characters, as in the format's schema. The check is `char_length`, not `<> ''`: SonarCloud reads SQL by Oracle's rules, where an empty string is NULL (plsql:NullComparison);
  - the tests are on the packages of the vectors of `spec.Format`, which the helper `internal/packages/packagestest` assembles, `Lay` lays out and `Read` reads, without a network. The second version of a module is the same vector with another version in the module manifest. The helper, like `storetest`, is excluded from Codecov's coverage: its uncovered branches are failures of the tests' environment;
  - `rpc.Deps.Packages`, which R41 left for this task, was not needed and is removed: `catalog` holds the set;
  - the job "Image" checks for both images the answer `503` with `Retry-After: 30` to a file of a digest that no index knows.

— *Why:*
- views at start: there are few of them — four per package —, and a broken view is the same as a broken package, and it is found by a failure of the deployment, not by a refusal to a learner;
- `ETag` is the file's digest: the file under it does not change, and after a day the browser asks again and gets `304` instead of taking the file again;
- `no-store` for refusals: the next request may find the deployment finished, and a withdrawn version is a refusal that must reach `app` at once;
- 30 s: a deployment of a Cloud Run revision takes minutes, and `app` will ask again several times without loading `api`;
- `nosniff` and the type by the extension: the format holds the signature of a file by its extension ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10), and `api`, on its own origin, does not let the browser read a media file as a page;
- a log row for a file: Connect's interceptor sees only methods, and without a row of their own the requests for files would leave no trace.

— *Rejected:*
- building a view on each request: the same work at each cold cache, and a failure of a view comes to the learner, not to the deployment;
- `http.FileServer` over the folder: it would serve paths that are not in the part's list, and a listing of the folder;
- holding media files in memory: each instance would hold all the files of all versions (R41);
- `immutable` and a year: R18 named a day — a withdrawn version does not live in the browser's cache longer than the withdrawal takes;
- a limit on the number of digests of `CheckPackages`: the contract does not name one, and `app` would not know how to split the request. The whole server has no limit of message size — that is Д22 in table 4 of RUN.md, for T36;
- splitting T32 into T32 and T43 — the executor's proposal: the author decided on one task.

— *Consequence:* T33 does not create the table of connected modules. Д14 is narrowed: the files and the withdrawn digests are done. — RUN.md, T32; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.3 and 3.9.

## The database of E3 in code

**R43. Attempts, lesson runs, the log of evidence, the cache of the skill state and the engine choices are tables of the migration `0005`, as a skeleton: keys, links to the profile, the constraints "one open" and the fields of the row's life. The fields whose meaning the code decides — the verdicts, the ladder, the words, the observation, the rubric, the fields of the choice — will be added by their own migrations by the tasks of E3 that write them. One open attempt per profile and one open lesson run per profile and lesson are held by unique partial indexes, the append-only log by a trigger, and what is abandoned is closed by two new steps of `api sweep`.** Task T33 of epic E3, 2026-11-10; the skeleton and the job's steps in T33 are the author's decisions in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - `engine_choices` — the ID, the profile, the kind — a task or a lesson — and the time of the choice. The rest that a choice stores (R06; R107 spec), T40 will add. An attempt refers only to a choice of a task, and a lesson run only to a choice of a lesson: in each row a generated column of the choice's kind is part of its reference, so one choice does not begin both an attempt and a lesson run;
  - `lesson_runs` — the ID, the profile, the full ID of the lesson, the module and its version, the package digest, the choice that opened the lesson run (`NOT NULL` and unique), the start, the last action, the reason and the time of the end. The reason is a word of the contract (`passed`, `unchecked`, `not_suitable`, `abandoned`), and is set together with the time;
  - `attempts` — the ID, the profile, the package digest, the module and its version, the format version and the version of `module-kit`, the task, the seed and the instance, the start, the last action, the reason and the time of closing. The start is exactly one of three: a choice (unique), a segment of a lesson run with a place from 1, or a previous attempt ("again"). The seed is the `uint64` of `module-kit` (`template.AttemptInstance`), in a `bigint` by the same bits;
  - `evidence` — `(profile_id, seq)` as the primary key, the attempt unique, the digest, the module, the time and the evidence itself as bytes of a type of `learner-model`. `learner-model` and `engine` are not in `go.mod` yet, and their `v0.1.3` stand on `spec` v0.9.16: whether they build beside v0.12.0, T39 and T40 will learn;
  - `skill_states` — a row per profile: the state as bytes, the cache mark — the version of `learner-model`, the release of `modules`, the format window — and the number of the last evidence taken into account, from 0 (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R06);
  - `idempotency_keys` gets the targets of E3, `attempt_id` and `lesson_run_id`, as the migration `0002` promised;
  - each reference between rows of E3 carries the profile — a composite foreign key `(profile_id, …)` —, so a row does not refer to a row of another profile and does not hold up its deletion;
  - the start of an attempt and of a lesson run, and their closing and end once written, do not change — triggers hold this (R10, R13);
  - indexes: the open attempts and lesson runs by the last action — for the job —, the profile's attempts by start, and each reference by which deletion goes has its own: the attempts of a lesson run, the previous attempt and the targets of keys;
  - the job (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R38): `store.CloseAbandonedAttempts` and `store.EndAbandonedRuns` — `UPDATE` in batches by the shared `inBatches`, the former `drop`, with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and the period checked once more in the outer `WHERE`. The time of closing is the time of the pass by which the period is judged, not the database's clock: a pass has one clock;
  - the dev database was aligned 2026-11-10 by the author's instruction: `just dev-check` of T32 applied `0004` before the edit by the finding of SonarCloud, and the former check `module_id <> ''` stood there. In one transaction `ALTER TABLE` replaced it with `char_length(module_id) BETWEEN 1 AND 128`, and the definition was read before and after.

— *Why:*
- a skeleton: the meaning of the fields of the verdict, the ladder and the choice is seen only by their code, and the schema changes only forward, so a wrong field would have to be removed by a migration `-- contract:`. The keys and the rules "one open" are needed by all tasks at once;
- "one open" by an index: two devices that began an attempt in one instant will not get two open ones, whatever the code checked before the write; the second request gets a key violation and reads the open one again;
- a trigger: the append-only log is the rule of R06, which the repeat of a verdict and the recomputation of the model rely on, and the database does not let it be broken, even by an error of the code;
- the job's steps now: the rule is one — until T39 no closing writes evidence —, and T37 and T39 will add the deletion of the words and the evidence to the same step.

— *Rejected:*
- all the fields of the table of section 3.2 at once — the proposal "all fields", the author chose the skeleton;
- "one open" by a check in the code: a race of two devices would give two open attempts;
- the log without a trigger: only the code would hold the append-only rule;
- the job's steps in T34 and T38 — the executor's proposal as the second option, the author chose T33.

— *Consequence:* T34–T40 add their fields by their own migrations. T39 creates the cache row, if there is none, before locking. — RUN.md, T33; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.5, 3.7 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T34; R44): the migration `0006` adds the module and the task to a choice of a task, to an attempt the number of wrong answers and the time when the task's technique was first given on a step, the table `submissions` and the key record's target `submission_id`. The check of the key record is widened: a record "done" holds an answer or a reference to a submission. A widening is not a narrowing, but the rule of R29 sees a removed constraint in it, and the migration is marked `-- contract:` with the reason: the former release writes each record "done" with an answer, and the widened check accepts it.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T38; R48): a choice of a lesson names the module and the local ID of the lesson — `engine_choices.lesson_id` of the migration `0009` —, and the table's check requires both for a choice of a lesson, as `module_id` and `task_id` for a choice of a task. A lesson run still holds the full ID of the lesson.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T39; R49): whether `learner-model` and `engine` build beside `spec` v0.12.0, T39 learned — for `engine` too, by the `go.mod` of its releases: neither library has Go dependencies except `learner-model` for `engine`, and `spec` for them is a submodule of documents. `go.mod` pins `learner-model` v0.1.4; `engine` is T40. The cache row, if there is none, is created by `store.HoldSkillState` before locking, as the consequence above promised.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T40; R50): the rest that a choice stores was added by the migration `0010`, and with it the references to the closing of an attempt and the run end after which the choice is given as the next one — `after_attempt_id` and `after_lesson_run_id`, unique. The migration `0011` widens the choice's kind to "nothing to give", `nothing`, with a mark of the outcome "not admitted". `engine` in `go.mod` is v0.1.4.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T43; R52): the migration `0012` — the fields of the consent contract at `consents` and the tables `consent_forms`, `account_texts`, `work_storage_consents` and `problem_reports`. The check "a profile only for a confirmed consent" is removed and marked `-- contract:`; a consent written by the former release during a deployment gets who gave it, the code and the state of the form by a trigger. The migration `0013` gives the child consents written before the table of ages `applied` 13 — the age by which the former code gave them —, and the same trigger gives it to a consent of the former release: otherwise a learner of 13–17 years would be asked the age again. `0012` is on the dev database by this day already, and it is edited only equivalently, by two reliability findings of SonarCloud on pull request #57: the filling of `giver` got `WHERE giver IS NULL`, which every row meets, and the check of the scan's name is written `char_length(object) >= 1`, as in the former migrations, instead of `object <> ''`, where the analyser reads an empty string as Oracle's NULL. On the dev database the former form of the check stayed, with the same meaning.

## Attempt without AI in code

**R44. The attempt is run by the package `internal/learning`: the beginning by the engine's choice and "again", the open attempt, the submission of a tapped choice with the verdict of `module-kit`, the beginning of a step and "Done". A task that AI would check is refused at the beginning with "not admitted": there is no admission to AI in the code yet. A submission is a database row with its answer, and the key record refers to it. The idempotency keys are run by the shared helper `rpc.Once`. The other methods of `LearningService` and `AttemptService` answer `unimplemented` until their tasks.** Task T34 of epic E3, 2026-11-10; one task and the beginning by a choice and by "again" are the author's decisions in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the beginning**, `BeginAttempt`, by a key. In order:
    - the consent and the form; a provider of an own key at a profile that is not `adult` — "key not for this profile";
    - the choice by the pair "profile, ID": none — "not found", a choice of a lesson — `invalid_argument`, it began an attempt already — "already exists". "Again" — the previous attempt by the pair, open or closed, from a lesson too: the new one goes outside the lesson;
    - the module is not connected — "not found", as the text of the cause `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND` prescribes: «no module connected with the ID named»;
    - there is no current version for the learner, or the task is not in it — "no current version";
    - the task does not suit the age category — "not suitable" (R51 spec): by its own categories, and without them by the module's audience;
    - AI would check the task — an answer slot that is not `choice`, or a rubric — "not admitted";
