    - the open attempt is closed by the same transaction (R10): "left", and an overdue one the way the check on request would close it, "unchecked" or "abandoned";
    - the seed is `crypto/rand`, the template's instance is `template.AttemptInstance` and `template.Instance` from the package's numbers.

    The answer is the ID, the open view of the instance (`openview.Task`, JSON of the format with its version) and the digest. The key record holds it: there is nothing in it that the learner sent;
  - **a lesson's segment** in `BeginAttempt` reads the lesson run by the pair: while there are no lesson runs — "not found", and their attempts are begun by T38;
  - **the check of the attempt** at each request on it. The attempt's row is held `FOR UPDATE`:
    - none — "not found";
    - closed — "attempt closed" with the reason;
    - the package's status is "no longer gives" — close "unchecked" (R07);
    - 7 days without actions — close "abandoned" (R10);
    - the package's status is "does not know" — `CAUSE_PACKAGE_UNKNOWN` without a write.

    A closing by the check stays in the database, and the request gets "attempt closed". For a request with a key the closing is committed by `rpc.Keep`, without the key record: a repeat is checked again and sees the closed attempt;
  - **the open attempt**, `GetOpenAttempt`: the beginning, assembled again from the package and the instance, the verdicts of the last submission, the number of wrong answers and the techniques given on a step. The rung and what is given on request are T37. A reading is not an action: it does not move the last action;
  - **the submission**, `SubmitWork`, by a key:
    - the verdict is `observation.Verdicts` on the chosen options, without an observation; the answer slot's `value` is the text of the chosen one;
    - "not yet" in even one answer slot is a wrong answer;
    - all items correct — the attempt is closed "correct", and the answer carries the solved task: the keys of the instance by the places of the answer slots and the solution as JSON of the format (R05 spec);
    - refusals of form, `invalid_argument`: a task with a rubric, work at a task with only `choice`, no work at a task that AI reads, a tap of the wrong answer slot, out of order or twice;
    - the answer slots that AI reads get "unchecked" with the outcome "not admitted", and the work is read by nobody — a path that does not exist while the beginning does not begin such tasks.

    A submission is a row of `submissions` with the answer — `SubmitWorkResponse` without `next` — and the number in the attempt, and the key record refers to it: the answer carries the chosen options (the table of section 3.2). `next` is empty until T40, and in a lesson until T38;
  - **the beginning of a step**, `BeginStep`: a step of the module's solving steps whose `gives` gives techniques, by place from 1; otherwise `invalid_argument`. The answer is the IDs of the instance's techniques, and the attempt remembers the time when the techniques were first given on a step. A repeat is by the state;
  - **"Done"**: at a task with a verdict — `invalid_argument`. At a task with a rubric — "no submission": there is no submission of an essay in the code yet, as there are no attempts with a rubric themselves;
  - **the keys** — `rpc.Once`, shared by `AccountService` and the attempts: the lock of the account's row, the reading of the record, the refusals "key already used" and "still going", the answer to a repeat. The fingerprint of the beginning and the submission is SHA-256, of the requests of `AccountService` argon2id, as before (R35). Neither the idempotency key nor the learner's own key enters the fingerprint: there is no hash of an own key anywhere (R84 spec). The record carries the targets: the profile, the attempt, the submission;
  - **the consent and the form** — the shared `rpc.Learner`, step 5 of section 3.1 with the profile's age category by one query, and `rpc.Malformed`: the catalog, the attempts and `AccountService` call them;
  - **the list of profiles** names the profile's open attempt — the ID and the segment. Open lesson runs are T38;
  - **the version of `module-kit`** of an attempt comes from the list of the build's modules, `debug.ReadBuildInfo`: the library has no constant of its own. A binary that did not record it begins no attempts;
  - **the tests** take the synthetic module `choices` of the helper `packagestest`: the package vectors of `spec.Format` have no answer slot `choice` at all.

— *Why:*
- "not admitted" before admission: an admission function that has no deployment facts (T35) admits nothing, and the contract names this refusal at the beginning of an attempt on a task that platform AI checks;
- a reference to the submission, and not the answer in the key record: the key record is without content (section 3.2), and the chosen option is the learner's work; the submission stores it as the observation anyway, and the export will take it from there;
- the verdict is recorded, not computed again on a repeat: a new image with another version of `module-kit` would give the repeat another answer;
- "not connected — not found": this is what the text of the contract's cause says, and "no current version" is for a module that is connected;
- a reading is not an action: opening an attempt on another device and leaving is not work on the task, and the period "abandoned" is 7 days without actions;
- `rpc.Keep`: a closing by the check is the truth about the attempt, not part of the request, and a rollback would bring back an open attempt that cannot be worked on. The same holds for the beginning of a new one: it closes an overdue attempt by its period or package, and not "left";
- SHA-256, and not argon2id, for the submission: the chosen option is found from the fingerprint by brute force — an answer slot has two to four options, and a slow hash does not save from this. But what is chosen lies in the submission of the same profile anyway, while the profile lives, and the key record lives no longer than 2 days and goes with the profile: the fingerprint holds nothing beyond what the database holds. The database does not hold the own key, and it does not enter the fingerprint;

— *Rejected:*
- the submission's answer in the key record: the record would hold the learner's choice;
- beginning tasks that AI checks without admission: the learner would get a task that there is nothing to check with;
- the verdict again on a repeat: see "Why";
- the fields of all future tasks now: their code decides their meaning (R43);
- T34 and T43 instead of one task — the executor's proposal as the second option, the author chose one;
- only "again", without the beginning by a choice: the main path of `app` would not be checked until T40.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-10 amendments to R07, R10, R17 and R43. T35 and T36 open the tasks that AI checks and the submission of work; T37 — the rung and what is given on request in the open attempt; T38 — the segment and `next` in a lesson; T39 — the evidence at closing; T40 — `next` outside a lesson and the choice that the engine writes. — RUN.md, T34; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.5, 3.9 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T35; R45): the beginning no longer refuses a handwriting task as a whole — it asks the admission gate. Not admitted — "not admitted", as before; admitted — `unimplemented`, until T36 brings the reading of `tutor`. A task with a rubric is still "not admitted": there is no path of the rubric in the code (R14).

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T36; R46): the submission with work is in the code. The answer slots that AI reads get a verdict by what two readers of `tutor` read, and "unchecked" with an outcome stays only for a call that gave no reading. The submission goes in three parts (`rpc.Across`), not in one transaction, and holds its observation.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T37; R47): the open attempt is complete: to the beginning, the verdicts of the last submission, the number of wrong answers and the techniques of a step were added the current rung with the words that the attempt stores, or its fallback text, "explain differently" on it, the techniques given on request, and the leading question given on request. "Hint" before the first wrong answer is an action on the attempt too, like the beginning of a step: it writes what is given.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T38; R48): a lesson's segment in `BeginAttempt` is in the code, on the lesson run's package, after the full check of the lesson run; an open attempt of a segment is given as it is. An answer that closes a segment's attempt by a correct answer or by showing the solution carries `next` — the next segment or "no more segments" —, and a repeat of the submission adds it to the submission's answer, which is stored without `next`. Outside a lesson `next` is empty until T40.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T39; R49): each closing of an attempt in `internal/learning` — "correct", "solution shown", "left", a closing by the request's check and the run end "not suitable" — appends, by the same transaction, the evidence that the profile's closed attempts owe; "correct" does so after the write of the closing submission. The answers do not change.

## Admission to AI in code

**R45. Admission to AI in code is the package `internal/admission` over `tutor/admission` `v0.1.21`: the admission table of `spec` at the tag of `go.mod`, the facts from the file `API_ADMISSION_FACTS_FILE` and the lowest rung `API_ADMISSION_RUNG`, `author` or `full`. They are read at the start of `serve` and by the command `api check`, which the deployment calls before the migrations; "author" on a deployment is a configuration refusal. The beginning of a handwriting task asks `Table.Select`: not admitted — "not admitted", admitted — `unimplemented` until T36.** Task T35 of epic E3, 2026-11-10; the gate at the beginning of an attempt, the growth of the binary and the refusal of "author" on a deployment are the author's decisions in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the variables** (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R106 spec; R25):
    - `API_ADMISSION_FACTS_FILE` — an absolute path to the facts file, outside the git working tree, as for a secret file (R26); on Cloud Run, a volume of an exact version of a secret. There is no variant without `_FILE`: R106 spec rejected one variable with JSON. No variable — no facts: platform AI is closed, an adult's own key is open;
    - `API_ADMISSION_RUNG` — `author` or `full`; without a value — `full`. Another value is a refusal: `Select` would silently read it as `full`. `author` with `K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is a refusal, with the name of the platform's variable. A deployment is recognized only by the variables of Cloud Run: an own deployment outside it passes `full` by its own rule, as it holds its own facts too (R25);
  - **the file** is read by `tutor`: `ReadFacts` by the table of this release. An empty file or only comments — without facts; an unknown provider or fact, not that YAML, no file — a configuration refusal with the variable's name and the words of `tutor`. `serve` refuses on it as on the variables, before the log and before anything is opened;
  - **the command `api check`** checks the same — the configuration of the log, the facts file and the rung — and serves no requests. The deployment of `infra` calls it as a Cloud Run job with the same image before the migrations; T41 writes its name into the release and section 3.11. Success is one row `admission`, a refusal is a text on stderr and code 1;
  - **the log**: the row `startup` and the command's row `admission` carry the version of the table, `facts_file` — whether a file is given, `rung` and `rung_set` — whether the rung is named. The log carries no providers, facts or paths;
  - **the gate**, `Gate.Handwriting`: `Select` with the capability `handwriting_reading`, the profile's age category and the deployment's rung. The route is platform AI, and with a provider of an own key, `own_key` and only that provider. Admitted is when an entry is chosen all of whose models the table admits. Without facts the table leaves an adult's own key open, but at the rung `full` there is no admitted model yet, so it is closed there too. The gate does not yet narrow the providers of platform AI: which of them have a platform key, the reading of `tutor` will bring (T36), and then the same list will go into `Select` at `api` and at `tutor`, and the submission will ask the gate again;
  - **the beginning of an attempt** (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R44): a handwriting task asks the gate — no — "not admitted", yes — `unimplemented` ("handwriting") until T36. A rubric is "not admitted": there is no path for it in the code;
  - **the dependency** is the Go module `tutor` `v0.1.21`, the latest on 2026-11-09, with Go's proxy and checksum database, as `module-kit`; no release file enters the code (RULES.md, Hard rules). The binary takes only the package `admission`: of the new modules, `tutor` itself, while `jsonschema/v6` and `yaml.v3` were in it already. The binary grew by 1.4 MB (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R26).

— *Why:*
- the gate now, not in T36: "without facts platform AI is closed" is checked by an end-to-end test of the beginning, and T36 changes one branch — `unimplemented` to an attempt;
- the refusal of "author" on a deployment, and not a record in the log: the rung "author" is models checked only on the author's handwriting, and AI would read with them the work of people on whose handwriting they were not checked. The refusal catches this by the job before the migrations, not after;
- the parsing of the file at `tutor`: the file's type and its reading are `tutor`'s (R57 tutor), and `tutor` asks the same table with the same facts before a call — `api` and `tutor` have one answer;
- the fields in the row `startup`, and not a row of their own at start: the operator sees what the revision started with, next to its version, and the row of a start failure stays one.

— *Rejected:*
- an own parsing of the facts by the types `admissionv0`: two readings of one file would diverge, and R25 gives the reading to `tutor`;
- only a record in the log on "author" on a deployment — an option for the author, see "Why";
- the gate only in T36 — an option for the author: T35 would be without an end-to-end check;
- waiting for a `spec` with a separate embedding of `admission/` — an option for the author: the growth is 1.4 MB, and the question is recorded as В44.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-10 amendments to R25, R26 and R44; Д18 is closed — the rung "full" waits for samples of adult volunteers (П-16, П-18), and that is a question of `spec` and `tutor`; В44 «в реестр spec». T36 replaces `unimplemented` with the reading of `tutor`, T41 writes `api check` into the release's commands. — RUN.md, T35; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.9 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T36; R46): the gate also builds `tutor.Tutor` — with the table, the facts, the rung and the platform keys —, and on the route of platform AI takes only providers that have a key, as `tutor.Choose` does. Without a key platform AI is closed even with facts. The branch `unimplemented` is removed: an admitted handwriting task begins, and a task in a form that `tutor` v0.1.21 does not read is "not admitted". The rows `startup` and `admission` also write `platform_keys` — how many platform keys are set, without providers.

## Handwriting check in code

**R46. The submission of a handwriting sheet is `SubmitWork` with a crop: the reading by two readers of `tutor` `v0.1.21` (`ReadSheet`), the join and the verdict of `module-kit` (`observation.Join`, `observation.Verdicts`), the observation at the submission. The request goes in three parts by `rpc.Across`: the checks and the key record "going" in one transaction, the AI call outside transactions, the verdict and the record "done" in the second. The platform AI keys are the secrets `API_ANTHROPIC_KEY` and `API_OPENAI_KEY`. AI spending is written by the row `ai_usage`, and the message limit is 4 MiB.** Task T36 of epic E3, 2026-11-10; one task, "attempt closed" for an attempt closed during the reading, "not admitted" for a task that `tutor` does not read, and a secret per provider are the author's decisions in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the form of the submission:** at a task that AI reads — a crop of the sheet as PNG, whole and no more than 4096 px on a side, as `tutor` reads; no crop, a workbook, not PNG, a broken or larger PNG — `invalid_argument`. If less than 6 s is left until the request's deadline, the submission is `deadline_exceeded` before the write and the call: a reading cut off by the deadline would stay under the key as "AI failure" forever. Taps go with the crop if the task has answer slots of choice. An adult's own key is the route `own_key` with `tutor.NewOwnKey`; it enters neither the fingerprint, nor the database, nor the log. `clock_offset_minutes` is not read: there is no quota (Д11);
  - **three parts** (`rpc.Across`):
    - the first transaction: the check of the attempt, as at each request (R44), the form and the taps, the record "going" with the profile and the attempt;
    - the call of `ReadSheet` outside transactions, until the request's deadline minus 3 s for the write;
    - the second transaction: the attempt is held again — closed by another device — "attempt closed" with the reason, nothing is written, and the record "going" is deleted; otherwise the verdict, the submission with the answer and the observation, and the record "done", by a reference to the submission.

    A repeat while the record "going" is younger than the request timeout (`API_REQUEST_TIMEOUT`, 30 s) is "still going"; older — the request fell, and the repeat goes again. A repeat after "done" reads the submission's answer and does not call AI. A task by tap is one transaction of `rpc.Once`, as in T34: which path, the attempt's task decides, read without a lock — it does not change at an attempt. A request finishes and deletes its own record "going" by the time when it was made, not another one that took its place; the deletion after a refusal goes on its own context, which the request's end does not cut off;
  - **outcome → verdict:** `Answered` — `Join` of the two readings, then `Verdicts` with the taps; two different readings — "unreadable", "ambiguous" (R68 spec). Otherwise the answer slots of AI are "unchecked", and the outcome is:
    - `Failed` → `CAUSE_AI_FAILURE`, as are one reading instead of two and a reading that `Join` did not accept;
    - `NotAdmitted` → `CAUSE_NOT_ADMITTED`;
    - `LimitReached` → `CAUSE_PLATFORM_LIMIT` for platform AI and `CAUSE_LIMIT_REACHED` for an own key;
    - `KeyRefused` → `CAUSE_KEY_REFUSED`.

    A wrong answer is only "not yet" (R09). The outcome is written by the request's row in the field `outcome_cause`;
  - **the observation** at the submission is the column `observation` of the migration `0007`: only the joined reading of the answer slots of AI; the taps are in the answer (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R05). The crop lives only in the request's memory and is dropped after the call (R15);
  - **the beginning of an attempt:** a handwriting task begins only if `tutor` reads its form — the sheet `handwriting_sheet` with one answer slot `anywhere`, the others `choice`; a workbook, `zone` and `sheet` are "not admitted" until the release of `tutor` by row 5.6 of `COORDINATION.md` of `spec` (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R45);
  - **the platform keys** are `API_ANTHROPIC_KEY` and `API_OPENAI_KEY`, secrets of R26 with `_FILE` — on Cloud Run by a volume of a Secret Manager secret, in the first app by the author's file outside the repository (R11). Both are optional: a provider without a key is not a route of platform AI;
  - **the spending log** is a row `ai_usage` per model of a call, next to the request's row: the method, the provider, the model, the tokens of input, output and thinking, the call's outcome and the time (R12); a call that no model answered is one row of the outcome and the time. The log carries neither the crop, nor what was read, nor the text of an error of `tutor`;
  - **the message limit** is 4 MiB (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R33; Д22);
  - **the dependency** is the root package of `tutor` `v0.1.21`; with it the Anthropic and OpenAI SDKs and 10 more modules enter the binary, all under MIT, BSD-3-Clause, ISC or Apache-2.0 — in `THIRD_PARTY`. `release-licenses` takes the license `COPYING` too. The binary grew from 58.1 to 111.0 MB (`-s -w`, `CGO_ENABLED=0`, linux/amd64), most of all by `openai-go`, which the admitted pair of Anthropic models does not call (В45).

— *Why:*
- the AI call outside a transaction: a reading takes seconds, and a transaction would hold the account's row and a connection of the pool, and the account's other requests would wait for it;
- "attempt closed", and not a verdict from memory: an answer with a verdict that is not in the database would diverge from the learner model, and the contract already names this refusal at `SubmitWork`;
- "not admitted" for a form that `tutor` does not read: the learner does not get a task that there is nothing to check with, and the engine's choice (T40) will pass it by;
- a secret per provider: this way a key is a secret of R26, like the others, and each can be revoked separately;
- `Join` and the verdict are at `api`, not at `tutor`: the rule of two readers is carried out by `module-kit` (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R68 spec).

— *Rejected:*
- the AI call inside the transaction of `rpc.Once`;
- a verdict from memory for an attempt closed during the reading — an option for the author;
- "unchecked" for a task that `tutor` does not read — an option for the author: the learner would solve it in vain;
- one file of keys — an option for the author;
- the leak check and the form check of what was read: they belong to the tutor's words, and that is T37.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-10 amendments to R05, R17, R33, R44 and R45; Д14 is narrowed, Д22 is closed; new Д23 and Д24, and В45 «в реестр spec». T37 is the second request, with the tutor's words, and the leak and form checks. — RUN.md, T36; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.5, 3.9 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T45; R54): the platform AI key is only Anthropic's. `api` gives `tutor` one provider adapter, `claude.Dial`, and `API_OPENAI_KEY` and `API_OPENAI_KEY_FILE`, when set, are a configuration refusal. The outcomes "platform limit", "limit reached" and "key refused" are given by `tutor` v0.1.24 in fact, and their handling is the same: of the pair the worst is taken — the limit, then the key, then "AI failure" (R95 `tutor`). There is still no flag "platform AI closed" — it is behind the quota (Д11).

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T71; R66): the port of reading in `internal/learning`, `sheetReader`, takes the instance beside the reading: `ReadSheet(ctx, reading, task)`. The gate's adapter, `gateReader`, drops it, and `tutor` is given the open view alone, as above; only the stub of the dev mode reads the key, to read a right value.

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T73; R68): the line `ai_usage` of each model also carries `model_ms` — how long its call took at `tutor`, the SDK's retries counted in (`Usage.Latency`) —, `retries` and `first_token_ms`, beside the call's `duration_ms` (R107 `tutor`). They are durations and a count, which R09 spec lets in.

## The hint ladder in code

**R47. The hint ladder and showing the solution — `GetHint`, `GetRungWords`, `ExplainDifferently`, `GetCorrectAnswerWords` and `ShowSolution` and the fields of the open attempt. `api` derives the rung and its answer slot from the answers of the submissions, the fallback text by the chain of the format, and the model's words pass `leak` of `module-kit` and `words.Hint` of `tutor`. The words of the rungs are stored by the table `attempt_rungs`, and the closing of the attempt deletes them by a database trigger. `tutor` v0.1.21 admits no models for words, so the learner reads the fallback text.** Task T37 of epic E3, 2026-11-10; one task, the path of the words call in it too, and an answer without words with the outcome "not admitted" for the correct answer words — the author's decisions in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the rung** is `min(wrong answers, 3)`, and before the first wrong answer there is no rung (R09). The rung's answer slot is the first answer slot "not yet" in order in the answer of the submission that gave the rung: the first one whose number of wrong answers reached the rung. The trap of the value in it is given again by `verdict.OfSlot` of `module-kit` — by the value that the answer of the submission holds. The answer slot is stored nowhere (R10);
  - **the fallback text** is by the chain of the [format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 19: on rungs 1–2 the text of the trap value, then the trap advice of its trap, then the rung template of the module manifest; on the third, the simpler case of the answer slot's item, then what the second would give; an answer slot that the code did not find gives the rung template, and the open attempt stays readable;
  - **the checks of words** — on the model's question: `leak.For` on the attempt's instance and `Search.In`, then `words.Hint` with the module's explanation language and the text of the task statement. If they did not pass — the fallback text without an outcome; a call without words — the fallback text with a cause, as for reading (R46);
  - **the request for words** to `tutor` is `WordsRequest`: the key of the rung's answer slot, the paragraphs of the solution, the task's traps with the one found, if the catalog describes it, the names of the techniques, the rung, the language, the model's previous questions of this attempt and the presentation style — by the age category: the profile has nothing else that goes to AI. The crop — only for the words of a rung of a task that AI reads;
  - **"Hint"** (`GetHint`, without a key): before the first wrong answer and for a task with a rubric — the IDs of the techniques and the instance's leading question. The attempt writes the time the techniques were first given and the time the question was first given — each only when the task has it: an empty answer is "nothing to hint". This is an action on the attempt. After a wrong answer — the current rung with the words that the attempt stores, or its fallback text, and the question is empty;
  - **the words of a rung** (`GetRungWords`, a key). Refusals:
    - a task with a rubric and no wrong answer — "no rung";
    - the wrong answer is not the last — "not current" with the number;
    - from the fourth wrong answer — "no more words".

    The work goes only when AI reads the task's answer slot, and it is checked as in `SubmitWork`. The words of a rung that the attempt stores already are its words with the flag "repeat", without a call. Otherwise, while the deployment admits no models for words, — `rpc.Once`: the fallback text, the outcome "not admitted", a row `fallback`. And when it admits them — `rpc.Across`, as reading (R46): the call outside transactions, then the attempt is held again. Closed — "attempt closed", a new wrong answer — "not current", and nothing is written; the words of a rung that another request wrote meanwhile are its words with the flag "repeat": the rung's row is one; The box of the first error is a mark of the answer, only with the model's words;
  - **"explain differently"** (`ExplainDifferently`, a key, without the work). Refusals — "no rung", "not current" and "already asked". From the fourth wrong answer — rung 3 (R09), and the request names any wrong answer from the third to the last: the words that the learner reads came with the third, and there is no new rung. "Already asked" is checked after the call too — a request beside it that got there earlier took the right. Without admission to platform AI — the refusal "not admitted", and the right stays; on an own key — the outcome "not admitted" with the words that the learner reads, and the right stays too. With admission — `rpc.Across`. The words that came, or the fallback text, the learner reads on the rung from then on. If these are exactly the words they were reading — the outcome "no other words". The right is spent once the call has gone;
  - **the correct answer words** (`GetCorrectAnswerWords`, a key). The refusal "no more words" if the attempt was closed not by a correct answer, is open, or the profile began another one. Otherwise — an answer without words with the outcome "not admitted": `tutor` has no such call (В47), there was no call, and nothing is spent. The work is checked by its form and goes nowhere;
  - **showing the solution** (`ShowSolution`, a key). The order of the checks: `can_manage` for an adult action, then "no showing", "ladder not passed" and "an adult is needed" (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R09). Then the closing "solution shown" and the task solved in the answer. A repeat — the same answer from the key's record;
  - **storage:**
    - the columns `attempts.requested_techniques_at` and `leading_question_at`;
    - the table `attempt_rungs` — a row per rung: the source `model` or `fallback`, the words, and for "differently", the time, the source and the words;
    - the trigger `attempts_words_go` — the closing of the attempt, `closed_at` from empty, nulls the words of its rungs in the same transaction, and the number and the source remain (R05);
    - the key's record holds the answer without words, and a repeat adds to it the words that the attempt stores — with the outcome that the request got (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R17);
  - **the log:** a words call writes a line `ai_usage`, as reading does, and the outcome is the field `outcome_cause`. The log carries neither words, nor questions, nor fallback text.

— *Why:*
- the answer slot comes from the answer of the submission, not from a column of its own: the answer holds the verdicts of the answer slots and what was read, so the same `module-kit` gives the same trap, and R10 decided not to store the answer slot;
- a trigger, not a deletion at every place of closing: six paths of code and the job `api sweep` close an attempt, and a missed path would keep the words longer than the attempt (R05);
- the path of the words call in code already now, on stub words: when `tutor` admits models, a move to its release is enough;
- an answer without words with the outcome "not admitted" for the correct answer words: the contract is fulfilled, `app` does not get `unimplemented`, and the right is not spent, as with "explain differently", while there is no call;
- `can_manage` in the handler: the right to showing chooses the kind of action of the request, and the table of methods knows one right per method.

— *Rejected:*
- storing the rung's answer slot or its trap in the submission: R10 does not store it, and the answer of the submission gives it again;
- the words in the key's record: they would outlive the closing of the attempt by the record's day;
- deleting the words at every place of closing — for the reason above;
- the ladder without the words call and the call as a separate task T43, after T127 of `tutor`, — an option for the author;
- `unimplemented` for the correct answer words — an option for the author;
- correct answer words whose right is spent even without a call — an option for the author: a second request would get "no more words", though there were no words at all;
- the presentation style from the profile: `PresentationStyle` carries only voicing, and it does not go to AI.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-10 amendments to R09, R10, R17 and R44; Д17 narrowed; new Д25 and В47 «в реестр spec». T39 takes the techniques and the leading question on request into the evidence separately, and the question only as a field of `learner-model` after its release with it (Д138 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). — RUN.md, T37; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.5, 3.9 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T44; R53): the leading question on request goes into the evidence in its own field `leading_question_on_request` of `learner-model` v0.1.6, kind 2.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T45; R54): the words are admitted. With `tutor` v0.1.24 Opus 5.5 · `high` is admitted for `hint` and `solution_analysis` on the rung `author` (R100 `tutor`), and the gate chooses them the same way as `tutor`: `ChooseCall` for words with the work, `Choose` for a hint, and the model of the analysis is the same as that of the hint. The first real call — 2026-11-12, at the author's with `app`:
- 3 words of a rung — all from the model;
- 3 "explain differently" — two from the model, and one set of words did not pass the leak check or the form check and became the fallback text. Which check they did not pass the log does not write — Д32 in table 4 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md);
- the correct answer words — still an answer without words with the outcome "not admitted": `tutor` has no such call (Д8 `tutor`; Д25).

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T46): the line `request` names the check that rejected the model's words — the field `words_check` from the closed set `empty`, `leak` and `form`: an empty question, a leak by `leak` of `module-kit` — and a task for which the leak check cannot be built, — or the form by `words.Hint` of `tutor`. The field is only on an answer where the words came and became the fallback text, for the words of a rung and for "explain differently"; there are still no words in the log. So Д32 is closed: the cause of the fallback text is visible without a record of the words, which `api` does not keep (R09 spec). *Why:* one "explain differently" of three on the first call became the fallback text, and which check it did not pass nobody knew (T45). *Rejected:* the cause as a column of `attempt_rungs` beside the source — a field of the database about the attempt that only the operator needs; an event of its own in the log instead of a field — the line of the request is one per answer anyway and already carries its outcome. — RUN.md, T46; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5.

## The lesson in code

**R48. The lesson is led by `internal/learning`: `OpenLesson`, `GetLessonRun` and `CompleteLesson`, the segment's attempt in `BeginAttempt` and `next` in the answers that close an attempt. The lesson run is checked at every request on it, and the end that the check found is written before the refusal "run ended". The choice of a lesson names the lesson by a new column, and a segment's attempt closed "unchecked" ends the lesson run by a database trigger.** Task T38 of epic E3, 2026-11-10; a segment that the code will not begin, without a check at opening — the author's decision in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - **opening** (`OpenLesson`, without a key). A choice that is not of a lesson — `invalid_argument`. The lesson run of this choice or an open lesson run of the same lesson — that one, after the check; an ended lesson run of the choice — "run ended". An open lesson run of the lesson from another choice that the check ended is not a refusal: this choice begins its own; Otherwise a new one — on the package of the current version, if the module is connected (otherwise "not found"), it has a current version for the learner with this lesson (otherwise "no current version"), and the lesson and each of its tasks suit the age category (otherwise "not suitable"; R58 spec). Two openings at once — one lesson run: an insert at a savepoint, and on a violation of the uniqueness of the choice's lesson run or of the lesson's open lesson run the request reads the row again;
  - **the check of the lesson run**, at opening, by ID (`GetLessonRun`) and before a segment's attempt. The order:
    1. a package that the image's index does not know — "package unknown", nothing written;
    2. a package that the image no longer gives — the end "unchecked";
    3. 30 days without actions — the end "abandoned";
    4. the module is not connected, there is no current version for the learner, the lesson or one of its tasks does not suit by the lesson run's package — the end "not suitable" together with the closing of its open attempt "not suitable".

    The end is written, and the request gets "run ended" with the reason. A check that passed is an action on the lesson run. "Lesson passed" checks only steps 1–3;
  - **the answer** `LessonRun` — the ID, the lesson's whole open view (`openview.Lesson`) with the format version, the digest, the places of the task segments that have ended and the segment's open attempt with its place. The list of profiles carries the open lesson runs of each profile;
  - **the segment.** The place is a task segment of the lesson, otherwise `invalid_argument`. The segment has ended already, or before it there is a task segment that has not ended, — "segment unavailable" with the places of those that have ended. The open attempt of this segment — that one. An attempt abandoned by its term is closed by the check, and the same request begins a new one. Otherwise an attempt on the lesson run's package, with its ID and the place. Refusals of admission ("not admitted") do not end the lesson run. A segment has ended — the attempt is closed "correct", "solution shown" or "done": the places of the segments that have ended the code reads from the attempts and does not store;
  - **`next`** — in the answers of `SubmitWork` with "correct" and of `ShowSolution`: the place of the next segment of any kind or `segments_ended`. A repeat of a submission adds `next` to the answer of the submission, and the record of the key of showing holds the answer with it;
  - **"lesson passed"** (`CompleteLesson`) — the end "passed" when all task segments have ended, otherwise "segments not ended" with the places. A repeat on a lesson run that ended "passed" — the same answer;
  - **the database** — migration `0009`: `engine_choices.lesson_id` with a check that a choice of a lesson names the module and the lesson; the trigger `attempts_end_run` ends an open lesson run "unchecked" when its attempt was closed "unchecked";
  - **the last action of a lesson run** is the latest of what is written in the lesson run's row and the last action of its attempts. A request on the lesson run writes its own, and an action on an attempt is written at the attempt. The step `abandoned_runs` of the job `api sweep` counts it the same way — and in choosing a batch too, so that a batch of recent ones does not stop the pass, — and the term of 30 days is `store.RunAbandonedAfter`;
  - **the order of locks:** a request on an attempt of a lesson first holds its lesson run (`store.HoldAttemptRun`), as requests on a lesson run do, and the beginning of an attempt holds the lesson run of the profile's open attempt. So the closing of an attempt that ends a lesson run and the end of a lesson run that closes an attempt take the rows in one order;
  - **there is no engine choice** in `RunEnd` until T40: `app`, by the contract, asks for "something else".

— *Why:*
- the end found by the check is written before the refusal: otherwise every request on a withdrawn package would find the same anew, and R13 names the check on request as a path of the end;
- the last action from the attempts, not a write to the lesson run's row from a request on an attempt: an action on an attempt has one source — the attempt itself, — and requests that only read an attempt do not move the lesson run;
- the lesson run before the attempt: the trigger "unchecked" writes the lesson run's row after the attempt, and the requests on a lesson run close its attempt after the lesson run's row, and in different orders two requests could deadlock;
- the trigger "unchecked": an attempt on a withdrawn package is closed by the check of a request, the beginning of another attempt and the deployment job T41, and a missed path would leave the lesson run open on a package that is not there;
- the places of the segments that have ended — from the attempts: they hold the place and the close reason anyway, and a second source could diverge from them;
- `next` is any segment, not only a task segment: after a task the lesson can go on with an explanation, and `app` opens the lesson on it.

— *Rejected:*
- checking at opening that the code can begin each task segment — an option for the author: "not suitable" would also mean "not admitted", and the contract does not name that refusal for opening;
- skipping a segment that the code will not begin — an option for the author: nothing in a lesson is skipped (R58 spec);
- the lesson in the choice as one full ID: the choice of a task names the module and the task separately, and the module package is looked up by the module ID;
- moving the last action of a lesson run from a request on an attempt — for the reason above;
- ending the lesson run "unchecked" only at its next request, without a trigger: R13 ends it in the transaction of the attempt's closing.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-10 amendments to R13, R43 and R44; new Д26; T40 — the engine choice in `RunEnd` and in `next` outside a lesson. — RUN.md, T38; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.7, 3.9 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T40; R50): the engine choice in `RunEnd` is in code, and "there is no engine choice in `RunEnd` until T40" above no longer holds.

## The learner model in code

**R49. The learner model is led by the package `internal/model` over `learner-model` v0.1.4: the graphs of the current versions, the evidence of a closed attempt, the log and the cache with its mark. Evidence is written by the catch-up of owed evidence under a lock of the cache row — at a closing in a request, before the skill map and progress, and by the step `evidence` of the job `api sweep`. `GetSkillMap` and `GetProgress` are in code.** Task T39 of epic E3, 2026-11-10; `learner-model` v0.1.4, the catch-up of owed evidence, an attempt without a package in the image without evidence, and the answer slot `time` as the kind "not stated" — the author's decisions in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the library** is `learner-model` v0.1.4, not v0.1.3, which T39 and row 6.5 of `COORDINATION.md` of `spec` name: its Go code is byte for byte the same as passed the gate `sim` in v0.1.3, and its submodule `spec` is v0.12.0, as that of `api`. The library has no Go dependencies. It names no version of its own in code, and the cache mark takes it from the build — `version.LearnerModel()`, like `ModuleKit()`. `serve` and `api sweep` do not start a build without it;
  - **the graphs** — `learnermodel.NewGraph` of the current version of each module of the index, one for all learners (`packages.Set.Current`; the 2026-11-02 amendment to R07): the bands of the manifest, the skills with an assessment, anchor skills, prerequisites and tier, `retired_skills` and the descriptions of the tasks. They are built at start, and a graph that the library rejects is a start failure. A module without a current version is "no current version", and its evidence waits (R08);
  - **evidence:**
    - the task (`model.Describe`) — the full ID, for a template its own; the band by place and ID; the difficulty; the skills of the task and of the items as full IDs, anchor skills as they are; the key — from the author, for a template from the solver, the tags — from the author (concepts 9.4 spec); for an answer slot — the check kind and the number of options of the choice, and `time` as the kind "not stated" (Д28);
    - the start band — `BandByGrade` by the profile's grade and the bands of the attempt's version;
    - the outcome — by the close reason and the wrong answers ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.5): "correct" without wrong ones — from the first submission, with wrong ones — after hint number `min(wrong ones, 3)`; "solution shown"; "left", "abandoned" and "not suitable" — "left unfinished"; "unchecked". "Done" and an attempt of a task with a rubric give no evidence, nor do "left", "abandoned" and "not suitable" without wrong answers;
    - the items — by the answers of the submissions in order: whether it was submitted, the reasons of the submissions without a verdict before the first verdict, the first verdict and the wrong answers before it, the verdict at the end, the wrong ones before the first "correct";
    - the traps — those that the program found by the value "not yet": `verdict.OfSlot` of the instance's answer slot anew, the skills — from the catalog of the attempt's version, the places of the items;
    - the time of practice — the attempt's last action; the technique at a step and on request; the number of "differently"; "has seen the solution already" — not for a template. There is no leading question until the release of `learner-model` with its field (Д17);
  - **the catch-up** (`Model.Settle`) — under `FOR UPDATE` of the cache row, which it creates if there is none. The mark is the version of `learner-model`, the release of `modules` and the format window. If it matches — the tail of the log after the number counted is appended by `Append`; if older, absent or the cache cannot be read — recomputation from the log by the same step `Append` from the empty state; if newer in at least one part by semver — the state is computed for the answer, and the cache is not written (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R06). Then each attempt that owes evidence, by `closed_at, id`: the evidence → `Append` → a row of the log with the evidence that the append returned. The evidence of a version withdrawn as `wrong_key` goes into the log, but not into the state: the recomputation cuts it off (R63 spec). The debt is the closed attempts on packages that the image holds, without a row of the log. What cannot be read or built stops nothing: a row of the log that cannot be read or that the model rejects goes into the state neither at an append nor at a recomputation, and an attempt whose evidence cannot be built or the model rejects stays in the debt and is not written — a fixed release will build it;
  - **who calls the catch-up:** each closing in `internal/learning`, in the same transaction, and "correct" after the closing submission is written; `GetSkillMap` and `GetProgress` — before reading; the step `evidence` of the job `api sweep` after `abandoned_attempts` — the profiles with a debt in pages, each in a transaction of its own. So `api sweep` reads the packages, as `serve` does, from `API_PACKAGES_DIR`;
  - **the order of locks:** the account, the lesson run, the attempt, the profile's row `FOR KEY SHARE`, the cache row. The profile's row comes before the cache: the deletion of a profile takes it `FOR UPDATE` before its cascade reaches the cache, and the rows of the log refer to it. The job's step takes only the profile and the cache row, and reads the closed attempts without a lock: their closing does not change. The step counts as done a profile about which it wrote at least one piece of evidence;
  - **`GetSkillMap`** — the module is connected (otherwise "not found"), it has a current version for the learner (otherwise "no current version"). A learner's action checks consent, as any learning request does, and an adult action checks the right `can_view`, which the handler checks; an adult's viewing does not require consent. The answer is the digest of the current version; the state of each skill of the graph with the assessment `verdict`: without practice — only the number of pieces of evidence, with practice — the estimate, the uncertainty and the retention at the time of the request, mastered — the band, the cautious probability and the review date or "time to review"; each anchor skill that the graph names as a prerequisite or in `teaches` — with its mastery, the reason and the skills that teach it. The engine's modules for reading are the connected ones that have a current version for the learner;
  - **`GetProgress`** — the profile's attempts, newest first, 50 at a time; the page marker is an opaque base64 of the beginning and the ID of the last attempt, a foreign one — `invalid_argument`. For an attempt — the full ID of the task, the version, the full ID of the lesson, the close reason, the wrong answers, the beginning and the closing; `chosen_at` and `reason_code` — from T40, `essay_submissions` — with the path of a task with a rubric. Repeating traps — on the first page, from the log: a trap of two closed attempts or more by full ID, without the evidence of versions withdrawn as `wrong_key`, with `description` and `trap_advice` of the catalog of the version of the last of them; if its package is not in the image, there is no trap (Д27).

— *Why:*
- the catch-up of owed evidence, not evidence only in the transaction of the closing: an attempt is also closed by the job `api sweep` — in a batch, without packages in memory, — by a trigger and by the previous image during a deployment. One function under one lock gives evidence to each of them in the order of closing, and there is no second path that could diverge from the first;
- `wrong_key` is not appended at an append: otherwise what was appended would diverge from what was recomputed, and the condition of R80 spec holds;
- what cannot be read is skipped: the catch-up goes at each closing and each reading of the model, and one attempt whose evidence cannot be built would otherwise refuse the profile everything — neither finishing a task nor opening the map. The append and the recomputation skip the same, so the cache and the log do not diverge;
- the profile's row before the cache row: the deletion of a profile and the catch-up would otherwise take two rows in different orders and could deadlock;
- the graphs at start: the current version is one for all and changes only with the image (R07), and a graph that the library rejects is better seen at start than at every request;
- the traps from the log: the evidence carries the traps that the program found anyway, and the digest for the filter `wrong_key`, and a second list could diverge from it;
- `time` as the kind "not stated" and without a leading question: the log is append-only, and the library has not yet given a number for the kind `time`, and a guessed number would forever name the wrong kind (Д138 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`).

— *Rejected:*
- evidence strictly in the transaction of the closing on all paths — an option for the author: the job `api sweep` would build it itself, and the closings of the previous image would stay without evidence;
- the task's description and the trap catalog in the attempt at its beginning — an option for the author: evidence would be built without a package too, but the attempt's row would grow for a case that in format v0 happens only with a move of `api` to a new line of `spec`;
- deferring the evidence of attempts with an answer slot `time` until the release of `learner-model` with this kind — an option for the author: such evidence would enter the log out of the order of closing;
- storing the traps found in an attempt apart from the log — for the reason above.
- an error of building evidence is a refusal of the request: for the reason above;
- evidence that the model rejects — into the log without the state: the log is append-only, and a fixed release could no longer rewrite it.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-10 amendments to R06, R10, R43 and R44; new Д27 and Д28; Д14 and Д17 extended. An attempt of a task with a rubric closed "unchecked" stays in the debt until the path of a task with a rubric (R14) removes it from there: in this code it does not begin. T40 takes the state, the graphs and the number of the last evidence for the choice and fills `chosen_at` and `reason_code` in progress; the attempts that the job after a deployment T41 closes on packages that left the image get no evidence (Д27). — RUN.md, T39; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.4, 3.5, 3.9 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T40; R50): `chosen_at` and `reason_code` of `AttemptRecord` of progress are in code: for an attempt outside a lesson — those of the choice of its task, for a segment's attempt — those of the choice that began the lesson run, and only for a choice with a code. An attempt "again" and a choice written before the codes have none.

— *Amendment 2026-11-10* (T41; R51): an attempt that the job after a deployment or its step in `api sweep` closes the catch-up writes in the same transaction as its closing, as with a closing in a request. An attempt on a package outside the window gets no evidence (Д27).

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T44; R53): the model is `learner-model` v0.1.6. An answer slot of the kind `time` goes into it as a kind of its own, not as "not stated", and a leading question on request as a field of evidence of kind 2; the numbers of the model and the kind of state are the same.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T55; R60; the 2026-11-11 amendment to R99 spec; Д210 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`): each anchor skill of the skill map, at any mastery of it, has `teaching_modules`: the modules of the pinned index whose current version suits the profile by audience and names the anchor in `teaches` of its index record, but for those connected to the profile. The order is by the bytes of the ID: the schema of the format writes the module ID in lowercase ASCII, and for ASCII the order of bytes and the order of UTF-16 code units, which the contract asks for, coincide. The list is computed before the request's transaction: the index does not change while `serve` runs. `teaches` is taken from the index record, not from the package's graph: that is how R63 spec names it, and `module-kit` builds the record from the same graph. The current version is by `packages.Set`, as for the catalog (R07), so a withdrawn newest version that teaches the anchor does not offer the module if the current one does not teach it. On the pinned `modules` v0.2.5 the anchors of `olympiad-math` are taught by `grade-3-math`, and its audience is only `under_13`: a child's profile gets it in the field, and the owner profile an empty field (a run on the packages of the release, 2026-11-12).

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T59): a page token of a time before 1970 or after the year 9999 is foreign, `invalid_argument`: an attempt begins by the service's clock, and the contract gives its beginning as a Timestamp of protobuf, which ends with the year 9999. Until T59 a token of a time before 4713 BC went to the database, which refused it, and the answer was `unavailable`. A probe of the database found it while the fuzz test `FuzzCursorOf` was written (the 2026-11-13 amendment to R27).

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T69; the 2026-11-12 amendment to R99 spec; Д228 spec): `AttemptRecord` of progress has `lesson_title` and `skill_titles`. The page reads each attempt's package digest, and both come from that package — of the version the attempt went on, not the current one —, as the traps do; when the image no longer holds it, both are empty. The lesson's title is at an attempt of a run, open or closed, by `lessonOf`. The skills' titles are only at an attempt closed "correct", "solution shown" or "done": the primary skills of its items, each item's own or else the task's, each once, in the order of the items and of their lists, by the titles of the package's graph; a skill the graph does not know has no title. `primarySkills` of the choice is not used: it sorts the skills and adds the task's beside the items' own. Nothing is stored for them: the titles live in the package.

## The engine choice in code

**R50. The choice is led by `internal/learning` over `engine` v0.1.4: "something else" — `ChooseNext`, `next` outside a lesson for "correct" and showing the solution, and `RunEnd` of a lesson run that the request itself ended. The engine's input is the learner model after the catch-up, the engine's modules with the pool and the lessons that the code will begin, the lesson runs, the last closed attempt and the summary of issues. Each choice is written with everything that a repeat needs — migrations `0010` and `0011`, — and "nothing to give" too.** Task T40 of epic E3, 2026-11-10; `engine` v0.1.4 — the author's decision in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the library** is `engine` v0.1.4, not v0.1.3, which T40 and row 6.5 of `COORDINATION.md` of `spec` name: its Go code is byte for byte the same as passed the gate `sim` in v0.1.3, and its submodule `spec` is v0.12.0, as that of `learner-model` in R49. It needs `learner-model` v0.1.0, and the build takes v0.1.4: its exported API did not change. The engine names no version of its own in code, and the record takes it from the build — `version.Engine()`; `serve` does not start a build without it;
  - **the engine's modules** are the connected ones that have a current version for the learner (R07). The graph of each is the same one that the model gets (R49);
  - **the pool** is the tasks of the current version that suit the learner and that `BeginAttempt` would begin: one rule `admitted` for the beginning and for the pool. Not begun are a task with a rubric (R14), a task on a workbook, in a zone and on the whole sheet (Д24), and handwriting without admission to AI (R45). Each task has the traps of the values of its items. The pool is limited to tapped tasks when handwriting is not admitted for the learner (concepts 12 spec);
  - **the lessons** are those that suit the learner whole and each task segment of which the code will begin: the engine does not give a lesson in which the learner would get stuck (Д26);
  - **the records** (R104, R107 spec):
    - of the lesson runs — the lesson, its skills and the end; the lesson's skills on an earlier package are named as the graph of the current version names them (R08), and for a lesson run whose package left the image, the lesson's skills come from the current version: so a lesson passed stays passed;
    - attempts with a rubric closed "Done" — there are none yet (R14);
    - the last closed attempt — the task, whether "left", the lesson of the lesson run, the result on each primary skill;
    - the summary of issues — for a skill, the time of the last issue by the skills that the record of a choice keeps, with the reason code and the result of the skill's last closed attempt; for a module, the number of choices; for a task, when its solution last went to the device;
    - the result on a skill — from the attempt's evidence: the items where the skill is primary, with the first verdict, how many of them are "correct" and whether the solution was shown;
  - **the record of a choice** is a row of `engine_choices`, migration `0010` (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R06). The references `after_attempt_id` and `after_lesson_run_id` name the closing or the end after which the choice was given as the next: a repeat of the request finds the same choice. "Nothing to give" is a row of its own kind `nothing` with a flag whether the answer carried "not admitted", migration `0011`; in the answer its `choice_id` is empty, as the contract requires;
  - **the skill from the map** holds: `next` and `RunEnd` call the engine with the skill of the last choice, if the engine's modules still know it, and `ChooseNext` with the request's skill, and without it returns "My subjects". A skill that none of the engine's modules knows is "not found", and the open attempt stays;
  - **"something else"** (`ChooseNext`, with a key) closes the open attempt "left" — or as the check of the request would close it, — and gives a choice. A repeat by the key — the same answer;
  - **"nothing to give"** carries the outcome "not admitted" only when closed AI left out of the pool a task that the code would begin with it: otherwise there is no outcome, as the contract requires;
  - **a choice that cannot be made** — the model or the engine rejected the input, the task cannot be described — gives an empty choice for `next` and `RunEnd`, and `app` asks for "something else": the answer to a closing is not rejected. A database failure rejects, as always. For `ChooseNext` both are a failure;
  - **a repeat of `next`:** a repeat of `SubmitWork` outside a lesson finds the choice by the closed attempt — and "nothing to give" with its outcome, — and if there was no choice, `next` is empty, as in the first answer; a repeat of `ShowSolution` takes the answer from the key's record;
  - **an end that nobody will get** asks for no choice: `OpenLesson` whose check ended an open lesson run of the lesson from another choice begins its own and does not call the engine.

— *Why:*
- the pool "what the code will begin": otherwise the engine would give a task that the beginning rejects with "not admitted", and the learner would see a refusal after the choice;
- the skills of the issue in the record: the summary is gathered from the records (R104 spec), and it does not need a package that left the image;
- `after_*` in the record of a choice: the answer to a submission is stored without the next (R48), and a repeat must give the same choice without calling the engine again;
- the outcome only for a narrowed pool: the contract requires "not admitted" only when closed AI left the learner without tasks, not when there is nothing to give for another reason;
- an empty choice on a refusal of the engine in the answers to a closing: the learner has already answered, and a refusal would take the verdict from them.

— *Rejected:*
- a pool of all tasks that suit the learner: the engine would give what the beginning rejects;
- the skills of the issue by the package when the summary is gathered: the package may no longer be in the image;
- "nothing to give" without a record: the skill from the map that "something else" without a skill gave to the engine would hold by the earlier record, and a repeat would lose the outcome;
- the choice in a separate package `internal/choice`, as the plan proposed: the rule "can be begun", the selection by category and the admission gate live in `internal/learning`, and a separate package would repeat them.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-10 amendments to R06, R13, R43, R48 and R49; Д26 narrowed, Д14 extended. The time of reading the engine's input is not measured — T42 (Д14). — RUN.md, T40; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.7, 3.9 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T54): the choice is led by `engine` v0.1.8. After v0.1.4 the build took v0.1.6 — the same choices to the bit, faster (R52), — and v0.1.7 and v0.1.8 have the same Go code as v0.1.6, to the byte, and the same golden vectors `testdata/choose` — compared on 2026-11-12 by the Go modules of both versions. Their `go.mod` names `learner-model` v0.1.6 instead of v0.1.0, and the build of `api` takes v0.1.6 anyway (R53). The set of the binary's modules did not change, nor did `THIRD_PARTY`. The tests of `engine` on the versions that the build of `api` chooses, the release test and `dev-check` are green. The engine's first branch — an open lesson run — still gives the lesson even when `api` did not give it among the lessons: this is for `api` to decide, T56 (В59).

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T68; R65): the pool, the lessons and the open runs the engine gets go by the route to AI that the request names — `own_key_provider`, or at `SubmitWork` the provider of `own_key` —, as `BeginAttempt` by the same route would begin: the rule `admitted` takes the provider, and "handwriting not admitted for the learner" above is on that route. Every request that gives a choice — `ChooseNext`, `next`, `RunEnd` — passes its route, and a repeat gives the choice from the record as before. The record of a choice gets no field for it.

## Deployment jobs in code

**R51. The release of `api` names to the deployment of `infra` four commands by the roles of its record `api_jobs`: `api check` — before the migrations, `api migrate` — the migrations of both databases, `api after` — when the new revision has taken all the traffic, and `api sweep` — on a schedule. `api after` closes "unchecked" the open attempts and lesson runs on packages that the image no longer gives, one at a time, in the order of a request, and writes the evidence of those whose package the image holds. The same two steps go in the daily `api sweep` too, before the closing of the abandoned ones.** Task T41 of epic E3, 2026-11-10; the command's name and the steps in `sweep` — the author's decisions in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the deployment commands** (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R106 spec; the second 2026-11-09 amendment to R15 `infra`). The README of the release names them, and `infra` runs them by role, not by name:

    | Role at `infra` | Command | When | What it reads |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `check` | `api check` | before the migrations | the file of facts and the admission rung (R45) |
    | `migrate` | `api migrate` | before the revision takes requests | the direct strings of both databases (R29) |
    | `after` | `api after` | when the revision has taken all the traffic | the pooler string of the database of `api` and the image's packages |
    | — | `api sweep` | on a schedule | what `serve` opens, but for the origin of `app` (R38) |
  - **`api after`** is one pass of two steps, `gone_attempts` and `gone_runs`, with the flag `-for`, as for `sweep`: 9 minutes by default. It reads `API_LOG_LEVEL`, `API_DATABASE_URL` with `_FILE` and `API_PACKAGES_DIR`; it opens neither OpenFGA nor the sign-in service. It reads the packages as `serve` does, and what the image "no longer gives" it judges by their index (R42). The log is a line `swept` per step, with counters; exit 1 if the pass is not clean;
  - **"no longer gives"** — the image's index knows the digest, but the version is withdrawn or outside the window (R07). A digest that the index does not know the job does not touch, as the check on request does not: it is a package of the previous image, and the index is cumulative (R63 spec);
  - **closing one at a time** — each attempt and each lesson run in a transaction of its own, which holds the rows the same way as a request: the account `FOR KEY SHARE` with a check of the mark "being deleted" (R29), the lesson run, then the attempt. The attempt is closed "unchecked", the words of its hints are deleted by the trigger (R47), and its lesson run is ended by the trigger `attempts_end_run` (R48). A lesson run without an open attempt is ended "unchecked" by itself, and an open attempt that the first step did not reach is closed with it. The job holds the attempt and reads it again before closing: the daily job closes the abandoned ones in a batch without holding their lesson runs, and an attempt that it closed "abandoned" while the job held the lesson run stays "abandoned", and the lesson run is ended "unchecked" by itself. What a request closed while the job was choosing, and an account that a deletion marked, are neither work nor a failure;
  - **the evidence** is written by the catch-up `model.Settle` in the same transaction, as with a closing in a request (R49). An attempt on a package outside the window does not get it: the package is no longer in the image (Д27);
  - **an end of a lesson run that the job found** asks for no engine choice: nobody will get it, as with the end of a lesson run of another choice at `OpenLesson` (R50);
  - **in `api sweep`** the same steps go after the deletion of profiles by period and before `abandoned_attempts`: an attempt that neither the job after the deployment nor a request reached the pass will close "unchecked", not "abandoned". The pass now has thirteen steps;
  - **`dev-check`** calls `api after` after stopping the server, on the dev database of Neon.

— *Why:*
- one at a time, in the order of a request: a batch `UPDATE … SKIP LOCKED`, as for abandoned attempts (R43), would hold the attempt before the lesson run that the trigger takes, and a request holds them in the reverse order, and they would deadlock;
- a command of its own, not a flag of `sweep`: the job needs one pooler string, and `sweep` also opens OpenFGA and the sign-in service — the job of `infra` would have to be given their secrets;
- the steps in `sweep`: [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3, names the daily job a safety net, and without the steps the safety net would close such attempts "abandoned" — another outcome in the evidence (concepts 11 spec).

— *Rejected:*
- a batch `UPDATE … SKIP LOCKED` — an order of locks reverse to that of a request;
- a flag `api sweep -after` — the job needs neither the rights nor the sign-in service;
- closing only by the job after the deployment, without the steps of `sweep`: a failed job would leave the attempts until the check on request, and otherwise until "abandoned" after 7 days;
- closing also the attempts on a digest that the image's index does not know: the previous image during a deployment would close the attempts of the new one (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R07).

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-10 amendments to R07, R28, R38 and R49. In Д8 the job after the deployment is done, and Д27 names it as a fact. There is no row «в реестр spec»: the job `after` in `api_jobs` is written by T39 of `infra` by the README of the release. — RUN.md, T41; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.3 and 3.11.

## The `spec` tag: v0.15.0

**R52. `api` moves to `spec` v0.15.0 — the latest tag as of 2026-11-11 — and takes everything that the tag brings it in one task: the legal texts `LegalService.GetLegalTexts` and their versions in the requests of consent, the consent contract of epic E4 with the operator's confirmation of the form, the problem report, the category by the country's age of digital consent and the export `api.zip`. The submodule and the Go modules `spec` and `spec/contracts` are at `v0.15.0`, `engine` at `v0.1.6`.** Task T43 of epic E3, 2026-11-11; the scope as one task, the port of the storage of scans with two adapters, the folder of texts of a copy by rules in Go and the export in this task were decided by the author in planning.
- **What the tag brought** (rows Д144–Д203 of table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`; R64, R113, R115–R117, R123, R130–R141 spec):
  - the contract only adds: `LegalService`, the methods `SendConsentForm`, `CancelChildConsent`, `GiveOwnConsent`, `WithdrawOwnConsent`, `RestateAge`, `SetWorkStorage` and `AttemptService.ReportProblem`, the consent fields of `Profile` and `ChildConsent`, `accepted_texts` of `CreateAccount`, `parent_consent_text` of `RequestChildConsent`, the action of `ExportProfile` and the causes 39–42. The handlers embed `Unimplemented…Handler`, so the code built without them too;
  - the parts `legal/`, `consent-age/` and `export/` are in the Go module `spec` as `spec.Legal`, `spec.ConsentAge` and `spec.Export`, each with its own `embed.FS` (the answer to В44, Д176), with the types `legalv2`, `consentagev1` and `exportv1`;
  - the export is required, and a text in force that the request did not name is `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT`;
  - the devcontainer of the template did not change: `--network-alias` will come with T115 of `spec` (Д195).
- **What was done:**
  - **the table of rules** — the rows of the new methods; `GetLegalTexts` is open, as the open views are. The adult and the learner for the export of a profile and the storage of works are one row `can_learn`, and the handler itself asks for `can_manage` for an adult action;
  - **the texts** — the package `internal/legal`: the part is read by the rules of its text — [legal/README.md](../spec/legal/README.md), section 5: the schema, `legal.files`, `legal.versions`, `legal.set-texts`, `legal.markdown`, and with the part of the previous release also `legal.in-force-unchanged`, — on `goldmark` with GFM and `santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema`, and it passes all the vectors of `legal/vectors/reading/`. The names in the refusals of the rules match the vectors; for the step of the schema only the step itself is compared: the paths of the errors of its own schema library need not match the paths of ajv. The platform has the texts of the tag, a copy has the folder `API_LEGAL_DIR`, which `serve` and `api check` read at start, and a broken one is a failure;
  - **the versions in requests** — one check: the request names the current version of each of its texts in force and no other one. The record of consent stores the version, and the account the versions of the policy and the terms, the table `account_texts`;
  - **the category** — the package `internal/consentage` by the [text of the part `consent-age/`](../spec/consent-age/README.md), section 6, on the vectors of `consent-age/vectors/category/`: the category, the day and `applied`, and "ask again" — when the country's age today is above `applied`. The country code is one of the 249 codes of the schema; the EU, the EEA and the United Kingdom are still closed by the list of countries (Д165);
  - **the consent contract:**
    - below the country's age — a parent's consent with a request code unique among those waiting;
    - from that age — the learner's own consent: their profile is created at once and waits for `GiveOwnConsent`;
    - `RestateAge` recomputes the category and, if the consent must be given by someone else, puts the profile to wait for a new one — by a new row of consent. The earlier confirmed one stays a minimal record, and its scan for its own period;
    - `CAUSE_AGE_NEEDED` — for learning requests (`rpc.Learner`);
    - the limit of those waiting is `CAUSE_TOO_MANY_CONSENTS`, and only consents without a profile are counted;
  - **the scan of the form** — the port `internal/forms` with two adapters: a Cloud Storage bucket through its JSON API with the token of the service account — only on a deployment, `API_CONSENT_FORMS_BUCKET` and `API_CONSENT_FORMS_PREFIX`, — and a folder outside git, `API_CONSENT_FORMS_DIR`. Without them `SendConsentForm` is `unavailable`. The table `consent_forms` remembers where the scan lies and until which day: for a deleted confirmed consent — 5 years, for a waiting one — today; the daily job erases it, step `consent_forms`. Each sending puts the scan under an object name of its own, and the database writes the row about it before the write to the storage, with the period "today", and binds it to the consent in the request's transaction. So a scan whose transaction did not go through, and one replaced by a new one, are erased by the same job, and the transaction does not wait for the storage; The operator — `api consents list | confirm | reject`;
  - **the problem report** — `ReportProblem` with a key and a period of review of 3 days, the table `problem_reports`, whose references to the account, the profile and the attempt become empty at deletion; the operator — `api reports list | review`, and the step `problem_reports` deletes those reviewed a year ago;
  - **the storage of works** — `SetWorkStorage` and a record of each turning on and off; a teenager over an adult — a record with it (Д203). There are no copies of works on the server yet (Д12), there is nothing to erase;
  - **the export** — the package `internal/export`, `api.zip` by the part `export/`, checked by its schemas in tests:
    - evidence, the state and observations — protobuf-JSON with the field names of `.proto`;
    - the descriptors of `learner-model` — an image of `buf build` from the `.proto` file of its release, which `release-verify` accepted. The recipe `just learner-model-proto` puts them in place, and the CI job "Modules" compares them;
    - the export reads the database in one read-only snapshot, and the rungs and submissions of the profile's attempts by two queries;
    - what the package tells an attempt — traps, techniques, the leading question — is taken while the package is in the image (Д27). The items — from the answers of the submissions; for an item without a verdict "correct" or "not correct" — the verdict of its last reason, for one not submitted — "unchecked";
  - **the message limit** — 11 MiB, for the sake of a scan of up to 10 MiB;
  - **the database** — migrations `0012` and `0013` (the 2026-11-11 amendment to R36); the deletion of an account erases its accepted texts too;
  - **`engine` v0.1.6** — the same choices to the bit, faster (Д196, Д199);
  - **Go 1.27.4** — for the sake of `GO-2026-6859`, which the required check "Go / vuln" found: the second difference of the devcontainer from the container of `spec` (the 2026-11-11 amendment to R30).

— *Why:*
- in one task and everything at once — the author's decision: the first app needs a contract shared with `app`, and a partial move would leave `app` on the answers "not implemented";
- the port of the storage and the folder — on the model of the port of the sign-in service (R32): the code is checked without a bucket, and the real bucket comes with the first deployment of `infra`;
- the rules of `legal/` in Go — the author's decision: a self-hosting copy reads its folder at start, and there is no Node in the image; a divergence from the check of `spec` is caught by the vectors;
- the descriptors from the release's file, not from the Go module of `learner-model`: the export must carry the `.proto` of the version that computed, from its verified file ([text of the part `export/`](../spec/export/README.md), section 9), and a new dependency, a `.proto` compiler, is not needed — the image is assembled by `buf` of the stage `toolchain`.

— *Rejected:*
- the move separately from the new code — the executor proposed it, the author chose one task;
- the scan of the form without a storage, `unimplemented` until the bucket — the consent contract would go without its step;
- the folder of texts of a copy later — its rules will be needed by the first self-hosting, and the author's decision is now;
- a `.proto` compiler at run time — a needless dependency in the binary;
- writing the scan to the storage inside the request's transaction — a failure of the transaction after the write would leave a scan that the database does not know about, and a slow storage would hold the row of consent;
- editing `0012` instead of `0013` — `0012` is already applied on the dev database, and goose will not apply it again.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-11 amendments to R03, R21, R26, R28, R30, R33, R36, R38 and R43; В43, В44, В45, В46 and В47 closed, Д12, Д15, Д20 and Д21 narrowed or closed, new В48, В49 and Д29–Д31. — RUN.md, T43; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.1, 3.2, 3.9, 3.10 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-11* (T44; R53): the `.proto` and the descriptors of the export are `learner-model` v0.1.6, with the field `leading_question_on_request` of the evidence.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14* (T74; R69): the part `legal/` is of version 3, the types `legalv3`, and **the versions in requests** above are those of the day of the request in UTC: the current version of each text, while a next version before its day is `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` too; `GetLegalTexts` gives the next versions in `next_texts`. The rules of reading have `legal.operator` after `legal.markdown`, and a folder it refuses stops the start as any rule does. The export is of version 2, `exportv2`, with `works` empty until T75. The alias that the devcontainer waited for came: `--network-alias=api`, and Д31 is closed.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14* (T75; R70): **the storage of works** above has copies now: while it is on, `PutWork` keeps each work of the profile, and switching it off erases them in its transaction. The export carries them in `works`.

## The packages of format 0.4
