**R53. `modules.json` pins `modules` v0.2.5 — the latest release as of 2026-11-11, on `spec` v0.15.0, — with the index `sha256:cebf23cf…aec6a`: in window 0.4 it has five packages, and the current versions are `novatrail/olympiad-math` 0.3.0, `novatrail/grade-3-math` 0.3.0 and `novatrail/english-essays` 0.2.0. `module-kit` stays v0.3.0, and `learner-model` moves to v0.1.6: an answer slot of the kind `time` goes into the model as a kind of its own, and a leading question on request into evidence of kind 2 as the field `leading_question_on_request`. The path of the first app on the pinned packages is checked by a test of the CI job "Modules".** Task T44 of epic E3, 2026-11-11; `learner-model` in this same task and the release test in "Modules" — the author's decisions in planning.
- **What the release carries** (the index v0.2.5, the archives of the packages in the releases v0.2.0 and v0.2.1 of `modules`, read on 2026-11-11):
  - `olympiad-math` 0.3.0 — the whole port of standalone, not only the first batch: 775 tasks — 122 tapped and 653 on a sheet with one answer slot "anywhere", — 41 lessons, a leading question in 322 tasks, 30 answer slots of the kind `time` and 41 worked examples;
  - `grade-3-math` 0.3.0 — 12 tasks on a workbook, which `tutor` v0.1.21 does not read (Д24): none of them begins, and the engine does not get 4 lessons;
  - `english-essays` 0.2.0 — 5 tasks with a rubric (R14) and 3 tapped: of 6 lessons the engine gets one;
  - the packages of 0.4 were read by `module-kit` v0.3.0 without changes: `api packages` lays out 5 packages and 1179 files, 17 MB, `packages.Read` takes about a second, `catalog.New` 14 ms.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the pinning** — `modules.json` with the release and the digest of the index, the rules of R07 and R41 are the same;
  - **`learner-model` v0.1.6** — format 0.4 in the model (R28 learner-model): `CHECK_KIND_TIME`, the kind of evidence 2 and field 20; the kind of state is the same 1, the change does not alter the numbers of the model, and v0.1.6 only speeds up `Readout.Task` with the same bits (R29 learner-model). Evidence of kind 1 in the log stays as it is: the field is "not written" for it, and the model reads it as "there was no question". The attempts closed from T39 to T44 where "Hint" gave the question are written the same way: the model counts their answers as data without help. These are attempts of the author's dev database — `api` has no other learners yet, — and the append-only log does not correct them. The cache of the state is recomputed on the change of the version of `learner-model`, as R49 requires;
  - **the question in the evidence** — `store.Owed` takes `leading_question_at` of the attempt, and the evidence writes `LeadingQuestionOnRequest` from it. `api` gives the leading question only on the request "Hint" before the first wrong answer (R47) — that is what the field means;
  - **the kind `time`** — `checkKinds` maps it to `learnermodel.CheckKindTime`; a rubric still has no kind in the model, and there is no evidence with it (О-17);
  - **the export** — the `.proto` and the descriptors of `learner-model` v0.1.6 from the release's file, `just learner-model-proto lay`: the JSON of the evidence carries `leading_question_on_request`;
  - **the release test** — `internal/learning/release_test.go`, `TestThePinnedReleaseIsLearnedOn`. It reads the folder that `API_RELEASE_PACKAGES` names, as `serve` does, and checks that the engine gets each lesson of `olympiad-math` for an adult whose handwriting AI may read, that the answer slot `time` is described by the kind `time`, and the owner's path: the connection `ConnectModule`; "something else" gives a lesson or a task, and what is given begins; the first lesson opens, and its first task segment begins; a tapped task from the engine's pool with a leading question — `GetHint` gives the question, a wrong option gives a wrong answer, `GetHint` after it gives the words of rung 1, the key gives "correct", — and evidence of kind 2 with a question on request and the outcome "after a hint". The test takes the task by its properties from the engine's pool, not by ID. Without the folder it is skipped: `ci-test` goes without network and on vectors. `just ci-modules` after `modules-check` lays out the release with `api packages`, runs this test with `-v` and fails if the line of its pass is not there: a renamed test would otherwise leave the job green. The job "Modules" got 25 minutes instead of 15: the build of the test in the stage `toolchain` goes without a cache. The verdict of the answer slot `time` the test does not check — AI reads it, — it is held by the vectors of `spec.Format` in `module-kit`;
  - **`dev-check`** — instead of "nothing to give": the owner's catalog with `olympiad-math`, `ConnectModule`, `LICENSE` of the device part — `200`, and "something else" gives a lesson or a task.
- **Checked on 2026-11-11:** `release-verify modules v0.2.5` — "signed by the shared release.yml of .github v0.1.15"; `release-verify learner-model v0.1.6` of the `.proto` file — "signed by the shared release.yml of .github v0.1.21"; `just ci-modules` — the three current versions and the release test are green; the test fails if the question does not go into the evidence; `just dev-check` on the dev projects — the engine gave a lesson.

— *Why:*
- v0.2.5 is the latest release, on the same tag of `spec` as `api`. There are no new packages after v0.2.1 — the index v0.2.5 names the archives of v0.2.0 and v0.2.1, — so the image is the same, and the next move has less to catch up;
- `learner-model` in this task — the author's decision: with the whole port in the image the first app gives tasks with a question and `time` at once, and evidence written without the field would stay in the log forever as kind 1 without a question (Д17, Д28);
- the release test in "Modules", not in `ci-test`: it needs network and 17 MB of packages, and "Modules" goes to the release on each pull request anyway. The vectors of `spec.Format` hold the rules, and the release test holds that exactly the pinned packages give the path of the first app.

— *Rejected:*
- `module-kit` v0.3.1 — it differs from v0.3.0 only by `go.mod` on `spec` v0.15.0 (R53 module-kit), and `api` resolves `spec` to v0.15.0 anyway; the task requires raising it only if v0.3.0 does not read the packages;
- `learner-model` as a separate task before T42, as Д17 said — the author decided in T44;
- only `dev-check` and the job "Image" without the release test: they check neither the path of a task with a question nor the evidence, and `dev-check` runs only at the author's;
- choosing the test's task by ID: the next release of `modules` may rename or withdraw it.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-11 amendments to R07, R41, R47, R49 and R52; Д8, Д17 and Д28 closed, Д24 and Д26 extended. The size of the image with packages is 130,402,471 bytes for the release image and the image from the source code, the job "Image" of pull request #58; the image of `main` after T43, without packages, is 115,392,656 bytes, so the packages add 15.0 MB. Д8 is closed. — `modules.json`; `go.mod`, `go.sum`; `internal/store/model.go`; `internal/model/`; `internal/export/`; `internal/learning/release_test.go`; `justfile`, `ci-modules`; `.github/modules.sh`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, the job "Modules"; `scripts/dev.sh`; RUN.md, T44; README.md; CLAUDE.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md).

— *Amendment 2026-11-14* (T76; R71): `modules.json` pins `modules` v0.2.7, `sha256:6c46a1a9…2083a`, where the packages of v0.2.5 stand with one more, `novatrail/check-board` 0.1.0; its current versions are those above and that one. The release test walks the owner's path on the board module too, on the stub of `tutor`.

## `tutor` v0.1.24

**R54. `api` moves to `tutor` v0.1.24 — the latest release as of 2026-11-12, epic E6 of `tutor` on `spec` v0.15.0 — and gives it one provider adapter, Anthropic: `openai-go` leaves the binary, and an OpenAI key that is set is a configuration refusal. The admission gate asks for the same choice that `tutor` makes before a call — `ChooseCall` by the fingerprint of the call's prompt. The tutor's words are admitted and with this release come from the model.** Task T45 of epic E3, 2026-11-12; the move as a separate task, only the Claude adapter and the run with `app` in this task — the author's decisions in planning.
- **What the release carries for `api`** (`tutor` v0.1.22–v0.1.24, read on 2026-11-12; R88–R100 `tutor`):
  - `tutor.Config.Adapters` — the provider adapters are plugged in by the service, and the root of `tutor` does not pull in an SDK: the answer of `spec` to В45, Д185 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec` (R90 `tutor`). A provider key without an adapter is an error of `tutor.New`;
  - the provider's refusals are told apart: a limit with a cause and a refused key, and for a pair the worse outcome (R95 `tutor`). `Kind` is the same, and the parsing of `outcomeCause` knew it already (R46);
  - the words are admitted: Opus 5.5 · `high` for `hint` and `solution_analysis` on the rung `author`, the hint prompt v3 (R100 `tutor`);
  - `Tutor.ChooseCall` — the choice of a record by the fingerprint of the call's prompt, which `tutor` requires calling before an attempt;
  - `Usage.USD` and `Tutor.Price` — the prices of a call for the quota (R94 `tutor`), `Tutor.CheckKey` — the own key check (R96 `tutor`), `ReadWorkbook`, `ReadEssay` and `AssessEssay` (R97–R99 `tutor`). No admission record lets the last three in, and the code of `api` does not need them until the quota (Д11), the own key and the path of a task with a rubric (Д24, Д26);
  - `ReadSheet`, `Words` and the package `words` are the same, and a text longer than 24,000 bytes is a refusal before the call.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the dependency** — `tutor` v0.1.24, whose requirements — `spec` and `contracts` v0.15.0, `module-kit` v0.3.0 — `api` has already;
  - **the adapter** — `admission.Adapters`, only `"anthropic": claude.Dial`. The binary has fewer modules by `openai-go/v3` and `coder/websocket`, and so does `THIRD_PARTY`. The release binary for `linux/amd64` went from 113,029,280 to 79,339,680 bytes (by the flags of `release-files`, 2026-11-12), and Д30 is closed;
  - **the OpenAI key** — `API_OPENAI_KEY` and `API_OPENAI_KEY_FILE` stay declared, but when set they are a refusal that names the variable, not the value: a deployment that still passes the key learns of it at start, instead of holding a key that nobody calls;
  - **the gate** — `Handwriting` asks for `ChooseCall(CallSheet)`, `Words` for `Choose` of the hint, and with the work also `ChooseCall(CallWords)` with the same model, as the words call of `tutor` requires (R59 `tutor`). The gate no longer holds the table, the facts and the rung itself: `tutor` holds them, and the choice of both is one. `tutor` has no kind of call for a hint without the work, so the gate asks for the hint by `Choose` — by the capability's first record. While `hint` has one record, this is the same choice, and a kind of call at `tutor` is asked for by В55 in table 3 of [RUN.md](../RUN.md). The own key in the choice is a stub: the choice reads only the key's provider. And an own key without a provider or without a value the submission and the words methods reject before the gate: the gate would ask for the route of the platform, and the call for that of the own key;
  - **the tests** — the gate admits the words on the rung `author` with an Anthropic key and with an own Anthropic key, and not on the rung `full`, for a child, without a platform key and with an own OpenAI key; an OpenAI key and a configuration that `tutor` rejects are a configuration refusal; the adapter is only Anthropic; an own key without a provider or a value is `invalid_argument`. The tests of `learning` with the way out to Anthropic blocked pass: none of them calls the provider.
- **Checked on 2026-11-12:**
  - `just ci-lint`, `just ci-test` under `-race`, `just ci-vuln` — only the earlier vulnerability in a module that the code does not call, — `just ci-licenses`, `just ci-modules` with the release test, `just dev-check` and `just -f spec/justfile docs-check-repo`;
  - the author's run with `app` on the dev projects — the 2026-11-12 amendments to R19 and R47.

— *Why:*
- the tutor's words are what the first app lacked before row 6.8 of `COORDINATION.md` of `spec`, and the release with them is on the same tag of `spec`;
- only Anthropic: the admission table lets in no OpenAI model, and an adapter that `api` does not give is an SDK that is not in the binary and that need not be checked for licenses and vulnerabilities. A new provider is an adapter and a variable, by an amendment to R46;
- `ChooseCall` is the same choice that the call will make: a record admitted on another prompt will not open a task that the call then rejects.

— *Rejected:*
- both adapters, as before: the configuration is the same, but the binary is a third bigger, and an OpenAI key opens nothing;
- `table.Select` in the gate, as before: it takes the capability's first record, whatever prompt it measured;
- the quota by `Usage.USD`, an own value of `Price` and `CheckKey` in this task — these are the epics of the quota and of the own key (Д11; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.11, "Order", item 3);
- the move at once, without a plan, and "not now" — the author chose a separate task with a plan.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-12 amendments to R19, R46 and R47; Д14 and Д30 closed, Д23 and Д25 narrowed, Д24 and Д26 extended, new В54 and В55 «в реестр spec» and Д32. — RUN.md, T45; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.5, 3.9 and 3.11.

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T71; R66): with `API_TUTOR_STUB=1`, off a deployment alone, the gate gives `tutor` the stub's adapter, `admission.StubAdapters`, instead of `claude.Dial`, and a key `stub` instead of the platform's: the adapter makes the client of Claude's catalog with no key, for its names and prices, and refuses every call. A key of Anthropic beside the switch is a refusal of the configuration.

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T73; R68): on `tutor` v0.1.33 the gate asks the words by their kinds of call: `Words` without the work — "explain differently" and a task a tap checks — is `ChooseCall(CallHint)` (R106 `tutor`), and with the work `ChooseCall(CallWords)`, which admits only with the hint on the analysis's model (R111 `tutor`; R84 spec), so the gate no longer compares the models itself and asks no `Choose`. The kind is `admission.WordsCall`, by which the quota reserves too. В55 has its answer, and Д35 is closed. The adapters of `tutor` take nothing from the environment (R105 `tutor`), and `api` gives `claude.Dial` with no option of its own: Д34 is closed too.

## Quota in code

**R55. The tariffs, their caps per family day, the typical tasks and the reserve by the kind of call are a table of the package `internal/quota`, which a pull request changes; the unit is whole microdollars. There are two tariffs: `closed_slice` — profile $0.50, account $1.50 — and `probe` — $0.08 and $0.10 — for a run where the quota runs out. The account's tariff is changed by the operator's command `api tariff -tariff <tariff> <code>`, which finds the account by its invite code.** Task T47 of epic E6, 2026-11-12; the executor's decisions in planning — the author entrusted the whole epic, and T52 will decide together with them.
- **What was decided:**
  - **where the values are** — in the code of `api`, the package `internal/quota`, not in the deployment's variables: these are values of the product, and R11 has them changed by a pull request of the configuration. The account's tariff knows its caps only from this table, and for a tariff that is not in it, and for an account without a tariff, platform AI is closed (R11). The list of tariffs of an invite code is the same one: `invite` no longer keeps its own;
  - **the unit** — `quota.Micro`, whole microdollars, like `exportv1.QuotaCounter` of the export. The dollars of `tutor` are converted up to a whole microdollar: the quota errs towards spending, not towards a free call. Below zero and not a number is zero;
  - **tariffs:**

    | Tariff | Profile | Account | What for |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `closed_slice` | $0.50 | $1.50 | the closed slice, the code's default (R11) |
    | `probe` | $0.08 | $0.10 | a run with `app` where the author sees the quota run out: enough for a typical task and a few more checks (T52) |
  - **the typical task** — $0.06 with handwriting and $0.06 with a rubric. With handwriting, two checks, words and praise: by the expected prices of `tutor` v0.1.24 that is $0.0093 for a check by a pair and $0.0182 for words (`Tutor.Price`, computed 2026-11-12), $0.055 in all, and the first app measured $0.014 per check (T42). With a rubric, two readings with an assessment, ≈ $0.06 (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R11);
  - **the reserve by kind** — when `Tutor.Price` gives no expected price (В13; R12): a sheet — $0.014 (T42), words — $0.0182, reading an essay and the assessment — $0.015 each, a workbook page — $0.014 per crop. Today `tutor` gives an expected price to the sheet and the words, and not to the workbook, the essay and the rubric (R94 `tutor`);
  - **changing the tariff** — `api tariff -tariff <tariff> <code>`. The code is the only argument, written in any way, as `invite.Read` reads it; one statement finds the account by the code it is bound to. A code that nobody redeemed and an account with the mark "being deleted" are a refusal without a change, and what is not a code is a usage refusal that does not repeat the argument. The output is only the tariff the account now has, and the log writes only a failure with the moment `tariff`: neither the account ID nor the code. The tariff of the code itself does not change: it passes to the account only on redemption.

— *Why:*
- code, not deployment variables: two deployments of one release with different caps would be two platforms that the log is silent about, and ten numbers are too few for a parser of their own and refusals of configuration of their own;
- whole microdollars: a sum of integers comes out the same in any order, and the export writes the counters the same way;
- `probe`: T52 has to show the author the exhaustion within a few checks, and the caps of `closed_slice` run out only after ≈ 16 tasks. Only the operator can give it to an account;
- the code as the argument: an invite code is redeemed and will not give an account a second time, and the operator holds it anyway.

— *Rejected:*
- deployment variables and an embedded YAML file — for the reasons above;
- dollars as a floating-point number: the sum would depend on the order of settlements;
- changing the tariff by the account ID: the ID would leave the platform (R04, R11);
- changing the code's tariff too: it gives nothing anymore, and the record of what the code was issued with is more useful;
- a value of its own for the typical task from `Tutor.Price` on each request: R11 calls it a value of the configuration, and the price of `tutor` changes with its release without a pull request of `api`.

— *Consequence:* Д11 narrowed: the tariffs and caps are in the code, and the counters, the reserve and the flag are not yet — T48–T51. — RUN.md, T47; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T52): `probe` has done its job. A check by a pair cost $0.0072–0.0135, words $0.013–0.0185, so a new task with handwriting closed after two or three checks, and an attempt already begun reached "unchecked" after 8 checks; `closed_slice` after `api tariff` opened it again within the same day (the 2026-11-12 amendment to R59).

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T73; R68): the table of reserves has the kind `CallHint` of `tutor` v0.1.33 — the hint without the work, "explain differently" and the words of a task a tap checks —, $0.0182 as the words: `Tutor.Price` gives it no expected price (R106 `tutor`), and the same prompt and model without the picture costs no more than the words.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14* (T74; R69): the counters of the export are `exportv2.QuotaCounter`, the same unit; the item **the unit** above names `exportv1` of the version before.

**R56. The family day in code is a pure function `quota.Clock` and three fields of the account: the offset, the start and the end of the current day. The offset and the day are written in the first transaction of the request to platform AI, which holds the account's row anyway, not in a transaction of their own. An offset outside −12…+14 hours or not a multiple of a quarter of an hour is skipped, and the day goes by the previous one.** Task T48 of epic E6, 2026-11-12; the executor's decisions in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the day function** — `quota.Clock{Offset, Day}`: `Sent` is the clock after a request with an offset, `Today` the day as of now, without a write. A new offset is stored at once, and takes effect from the next day: the current one ends at its own time. The next day ends at the first midnight by the offset after "now" that is no earlier than 20 hours after the end of the previous one, and begins at the midnight 24 hours before its end — or at the end of the previous one, if that midnight has not come yet or came before it. So a day is from 20 to 44 hours after a change of the offset and exactly 24 without one, and after a break without requests it is the day that holds "now". While there was no offset, the day is UTC. A server clock that went back stays within the stored day;
  - **the start of the day** — the column `accounts.day_starts_at`, migration `0014`: the first day by a new offset may begin at the end of the previous one, not at midnight, and the counters are keyed by the start of the day (R12). Deleting an account erases it together with the offset and the end of the day;
  - **where it is written** — `store.FamilyDay` in the first transaction of `SubmitWork` with handwriting, `GetRungWords`, `ExplainDifferently` and `GetCorrectAnswerWords` on platform AI: there T50 takes the reserve too, and the account's row is already held `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` by the idempotency key (R35). Only what changed is written. A request with an own key writes no offset: it comes only with platform AI (R97 spec). `SubmitEssay` is not in the code yet (Д26). The start of an attempt and "something else" carry no offset and read the day by `store.Today`;
  - **the log** — the offset is in no line (R12).

— *Why:*
- the request's transaction, not one of its own: T50's reserve is needed within the same day, and a transaction of its own would hold the account's row a second time and could diverge from the reserve;
- a column for the start, not deriving it from the end: the start of the first day by a new offset cannot be derived from the end;
- the offset is stored at once: so it is in R293 standalone too, and "now" after a break already belongs to the next day, which goes by the new offset.

— *Rejected:*
- a transaction of its own for writing the offset — for the reason above;
- refusing a request with an offset out of bounds: R11 has it counted by the previous one;
- the start of the day by the account's earliest counter: a day without a reserve has no counter (R12).

— *Consequence:* Д11 narrowed: the family day is in the code, and the counters and the reserve are not yet — T49, T50. — RUN.md, T48; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.6.

**R57. The quota counters are two tables, `account_quota` and `profile_quota`, a row per day with a reserve, and the flag "platform AI closed" is the table `platform_ai` of one row, migration `0015`. The race of two reserves is held by the account's row, which a request under a key holds `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` anyway, and the reserve reads the counters `FOR UPDATE` — against the settlement, which goes in a transaction of its own. The step `quota` of the job `api sweep` deletes the counters a day after the end of their day.** Task T49 of epic E6, 2026-11-12; the executor's decisions in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - **tables** — one per owner: the profile and the account each have their own foreign key with `ON DELETE CASCADE`, the primary key "owner and start of the day" and the end of the day, by which the job deletes them. Spent and in reserve are whole microdollars, no less than zero (R55). A counter is created by the first reserve of the day (R12);
  - **the flag** — the row `platform_ai` with the time when platform AI was closed, empty when it is open; a second row is kept out by the key. `store.ClosePlatformAI` sets it, keeping the time of the first closing, and `store.OpenPlatformAI` clears it;
  - **the reserve** — `store.ReserveQuota` in the transaction that holds the account's row: the account's tariff, the day's counters `FOR UPDATE`, the check of both caps (`quota.Room` — the profile's first), then the addition to the reserve of both by one insert with `ON CONFLICT`. If it did not fit, nothing is written, and the cause is the profile's cap, the account's or "no tariff". `store.QuotaFits` is the same check without a write or a lock;
  - **the settlement** — `store.SettleQuota`, in a transaction of its own: the reserve leaves both counters, and the price of the call is added to what is spent. A counter that no longer exists is not a failure;
  - **the period** — the step `quota` of the job `api sweep`, before `keys`: it deletes the counters whose day ended more than a day ago. The job runs once a day, so a counter lives no longer than 2 days after the end of its day, as R12 requires. T49 says "more than 2 days ago" — that would give up to 3 days, and the step follows R12;
  - **deletion** — the profile's counters go with the profile by the key, the account's in `ClearAccount` together with the account's fields, and with them the start of the day (R56). Deleting a profile does not touch the account's counters (R12);
  - **the export** — `QuotaCounters` of the profile and the account: the start of the day, spent and in reserve, in the order of the days (R52).

— *Why:*
- two tables: each has a simple key and its own cascade, and a table with the kind of owner would keep two partial unique indexes and a check that exactly one of the owners is set;
- the account's row, not a lock of the day of its own: every request under a key holds it anyway (R35), and the counters of a new day do not exist before the first reserve — there is nothing to lock; a conditional `UPDATE` on two counters would need the first rolled back when the second did not fit;
- `FOR UPDATE` of the counters: the settlement does not hold the account's row, and without a lock the reserve could read the counters before the settlement;
- the settlement in a transaction of its own: the call has already been made, and the settlement must not roll back with a refusal of the request's second transaction — an attempt closed by another device.

— *Rejected:*
- one table with the kind of owner — for the reason above;
- `FOR UPDATE` of the day's row and a conditional `UPDATE` — for the reasons above;
- the period «больше 2 суток после конца» from the text of T49 — it diverges from R12.

— *Consequence:* Д11 narrowed: the database has the counters and the flag, and there is no reserve on the calls yet — T50, T51. — RUN.md, T49; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2, 3.6 and 3.10.

**R58. Every call of platform AI — reading a sheet, the words of a rung, "explain differently" — takes a reserve in the first transaction of the request by `Tutor.Price` of the kind of call and is settled right after the call in a transaction of its own by `Usage.USD`, before the second transaction and whatever happens to the answer. Settling the running requests when an instance stops needs no mechanism of its own: those that live to see the stop settle themselves, and those cut off leave the reserve as the spending of their day.** Task T50 of epic E6, 2026-11-12; the executor's decisions in planning.
- **What was decided:**
  - **where the reserve is taken** — the phase `Prepare` of `rpc.Across`, the first transaction, which holds the account's row and writes the key's record "going", — right after the family day (R56) and only when the call will go: platform AI, the words are admitted, the rung has no words yet. The amount is `quota.Reserve`: the expected price of `tutor` through `admission.Gate.Price` or the value of the kind (R55). Reading is `CallSheet`, of both readers together; the words and "differently" are `CallWords`. An own key neither takes nor spends the quota;
  - **if it did not fit** — there is no call, and the request answers as R11 and the contract require:
    - reading a sheet — the AI's slots "unchecked", the outcome `CAUSE_QUOTA` with `next_family_day`, the end of the day, — no wrong answers are added;
    - the words of a rung — the fallback text with the same outcome, and it becomes the rung's words, as on a failure of AI (R47);
    - "explain differently" — the refusal `resource_exhausted` `CAUSE_QUOTA` with `next_family_day`, and the right stays. The refusal is `rpc.Keep`: the day written by the request stays;
    - a tariff without caps — the same, but with the outcome or the refusal `CAUSE_NOT_ADMITTED` and without the next day: such platform AI is closed "by default" (R11), not until tomorrow;
  - **where the settlement is** — the phase `Call`, right after the call: `store.SettleQuota` in a transaction of its own, on a context without the request's cancellation, with a limit of 5 s. The second transaction goes only after it: if it refuses — another device closed the attempt — the settlement stays. A settlement that failed leaves the reserve as the spending of the day, and the request's line names the failure;
  - **the price** (the table of R12) — `cost`: "not admitted" is zero, there was no call; no record at all is the reserve; all records exact is their sum, so for a pair where one reader refused it is the record of the one that answered; a record by the worst case is the reserve, or the sum of the exact ones if it is larger;
  - **the log** — the line `ai_usage` carries the model's price `cost_micro_usd` and the sign `billed`: the worst case goes into the log, as R12 requires. An exhaustion is the line `quota_exhausted` beside the request's line: the method and the cap — `profile`, `account` or `tariff`, without IDs or sums;
  - **an instance stopping** — section 3.6 left it to the code epic. On a stop the server waits for the running requests (R26), and they settle themselves. A request cut off by the stop leaves the reserve as the spending of its day, like a crashed process (R12), and a day after the end of the day the counter goes (R57);
  - **a repeat** by the key after "done" reserves nothing: it answers from the record without reaching `Prepare`.

— *Why:*
- the reserve in `Prepare`: the account's row is already held there, and the reserve goes together with the record "going" — if one rolled back, so did the other;
- the settlement before the second transaction and in its own: the call is paid for even if the answer is refused afterwards, and a rollback of the second transaction must not return money to the quota;
- no mechanism for the stop: the server's waiting on a stop lets the requests finish anyway, and one cut off is the same case as a crashed process, which R12 has already decided.

— *Rejected:*
- the settlement in the second transaction: the refusal "attempt closed" would roll it back, and the reserve would stay as spending while the price is known;
- a phase of settlement in `rpc.Across` itself: only requests of AI need it, and in `Across` `Call` sets them anyway;
- a log of reserves in the database for settling after a crash: R12 has the reserve stay, and the log would add data about attempts with no need;
- a reserve by the worst price — R12 rejected it.

— *Consequence:* Д11 narrowed: the reserve and the settlement on the calls exist, and the quota at the start of a task, in the engine choice and the flag do not yet — T51. — RUN.md, T50; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T73; R68): the words of a rung and "explain differently" reserve by the kind of call of the request they send — `CallWords` when the crop goes, `CallHint` without it, as `tutor` picks it —, and `CallHint` reserves its value from `internal/quota`, since `tutor` expects no price of it (the 2026-11-13 amendment to R55).

**R59. Platform AI is closed to a new task with handwriting — by a quota that is not enough for a typical task, by the flag "platform limit" or by a tariff without caps — by one rule at the start of a task, of a segment and in the engine choice. The flag is set by the outcome "limit reached" of a call on the platform's key and is read on every request; the operator's command `api reopen` opens it. The cause of "nothing to give" is kept by the choice's record, migration `0016`.** Task T51 of epic E6, 2026-11-12; the executor's decisions in planning and by the review.
- **What was decided:**
  - **the start of a task and of a segment** — for a task that AI reads, on platform AI, after admission (R45): the flag is the refusal `resource_exhausted` `CAUSE_PLATFORM_LIMIT`; what is left of the day the account holds being less than a typical task for the profile or the account is `resource_exhausted` `CAUSE_QUOTA` with `next_family_day`; a tariff without caps is `failed_precondition` `CAUSE_NOT_ADMITTED`. The refusal rolls the start back, and the lesson run stays open; an open attempt of the segment is given as it is and waits (R11). The day is by the time of the request, `store.Today`: the start carries no offset. The typical task is one with handwriting: a task with a rubric does not begin here at all (R14), and `quota.TypicalRubric` waits for its path;
  - **the engine choice** — the same rule, `closedTo`: when AI is closed, the pool and the lessons have only tapped tasks, and the choice's record marks the pool as limited (R50). "Nothing to give" with a pool so narrowed carries the outcome "not admitted", "quota" with the time of the next day, or "platform limit". The cause and the time are kept by the new columns `engine_choices.nothing_cause` and `next_family_day`, migration `0016`, and `not_admitted` is written as before — for the release that still serves during a deployment. A choice recorded before `0016` reads as "not admitted";
  - **the flag** — the row `platform_ai` (R57). It is set by the outcome "limit reached" of a call on the platform's key — right after the call, together with the settlement (R58) —, and the first setting is a warning `platform_limit` in the log with the method, without IDs. While it is set, no reserve is taken and there is no call: reading is "unchecked", the words are the fallback text, both with the outcome "platform limit", and "differently" is the refusal `resource_exhausted`. The flag is read on every request that would call platform AI, and at the start and in the choice: one row by the primary key;
  - **the command** — `api reopen`: clears the flag, prints whether it was set, and writes the line `platform_ai_opened` with `was_closed`. The limit at the provider the operator lifts themselves, before the command (Д11);
  - **own key** — neither the quota nor the flag touches it. But `ChooseNext` does not carry the provider of the own key, and the engine choice judges by platform AI, as with admission (R50): an owner with an own key, while the platform's quota is exhausted, will get a pool of tapped tasks — В56 in table 3 of RUN.md, «в реестр spec»;
  - **by the review** (`/code-review high` of the epic, one round, 2026-11-12):
    - the reserve for reading is only on the path of `rpc.Across`: on `rpc.Once` nobody would settle it;
    - the order of the counters' locks — the profile, then the account — in the reserve, the settlement and the deletion of an account, which deletes the profiles before the account's counters: otherwise the settlement and the deletion could wait for each other;
    - a failure of the settlement and a failure of setting the flag the request's line names together;
    - the flag is read by one function `platformLimit` both in the reserve and at the start and in the choice;
    - the day of the start and of the choice is by their time of the request, not by a second reading of the clock;
    - not fixed: "not admitted" is the only outcome without a call that `tutor` tells apart from a call without records, so a failure without records is counted as the reserve, as R12 requires for "there is no message at all"; the two readings of the account's row per reserve — the tariff and the day — are left: they are in one transaction, which already holds the row.

— *Why:*
- one rule for three causes — so R11 requires: a learner does not begin a task that nobody can read today;
- the flag is read, not cached: there are several Cloud Run instances, and a cache would keep closed an AI that the operator opened, or open one that another instance closed;
- a warning, not information: it is a signal to the operator that platform AI is closed to everyone;
- the cause in the choice's record: a repeat of `SubmitWork` takes `next` from the record (R50), and without the cause the outcome "quota" would become "not admitted".

— *Rejected:*
- a cache of the flag in the process for a minute — for the reason above;
- the quota at the start of a tapped task — the code checks it without AI (architecture 4.2 spec);
- refusing the start for an essay with `quota.TypicalRubric` now — a task with a rubric does not begin here at all;
- the cause of "nothing to give" only in the answer — a repeat would lose it.

— *Consequence:* Д23 closed: the flag and the command that opens it are in the code. Д11 narrowed to the run T52 with `app` and the spending limit of the workspace at the provider, which the author sets (rule 4). A new В56 «в реестр spec». T52 checks the quota on the dev project with `app`. — RUN.md, T51; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T52): the run with `app` on the dev project, the tariff `probe`; the author walked the path up to the change of tariff in a browser, and after midnight, by their decision, the executor did, by requests of `app` with the token of the author's user.
- **The first family day** — by the offset that `app` sent with the first request, −6 h: from the author's midnight to midnight (R56).
- **Exhaustion.** 4 checks and 2 calls of words — $0.0683 for the profile. The reserve for the next words, $0.0182, no longer fit: the words of the rung came as the fallback text with the outcome "quota", and "explain differently" as the refusal `resource_exhausted` `CAUSE_QUOTA` with the time of the next day.
- **The next day** began at the author's midnight. The checks went again, and a new task with handwriting — "again" and a lesson's segment — got the refusal "quota" as soon as what was spent exceeded $0.02. The attempt already begun went on to $0.0763; the next submission came "unchecked" with the outcome "quota", and no wrong answers were added.
- **Changing the tariff.** After `api tariff -tariff closed_slice` a new task with handwriting began within the same day, and the check gave "correct".
- **The run's cost** — $0.1518: 15 checks, 1 rung's words and 1 "explain differently", by Anthropic's prices, checked 2026-11-12. The export's counters — $0.068326 and $0.083508 — match the sum of `cost_micro_usd` of the log to the microdollar, and the reserve is zero.
- **The log** — every line is JSON, without the UID, email, keys, token and connection strings; the lines `quota_exhausted` carry the cap `profile`.
- **What was found:** with AI closed, "What next" gives again and again the lesson of the open run whose segment with handwriting the start rejects: the engine's first branch gives it, although the lesson is not among those that `api` gave. The learner never gets to the tapped tasks — В59 in table 3 of RUN.md. The outcome "quota" on the words of a rung `app` does not show — В60.

— *Amendment 2026-11-12* (T56; R50): an open lesson run whose next task segment the code would not begin now does not go to the engine. В59 was decided by the author when epic E7 was set up — option (a).
- **The rule.** The next task segment is the first task segment of the lesson that has not ended, on the run's package. Whether the code would begin it is judged by the same rule as the pool and the lessons: the task suits the learner, AI is admitted to read it, and with AI closed by the quota or the flag, only a tapped task. If it would not, the run is not in the engine's input, and its lesson is not among the module's lessons. The lesson is removed too: otherwise a run on an earlier package, whose lesson in the current version begins whole, would come back as a new lesson — `OpenLesson` gives the open run of a lesson (R13). The cost of this rare case: a removed lesson of the author's order, while the run waits, does not hold back the lessons that come after it — the engine reads the order by the lessons it got.
- **A package the image no longer gives** does not wait: such a run goes to the engine as before, and an open one ends "not checked" (R13). Its segments ended on the earlier package, and judging them by the current version would mean taking the wrong segment.
- **It waits, it does not end.** The run stays open, and the period of 30 days runs as before. When AI opens, the next choice gives it by the engine's first branch. A run whose task segments have all ended waits only for "lesson passed" and always goes to the engine.
- **"Nothing to give"** carries an outcome also when a run is waiting whose segment is rejected only by AI: by the quota, the flag or admission. A segment with a rubric or on a workbook gives no outcome, as in the pool.
- **The engine's input.** `engine` v0.1.8 accepts a last attempt of a segment whose lesson is not named among the open runs. `Last.Lesson` is read only by the first branch, comparing it with the runs, and the checks of `Choose` — `checkRuns`, `checkResults` and the others from its comment — do not link `Last` with `Runs` (by the module's code, checked 2026-11-12). This needs no new field of the choice's record: which run waited follows from `pool_limited` of the record, the run's package and the attempts of its segments closed before `chosen_at` — by `chosen_at` a repeat restores anyway the runs and the closed attempts that the choice saw (`exportv1`). There is no code of the repeat in `api`, and `store.EndedSegments` reads the segments as of the request: a repeat has to limit them by the time of the choice.
- **An open attempt of a segment** does not change the rule: "something else" first closes it "left", and `app` finds the open attempt with the profile.
- **Rejected:**
  - giving the run to the engine as ended, "abandoned" with a made-up time: the engine would read an end that did not happen, and the choice's record would not match the database on a repeat;
  - a change of `engine` where the first branch would give only a lesson from those that `api` gave, and "nothing" — the executor proposed them when the epic was set up, and the author chose (a).
- **Code and tests.** `history` in `internal/learning/choose.go` (`waits`, `nextTask`, `withoutLesson`); `store.Run` has the run's ID. The test — the quota ran out in the middle of a lesson: "What next" gives a task that begins, or "nothing to give" with an outcome. A new day or a cleared flag — the same run again. All segments ended — the lesson and "lesson passed" with AI closed. For a child without admission — not the lesson, and the run on a removed package goes to the engine and ends "not checked".

— *Consequence:* В59 decided. Д26 narrowed: a run with a segment that the code will not begin no longer holds the learner, but waits. — RUN.md, T56; README.md; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.6 and 3.7.

— *Amendment 2026-11-13* (T68; R65): the item **own key** above no longer holds. Every request that gives an engine's choice names the route of the own key, and the choice judges by it: on the own key neither the quota nor the flag narrows the pool or keeps a run waiting, only the admission of its provider does, and "nothing to give" there carries "not admitted" alone. The same rule `closedTo` holds for the choice on platform AI. В56 is answered by the contract of T143 spec and this code.

— *Amendment 2026-11-14* (T74; R69): the export the item **the engine's input** names is of version 2, `exportv2`; what a repeat restores from it is the same.

## The `spec` tag: v0.15.1

**R60. `api` moves to `spec` v0.15.1, the latest tag as of 2026-11-12. The submodule, the Go modules `spec` and `spec/contracts` and the lock file of gopls are at the tag, and `.claude/settings.json` and «Как пользоваться» of RUN.md take the rule "one task is one session" together with the template's keys: the session hook of `spec`, compaction at 400 thousand tokens, and the claude.ai connectors turned off.** Task T53 of epic E7, 2026-11-12; the keys of `.claude/settings.json` — all three, and now — were decided by the author in planning.
- **What the tag brings `api`** (`gh api repos/altnovatrail/spec/compare/v0.15.0...v0.15.1`, read 2026-11-12; `gh release verify v0.15.1` — the release is verified):
  - Go 1.27.4 in the stage `toolchain` of the template — the answer to В49 (the 2026-11-11 amendment to R64 spec): the Go of the devcontainer of `api` now comes from the container of `spec` (the 2026-11-12 amendment to R30), and the template's kind `go` has `go 1.27.4` in `gopls/go.mod`;
  - the answer to В48 — Д163, Д184 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec` and architecture 3.11 spec: the service and the job `api sweep` get `API_CONSENT_FORMS_BUCKET` and `API_CONSENT_FORMS_PREFIX`, and their service accounts the right to create and erase objects under the prefix, without reading; the folder of the copy's texts — `API_LEGAL_DIR` — is given by the copy's `deploy/`. `infra` does this, and `api` changes nothing;
  - the contract `contracts/v0.15.1` only adds: the field `teaching_modules` of `AnchorSkillState` (the 2026-11-11 amendment to R99 spec; Д210 in table 4 of RUN.md of `spec`). A module that teaches an anchor skill is offered by `api` on the skill map, not by the engine choice (concepts 4 spec). Until T55 the field is empty;
  - RULES.md, How we work — one task is one session: the inbox triage, the planning and the execution of a task are one session, the next is a new session or `/clear`, and the hook `UserPromptSubmit` from `spec/tools/session/` stops a prompt about another task (R143 spec). The template of RUN.md got a paragraph about it, and the template of `.claude/settings.json` the hook, `autoCompactWindow: 400000` and `disableClaudeAiConnectors: true` (repositories 9.7 spec);
  - the recipe `tokens-report` — where the tokens of this repository's sessions on this machine go, by their transcripts (repositories 9.1 spec);

- **What was done:**
  - the submodule `spec` is `v0.15.1`, `github.com/altnovatrail/spec` and `github.com/altnovatrail/spec/contracts` are `v0.15.1`; `ci-lint` checks that they match the submodule (R27);
  - `.devcontainer/gopls/go.mod` — `go 1.27.4`, as in the template's kind `go`, file for file, and `go.sum` as before;

A trial run 2026-11-12: the hook stops the prompt «Выполни задачу T54» after «планируй задачу T53», and lets «Выполни задачу T53» and a prompt without a task phrase through;
  - RUN.md, «Как пользоваться» — the template's paragraph about one session; the rows В48 and В49 left table 3;

The keys of T129 `spec` are now the same in `api` as in the template; if T130 or another task of E10 `spec` changes the template's `.claude/` again, the move to its tag will take it — T123 `spec`;

## Withdrawal on a schedule

**R61. A withdrawal of a version is noticed by the job "Withdrawals" — `.github/workflows/withdrawals.yml`, once a day at 05:17 UTC and by hand. It takes the newest release of `modules` by the redirect of the public `releases/latest`, checks the signature of its index by `release-verify`, and fails while the index of the newest release withdraws a version that the pinned release gives. The failing run is the signal to the author: there are no permissions beyond `contents: read`, and no repository settings or secret either.** Task T57 of epic E7, 2026-11-12; the signal being a failing run, and once a day, are the author's decisions in planning.
- **What was decided** ([architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.3; the sources — section 4):
  - "gives" — as in R07: an entry of the pinned index whose format version is in the window of `api` and which it did not mark `withdrawn`. This is also an earlier version on which lesson runs still go, not only the current one: a withdrawal closes them "unchecked" too, but also only with a pull request (R07);
  - the rule is `module-kit`'s, not one of its own: `moduleindex.WithLatest(release, latest)` gives the pinned index with the withdrawals of the newest, as for the command `open` of `canvas` (R43 module-kit). An index that does not keep the entries of the pinned one as they are — for example, the index of an older release — is `ErrLatest`, and the job fails too;
  - in code — `packages.Withdrawals`: the pinned index by the digest of `modules.json`, the newest by `moduleindex.Read`, and a file that does not read as an index is `ErrNewestIndex`. The list is in the order of modules and versions, with the withdrawal reason and a mark of the current version;
  - `cmd/modules-check` gets the flag `-latest`; it does not combine with `-main-config` and `-main-index`, and an empty one is a command-line error, not a check of a pull request. Without withdrawals the command prints "no version that <release> gives is withdrawn" and exits with 0, with withdrawals a line per version, and exits with 1;
  - the recipe `just ci-withdrawals`: the tag comes from the address that `https://github.com/altnovatrail/modules/releases/latest` leads to, only `…/releases/tag/vX.Y.Z`, otherwise a refusal. A tag lower than the pinned one by `sort --version-sort` — the mark "latest" was set by hand — is replaced by the pinned one: the index of an older release would fail every run. The index and the bundle of the newest come from the public address, without a token, and `release-verify` on every run, even when the newest is the pinned one. The index of the pinned one is held by its digest, and it needs no signature (R77 spec): when the newest is the pinned one, it is the same file, without a second download. Then `modules-check -latest`;
  - the workflow — one job on `ubuntu-24.04` with a timeout of 20 minutes: `actions/checkout` with the submodule `spec` — for `release-verify` — and `.github/modules.sh ci-withdrawals` in the stage `toolchain`, like the job "Modules". `.github/modules.sh` now takes the recipe as an argument, `ci-modules` or `ci-withdrawals`;
  - the job is not a required check and does not run on a pull request: its signal is about a release of `modules`, not about a pull request.
- **Checked 2026-11-12:** `just ci-withdrawals` in the devcontainer — "modules v0.2.5 is the newest release; modules.json pins v0.2.5", `release-verify` — "signed by the shared release.yml of .github v0.1.15", "no version that v0.2.5 gives is withdrawn", exit 0. The test — the index vectors of `spec` `withdrawal` and `next-release` from `spec.Format`: the first withdraws `example/smallest` 0.1.0, the current version, and `example/times-tables` 0.1.0, already withdrawn in the pinned one, gives no signal; the second and the pinned index in the role of the newest give nothing; `next-release` the other way round — `ErrLatest`. With indexes of its own — a withdrawal of an earlier version without the mark of the current one, and a withdrawal of an entry outside the window, which gives no signal. The timeout is 20 minutes: the job "Modules" with the same build of the stage `toolchain` and a test of the release besides took 3.5 minutes (the run of `main` 2026-11-12).

— *Why:*
- a failing run — the author's decision: it needs no write permissions, settings or secret, and the notification goes to whoever last changed the cron line, that is, the author, who commits. GitHub writes: "Notifications for scheduled workflows are sent to the user who initially created the workflow", and if another person changes the cron line, "subsequent notifications will be sent to that user instead" (GitHub's documentation, [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 4, read 2026-11-12). The failing run repeats every day while the withdrawn version stays current — exactly as long as the harm lasts that Д9 warned of;
- once a day — the author's decision: a withdrawal reaches learners only with a pull request and a deployment anyway, so a day is the order of the author's reaction. The minute is not round, because "High load times include the start of every hour";
- the newest by the redirect, not through the REST API: the address is public, without a token and without the API's rate limit, like the other files of the release (R07). GitHub calls the newest "the most recent non-prerelease, non-draft release, sorted by the created_at attribute", and a new release has `make_latest` `true` by default. `modules` puts out releases by the shared `release.yml` on one line, so it is also the newest by semver. A release older than the pinned one the recipe replaces with the pinned one, and a release between the pinned one and the newest the check would not tell from the newest: that is possible only if the mark "latest" of `modules` is set by hand. The releases of `modules` are immutable — v0.2.5 has `immutable: true`, and the index files were uploaded 2026-11-11 at 04:09:38, two seconds before the publication at 04:09:40 (`gh api`, 2026-11-12), — so a release that the address already names does not stand without an index;
- the newest's signature on every run, even when the newest is the pinned one: without it no file of a release enters CI (RULES.md, Release files are verified), and a run has one path, so `release-verify` is checked every day, not only on the day of a new release.

— *Rejected:*
- an issue from the job: it needs `issues: write`, and whether an issue reaches the author depends on their subscription to the repository, which cannot be checked — the subscription API requires the scope `notifications`;
- a pull request for a new release from `GITHUB_TOKEN`: it needs the setting "Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests", which is off in `api`, and CI would not run on such a pull request — "other `GITHUB_TOKEN`-triggered events do not create workflow runs at all", — so the required checks would wait. A move to a release is also a task with a decision, not one line;
- a pull request from a PAT or a GitHub App: the same pull request, but with a secret in the repository and a step of the author's;
- four times a day — up to four emails a day for the same pull request; once a week — a withdrawn version would go into new attempts for a week;
- the rule in shell and `jq`: it would diverge from what `module-kit` gives — as in R41;
- the newest by the REST API's list of releases with a token: a token in the stage `toolchain` for what the public address gives;
- the newest by the tags of `git ls-remote`: a tag that has no release yet would give a false failing run;
- checking the newest in the job "Modules" on every pull request: it would be silent while there is no pull request — and Д9 is precisely about such a time.

— *Cost:*
- a failure of the network, GitHub or Sigstore fails the job too, and it can be told from a withdrawal only by the run's log: `modules-check` prints the lines of withdrawn versions, and a failure names its step;
- "In a public repository, scheduled workflows are automatically disabled when no repository activity has occurred in 60 days": a repository without commits for two months loses the job until it is enabled on the Actions tab. The notifications then go to whoever enabled it;
- the run is free: "Standard GitHub-hosted runners in public repositories" are among what "remains free".

— *Consequence:* Д9 closed; the 2026-11-12 amendments to R07 and R41. After the merge the author starts the job by hand, and from then on it goes by the schedule. — RUN.md, T57; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 2, 3.3, 3.9, 3.11 and 4.
